```
1	---
2	name: quality-analysis
3	description: Comprehensive quality analysis for BMad workflows and skills. Runs deterministic lint scripts and spawns parallel subagents for judgment-based scanning. Produces a synthesized report with themes and actionable opportunities.
4	menu-code: QA
5	---
6	
7	# Quality Analysis
8	
9	Communicate with user in `{communication_language}`. Write report content in `{document_output_language}`.
10	
11	You orchestrate quality analysis on a BMad workflow or skill. Deterministic checks run as scripts (fast, zero tokens). Judgment-based analysis runs as LLM subagents. A report creator synthesizes everything into a unified, theme-based report.
12	
13	## Your Role: Coordination, Not File Reading
14	
15	**DO NOT read the target skill's files yourself.** Scripts and subagents do all analysis.
16	
17	You orchestrate: run deterministic scripts and pre-pass extractors, spawn LLM scanner subagents in parallel, then hand off to the report creator for synthesis.
18	
19	## Headless Mode
20	
21	If `{headless_mode}=true`, skip all user interaction, use safe defaults, note any warnings, and output structured JSON as specified in the Present Findings section.
22	
23	## Pre-Scan Checks
24	
25	Check for uncommitted changes. In headless mode, note warnings and proceed. In interactive mode, inform the user and confirm before proceeding. In interactive mode, also confirm the workflow is currently functioning.
26	
27	## Analysis Principles
28	
29	**Effectiveness over efficiency.** The analysis may suggest leaner phrasing, but if the current phrasing captures the right guidance, it should be kept. Over-optimization can make skills lose their effectiveness. The report presents opportunities — the user applies judgment.
30	
31	## Scanners
32	
33	### Lint Scripts (Deterministic — Run First)
34	
35	These run instantly, cost zero tokens, and produce structured JSON:
36	
37	| #   | Script                           | Focus                                   | Output File                |
38	| --- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
39	| S1  | `scripts/scan-path-standards.py` | Path conventions                        | `path-standards-temp.json` |
40	| S2  | `scripts/scan-scripts.py`        | Script portability, PEP 723, unit tests | `scripts-temp.json`        |
41	
42	### Pre-Pass Scripts (Feed LLM Scanners)
43	
44	Extract metrics so LLM scanners work from compact data instead of raw files:
45	
46	| #   | Script                                  | Feeds                        | Output File                       |
47	| --- | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
48	| P1  | `scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py` | workflow-integrity scanner   | `workflow-integrity-prepass.json` |
49	| P2  | `scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py`     | prompt-craft scanner         | `prompt-metrics-prepass.json`     |
50	| P3  | `scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py`     | execution-efficiency scanner | `execution-deps-prepass.json`     |
51	
52	### LLM Scanners (Judgment-Based — Run After Scripts)
53	
54	Each scanner writes a free-form analysis document (not JSON):
55	
56	| #   | Scanner                                     | Focus                                                                     | Pre-Pass? | Output File                             |
57	| --- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------- | --------------------------------------- |
58	| L1  | `quality-scan-workflow-integrity.md`        | Structural completeness, naming, type-appropriate requirements            | Yes       | `workflow-integrity-analysis.md`        |
59	| L2  | `quality-scan-prompt-craft.md`              | Token efficiency, outcome-driven balance, progressive disclosure, pruning | Yes       | `prompt-craft-analysis.md`              |
60	| L3  | `quality-scan-execution-efficiency.md`      | Parallelization, subagent delegation, context optimization                | Yes       | `execution-efficiency-analysis.md`      |
61	| L4  | `quality-scan-skill-cohesion.md`            | Stage flow, purpose alignment, complexity appropriateness                 | No        | `skill-cohesion-analysis.md`            |
62	| L5  | `quality-scan-enhancement-opportunities.md` | Edge cases, UX gaps, user journeys, headless potential                    | No        | `enhancement-opportunities-analysis.md` |
63	| L6  | `quality-scan-script-opportunities.md`      | Deterministic operations that should be scripts                           | No        | `script-opportunities-analysis.md`      |
64	
65	## Execution
66	
67	First create output directory: `{bmad_builder_reports}/{skill-name}/quality-analysis/{date-time-stamp}/`
68	
69	### Step 1: Run All Scripts (Parallel)
70	
71	Run all lint scripts and pre-pass scripts in parallel:
72	
73	```bash
74	python3 scripts/scan-path-standards.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/path-standards-temp.json
75	python3 scripts/scan-scripts.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/scripts-temp.json
76	uv run scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/workflow-integrity-prepass.json
77	python3 scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/prompt-metrics-prepass.json
78	uv run scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/execution-deps-prepass.json
79	```
80	
81	### Step 2: Spawn LLM Scanners (Parallel)
82	
83	After scripts complete, spawn all applicable LLM scanners as parallel subagents.
84	
85	**For scanners WITH pre-pass (L1, L2, L3):** provide the pre-pass JSON file path so the scanner reads compact metrics first, then reads raw files only as needed for judgment calls.
86	
87	**For scanners WITHOUT pre-pass (L4, L5, L6):** provide just the skill path and output directory.
88	
89	Each subagent receives:
90	
91	- Scanner file to load
92	- Skill path: `{skill-path}`
93	- Output directory: `{report-dir}`
94	- Pre-pass file path (if applicable)
95	
96	The subagent loads the scanner file, analyzes the skill, writes its analysis to the output directory, and returns the filename.
97	
98	### Step 3: Synthesize Report
99	
100	After all scanners complete, spawn a subagent with `./report-quality-scan-creator.md`.
101	
102	Provide:
103	
104	- `{skill-path}` — The skill being analyzed
105	- `{quality-report-dir}` — Directory containing all scanner output
106	
107	The report creator reads everything, synthesizes themes, and writes:
108	
109	1. `quality-report.md` — Narrative markdown report
110	2. `report-data.json` — Structured data for HTML
111	
112	### Step 4: Generate HTML Report
113	
114	After the report creator finishes, generate the interactive HTML:
115	
116	```bash
117	python3 scripts/generate-html-report.py {report-dir} --open
118	```
119	
120	This reads `report-data.json` and produces `quality-report.html` — a self-contained interactive report with opportunity themes, "Fix This Theme" prompt generation, and expandable detailed analysis.
121	
122	## Present to User
123	
124	**IF `{headless_mode}=true`:**
125	
126	Read `report-data.json` and output:
127	
128	```json
129	{
130	  "headless_mode": true,
131	  "scan_completed": true,
132	  "report_file": "{path}/quality-report.md",
133	  "html_report": "{path}/quality-report.html",
134	  "data_file": "{path}/report-data.json",
135	  "warnings": [],
136	  "grade": "Excellent|Good|Fair|Poor",
137	  "opportunities": 0,
138	  "broken": 0
139	}
140	```
141	
142	**IF interactive:**
143	
144	Read `report-data.json` and present:
145	
146	1. Grade and narrative — the 2-3 sentence synthesis
147	2. Broken items (if any) — critical/high issues prominently
148	3. Top opportunities — theme names with finding counts and impact
149	4. Reports — "Full report: quality-report.md" and "Interactive HTML opened in browser"
150	5. Offer: apply fixes directly, use HTML to select specific items, or discuss findings
151	
```

**Top opportunities (5 themes, 19 findings):**

1. **Lazy-load discipline gaps** — *medium, 4 findings, ~22KB / ~6K tokens/run*
   Four unconditional loads contradict the skill's stated discipline: schema asset in step-04 §1, version-resolution.md in step-01 §3b, duplicated presentation block in step-04 §3, and step-05 §5's 50-line Deep-tier-only QMD block parsed every run.

2. **Step-01 carries reference-shaped content** — *medium, 3 findings*
   At 303 lines it's the heaviest file. Three blocks should extract: 5-example description-voice (asset, interactive-only), 16-row headless arg table (reference), §1b monorepo detection (lift to a script — biggest single script-lift, ~750 tok/run).

3. **Headless degrades to coarse defaults** — *medium, 3 findings*
   Interactive heuristics (scope-type recommendation §2c, description synthesis §7b) drop to generic stubs in headless. Three lifts: signal-based scope recommendation, GitHub repo description as description seed, owner.login vs `gh api user` for source_authority.

4. **Non-first-timer affordances** — *low, 5 findings*
   `[X] Cancel` exit, `--preset` arg, pre-flight write probe, `.brief-draft.json` resume, QMD portfolio similarity at Deep tier.

5. **Prompt-craft cleanups** — *low, 4 findings*
   Language-distinction one-liner, version-precedence parenthetical, "three alternates" framing, design-rationale placement.

1	# BMad Method · Quality Analysis: skf-brief-skill
2	
3	**Analyzed:** 2026-05-02T20:17:02Z | **Path:** /home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill
4	**Interactive report:** quality-report.html
5	
6	## Assessment
7	
8	**Excellent** — `skf-brief-skill` is a structurally sound, high-craft workflow with exemplary determinism delegation, an unusually strong headless contract, and stated-and-honored lazy-load discipline. There are no broken or critical-severity findings; the opportunities below are tuning passes — close a small set of lazy-load gaps, lift step-01's weight, and promote the interactive recommendation logic into headless defaults.
9	
10	## Opportunities
11	
12	### 1. Lazy-load discipline has small but consistent gaps (medium — 4 observations)
13	
14	The `## Workflow Rules` section in SKILL.md explicitly mandates lazy-loading and even names opt-out cases by file. Most of the workflow honors this rigorously, but four assets/references load unconditionally in places where the runtime path doesn't actually need them. On a typical interactive run this leaks ~6–8K tokens; on the headless path (which never hits the rare branches) it leaks more. The fix is uniform: make the unused load conditional on the branch that actually consumes it.
15	
16	**Fix:** Audit every "Load `{...}`" instruction in steps 01, 04, 05 and gate it on the branch that consumes it. Specifically: (a) move `{briefSchemaFile}` load from step-04 §1 into the inline-adjustment branch in §5 only; (b) drop the `{versionResolutionFile}` load from step-01 §3b (the regex is already inlined; the writer script enforces precedence); (c) extract step-05 §5 (QMD-collection registration, ~50 lines, Deep-tier-only) into `references/qmd-collection-registration.md` and load only when the Deep-tier conditional is true; (d) replace step-04 §3's inlined presentation block with a single-line "Render per `{briefSchemaFile}` §Human-Readable Presentation Format" pointer so the schema asset is the single source of truth.
17	
18	**Observations:**
19	
20	- Schema asset (~18 KB) loaded unconditionally in step-04 §1 even though the common path never opens it — `steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md:25-27` (execution-efficiency)
21	- `version-resolution.md` loaded in step-01 §3b regardless of need — script-supported path in step-02 already opted out, step-01 didn't get the same treatment — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:120-121` (execution-efficiency)
22	- Step-04 §3 inlines the human-readable presentation block already in `assets/skill-brief-schema.md:176-213`, and the two have already drifted in subtle wording — `steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md:60-96` (prompt-craft)
23	- Step-05 §5 (QMD Collection Registration, ~50 lines) is full-tier-only content that loads on every run — `steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md:133-182` (prompt-craft)
24	
25	### 2. Step-01 carries reference-shaped content that belongs in extracted assets (medium — 3 observations)
26	
27	Step-01 is the heaviest file in the workflow (303 lines / 18 KB). It already pulls genuine weight on the interactive judgment side (description voice synthesis, scope hints, name derivation). But three blocks inside it are reference-shaped, not orchestration-shaped: the 5-example description-voice block fires on every activation including headless, a 16-row headless argument table doubles as canonical operator docs, and the §1b monorepo detection rules are a pure file-system check phrased as LLM inspection prose. Extracting these would lighten step-01 without changing behavior and would unblock script delegation for the monorepo case.
28	
29	**Fix:** (a) Move the 5-example description-voice block to `assets/description-voice-examples.md`, loaded only in the interactive §7b branch (headless skips §7b entirely); (b) move the headless argument table to `references/headless-args.md` referenced via frontmatter; (c) lift §1b monorepo detection into a `skf-detect-workspaces.py` script (or a `--mode detect-workspaces` flag on `skf-extract-public-api.py`) returning `{is_monorepo, workspaces[], manifest_kind}` JSON.
30	
31	**Observations:**
32	
33	- 5 description examples + meta-paragraph fire on every step-01 activation, even headless — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:225-237` (prompt-craft)
34	- Step-01 is 303 lines and mixes interactive prose with a 16-row reference table — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` (skill-cohesion)
35	- Step-02 §1b monorepo detection is pure file-system rules costing ~250 tokens of inline rules + ~500–2000 tokens of raw manifest parsing per invocation — `steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md:57-80` (script-opportunities)
36	
37	### 3. Headless mode degrades to coarse defaults where interactive logic already exists (medium — 3 observations)
38	
39	The headless contract itself is best-in-class — stable exit codes, JSON-schema-validated envelope, every gate has a documented auto-resolution. The gap is one layer up: where interactive mode applies signal-driven heuristics (scope-type recommendation in step-03 §2c, description synthesis in step-01 §7b), the headless path falls to coarse defaults (`scope_type: full-library`, a generic stub description). For an automator running 30 briefs overnight, this silently produces uniformly under-specified briefs.
40	
41	**Fix:** Promote the existing interactive heuristics into headless defaults. (a) When `scope_type` is missing in headless, run §2c's signal-based recommendation against the post-step-02 module count + intent text and use that instead of `full-library`. (b) When `intent` is missing in headless, seed description synthesis from the GitHub repo's `description` field (already in the `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` response) before falling to the generic stub. (c) Detect "is this the user's own repo" by comparing `owner.login` against `gh api user --jq .login` and prefer that signal over the `community` default for `source_authority`.
42	
43	**Observations:**
44	
45	- §2c scope-type recommendation runs interactive-only; headless defaults to coarse `full-library` regardless of signals — `steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md:84-92` (enhancement-opportunities)
46	- Headless description fallback stamps the same generic "Use the {skill_name} skill…" template, making routing hard for downstream agents — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:225-237` (enhancement-opportunities)
47	- Headless source_authority defaults to `community` without checking whether the target is the user's own repo — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` §3.3 (enhancement-opportunities)
48	
49	### 4. Missing affordances for non-first-timer archetypes (low — 5 observations)
50	
51	The skill is exquisitely well-instrumented for failure (exit codes, halt_reason enum, recovery branches) but assumes every invocation is a first run. Five small affordances would lift the experience for the expert, the confused user, and the hostile-environment user without changing the first-timer flow.
52	
53	**Fix:** Add `[X] Cancel and exit` to every menu (with a stable exit code 6 = `user-cancelled`); allow `--preset {name}` headless argument mapped to `{sidecar_path}/brief-presets/{name}.yaml`; add a 50ms pre-flight write probe on `{forge_data_folder}` at step-01 §1 to fail fast on disk-full / read-only-mount; persist a `.brief-draft.json` checkpoint at end of step-01 §7 and offer "Resume?" on re-entry; when forge tier is Forge+/Deep with QMD available, run a similarity search at step-01 §6 to catch near-duplicate briefs before scope work begins.
54	
55	**Observations:**
56	
57	- No "[X] Cancel and exit" affordance in any menu — confused users have no clean exit — every menu in step-01/03/04 (enhancement-opportunities)
58	- No preset / recipe support — experts re-enter the same answers across many briefs — step-01 entry, step-03 scope (enhancement-opportunities)
59	- No pre-flight write check on `{forge_data_folder}` — disk failures only surface at step-05 after 5+ minutes of conversation — step-01 §1 / step-05 §3 (enhancement-opportunities)
60	- No partial-state checkpoint — step-01 takes 5–15 min; interruption forces a full restart — workflow-wide (enhancement-opportunities)
61	- Skill-name collision check is exact-match only; no portfolio similarity awareness even at Deep tier where QMD is already integrated — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:177-199` (enhancement-opportunities + script-opportunities)
62	
63	### 5. Minor prompt-craft cleanups (low — 4 observations)
64	
65	A handful of low-severity drift between text and design discipline. None affect correctness; together they're worth one cleanup pass.
66	
67	**Fix:** (a) Add a one-line clarification in SKILL.md Workflow Rules distinguishing `{communication_language}` (user-facing prose) from `{document_output_language}` (written artifact text) so step-05's local rule is a refinement, not an apparent contradiction; (b) drop the parenthetical version-precedence restatement at step-05 §3 — the script enforces it, the reference is one click away; (c) replace step-01 §6's "exactly three alternates" prescription with "2–3 non-colliding alternates by appending a discriminator" (the current rules can yield only 2 when `source_authority: community`, breaking the "three" framing); (d) either move design-rationale narrative ("fail fast at point of capture, not 5+ minutes later in step-02") into the SKILL.md Workflow Rules / a `references/design-rationale.md`, or accept it as inline maintainer comment — but be deliberate, not accumulating.
68	
69	**Observations:**
70	
71	- `{document_output_language}` distinction appears only in step-05's local Rules, looking like a contradiction of SKILL.md's global rule — `SKILL.md:20-29` vs `steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md:23` (skill-cohesion)
72	- Step-05 §3 parenthetically restates the version-precedence rule the script enforces — invitation to second-guess the script — `steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md:101-106` (prompt-craft)
73	- "Three candidate alternates" framing in step-01 §6 can produce only 2 when `source_authority: community` — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:179-184` (prompt-craft)
74	- Design-rationale narrative aimed at maintainers (e.g. "fail fast at point of capture, not 5+ minutes later in step-02") accumulates ~30–50 tokens per occurrence — `steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:99,131-133` (prompt-craft)
75	
76	## Strengths
77	
78	- **Determinism delegation is a model pattern.** Every YAML render, JSON envelope, version-precedence application, atomic write, and registry mutation is delegated to a shared script with a JSON Schema contract. The LLM owns assembly and prose; scripts own correctness. Step-05 makes this design principle explicit.
79	- **Headless contract is exhaustive and best-in-class.** Every interactive gate has a documented headless default, every HALT branch maps to a stable `halt_reason` and exit code, and the result envelope is JSON-Schema-validated. Automators can reason about failure classes deterministically.
80	- **Stated-and-honored lazy-load discipline.** SKILL.md Workflow Rules name specific opt-out cases by file (e.g. `version-resolution.md` skipped when the script returned a version, `scope-templates.md` skipped on docs-only). The discipline is unusually precise; the gaps in Theme 1 are exceptions to an otherwise consistent rule.
81	- **Recommendation-with-rationale over equal-weighted menus** in step-03 §2c. Rather than presenting `[F]/[M]/[P]/[C]/[R]` as equal, the step picks a recommended type from documented heuristics and explains why — exactly the "Informed Autonomy over Scripted Execution" pattern.
82	- **Fail-fast-at-point-of-capture is a stated and applied principle.** HEAD-checks at step-01 §3.2/§3.3 catch typos before brief work begins; `gh auth status` surfaces auth failure now rather than 5 minutes later. Same pattern in step-03 §2b for supplemental doc URLs.
83	- **Re-entry from step-04 [R] preserves prior selections.** Step-03's Rules explicitly state prior selections are re-presented as the existing answer, not reset — avoids the "I have to re-answer 12 questions to fix one" failure mode common in iterative workflows.
84	- **Description synthesis avoids template-stamping.** Step-01 §7b shows five voice examples that lead differently and explicitly tells the LLM "do not template-stamp." Authorial care for a creative-judgment task done well.
85	- **Parallel Bash batching mandated at every multi-call site.** Each multi-call instruction names "single message with N parallel Bash calls" — unambiguous to an executing agent.
86	- **Step-06 path-resolution comment is forward-looking.** The frontmatter comment ("`shared/health-check.md` resolves relative to the SKF module root, not this step file") catches a trap that costs hours when it bites, in the file that uses it.
87	- **No template artifacts present.** Zero matches for `{if-…}` / `{displayName}` / `{skillName}` markers. All `{…}` placeholders are intentional config variables or front-matter aliases.
88	
89	## Detailed Analysis
90	
91	### Structure & Integrity
92	
93	The workflow-integrity prepass found 0 issues across 6 stages: every referenced stage exists, every stage references its successor via `nextStepFile` frontmatter, no orphaned stages, no missing-stage defects, and no template artifacts. Sections in SKILL.md appear in canonical order (Overview → Role → Workflow Rules → Stages → Invocation Contract → Exit Codes → Result Contract → On Activation). Path-standards scan returned 0 findings across all 10 files. Verdict: PASS — structurally ready to ship.
94	
95	### Craft & Writing Quality
96	
97	Overview at SKILL.md:8-14 is mission-context done right — it states what the skill produces, where it sits in the pipeline, and the load-bearing design rationale ("a brief covering too much is a worse failure mode than a brief covering too little"). SKILL.md is 82 lines / ~1774 tokens — well under the multi-branch guideline. Progressive disclosure is well-structured: per-step prompts in `steps-c/`, references and assets extracted, lazy-load discipline named in Workflow Rules. Prompt health: 6/6 stages have config-header frontmatter and explicit progression conditions. Findings beyond Themes 1 and 5 above: none.
98	
99	### Cohesion & Design
100	
101	The promises-vs-behavior trace lines up cleanly across all six axes: stage flow (linear, no dead-ends, [R] revise preserves state), purpose alignment (every promise in SKILL.md Overview is honored by execution), complexity appropriateness (six steps for "scope + write" earns its weight — each step pulls genuine load), gap/redundancy detection (no missing core stages; closest redundancy candidates are intentional two-tier checks), dependency-graph logic (clean linear chain with one declared revise edge), external-skill integration (advanced-elicitation/party-mode bounded to step-03/04, shared/health-check.md as terminal step, shared scripts with JSON Schema contracts).
102	
103	### Execution Efficiency
104	
105	Pre-pass dependency-graph found 0 cycles, 0 transitive redundancies, 0 over-constrained edges. Stage ordering enforces fail-fast (validation → tree-fetch → parsing → write). Subagent delegation correctly not used (no thresholds crossed). Parallel Bash batching explicitly mandated at every multi-call site. The medium-severity efficiency findings (schema-load in step-04, version-resolution-load in step-01) are folded into Theme 1 above. Estimated lazy-load savings if Theme 1 is fully addressed: ~22 KB / ~6K tokens per typical interactive run.
106	
107	### User Experience
108	
109	The workflow handles first-timers well (clear §3.1 prompt, recommendation system in §2c, clean step-04 digest) and is best-in-class for the automator (stable exit codes, schema-validated envelope, deterministic failure classes). The expert, confused user, and hostile-environment user are less well-served — no [X] cancel, no preset/recipe, no pre-flight write check, no resume, generic welcome banner regardless of context. Headless level: easily-adaptable trending toward headless-ready; the gap is heuristics-promotion (Theme 3), not contract.
110	
111	### Script Opportunities
112	
113	The skill is already heavily script-leveraged with five shared scripts owning all deterministic operations. Three new opportunities remain: monorepo detection lift (Theme 2, ~750 tokens/run), target probe consolidation (~150 tokens/run, low-medium priority), URL HEAD-check batching (~160 tokens deduplication, low priority). Aggregate ceiling if all script lifts are done: ~1,290 tokens/run worst case. The biggest single ROI is monorepo detection.
114	
115	## Recommendations
116	
117	1. **Close the four lazy-load gaps in steps 01, 04, 05** — resolves 4 observations, saves ~22 KB / ~6K tokens per typical run. Effort: low.
118	2. **Extract step-01's reference-shaped content (description examples, headless arg table) and lift §1b monorepo detection to a script** — resolves 3 observations, lightens step-01 from 303 lines, saves ~750 tokens/run on monorepo targets. Effort: medium.
119	3. **Promote interactive recommendation heuristics (scope-type, description synthesis seed, source_authority detection) into headless defaults** — resolves 3 observations, dramatically lifts the floor on automator-produced briefs. Effort: medium.
120	4. **Add the five non-first-timer affordances ([X] cancel, --preset, pre-flight write probe, .brief-draft.json checkpoint, QMD similarity search at §6)** — resolves 5 observations across the expert / confused / hostile-environment archetypes. Effort: medium (each individually low; bundle is medium).
121	5. **One pass of prompt-craft cleanups (language-distinction one-liner in Workflow Rules, drop version-precedence parenthetical, soften "three alternates" to "2–3", decide on design-rationale placement)** — resolves 4 observations, improves prompt fidelity to stated discipline. Effort: low.
122

8	# Step 1: Gather Intent
9	
10	## STEP GOAL:
11	
12	To initialize the brief-skill workflow by discovering the forge tier configuration, then gathering the user's target repository, intent, and any upfront scope hints for skill creation.
13	
14	## Rules
15	
16	- Focus only on gathering intent — do not analyze the repo yet (Step 02)
17	- Do not examine source code or list exports in this step
18	- Open-ended discovery facilitation — collect target repo, user intent, scope hints, skill name
19	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
20	
21	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
22	
23	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
24	
25	### 1. Discover Forge Tier
26	
27	Attempt to load `{forgeTierFile}`:
28	
29	**If found:**
30	- Read the tier level (quick, forge, forge+, or deep)
31	- Note available tools for scoping guidance later
32	
33	**Apply tier override:** Read `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml`. If `tier_override` is set and is a valid tier value (Quick, Forge, Forge+, or Deep), use it instead of the detected tier.
34	
35	**If found but the YAML cannot be parsed (corrupted or truncated):**
36	- Display: "**Cannot read forge-tier.yaml** at `{forgeTierFile}` — the file exists but failed to parse: `{parser error message}`. The setup workflow can rewrite it cleanly. Until then, the brief workflow falls back to **Quick** tier (no extra tools assumed)."
37	- Continue with `tier = "Quick"` and `tools = {}` — do not HALT. Record `tier_source: "fallback-corrupted-config"` for later diagnostics.
38	
39	**If not found:**
40	- "**Cannot proceed.** forge-tier.yaml not found at `{forgeTierFile}`. Run the **setup** workflow first to configure your forge tier (Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep)."
41	- HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "forge-tier-missing"`) — do not proceed.
42	
43	### 2. Welcome and Explain
44	
45	"**Welcome to Brief Skill — the skill scoping workflow.**
46	
47	I'll help you define exactly what to skill and produce a `skill-brief.yaml` that drives the create-skill compilation workflow.
48	
49	We'll work through this together:
50	1. **Now:** Understand what you want to skill and why
51	2. **Next:** Analyze the target repo structure
52	3. **Then:** Define scope boundaries
53	4. **Finally:** Confirm and write the brief
54	
55	{If tier override was applied:}
56	**Your forge tier:** {override tier} (overridden from {original tier}) — {tier_gloss}
57	{Else:}
58	**Your forge tier:** {detected tier} — {tier_gloss}
59	
60	(Substitute `{tier_gloss}` with the matching one-liner so the user knows what the tier label means: `Quick` → "text-only extraction"; `Forge` → "AST-grep on, semantic discovery off"; `Forge+` → "AST-grep + ccc semantic discovery"; `Deep` → "full pipeline — AST + ccc + qmd portfolio search + LLM re-ranking". The tier sets the ceiling for what the downstream create-skill workflow can do; you can re-run setup later to change it.)
61	
62	Let's get started."
63	
64	### 3. Gather Target Repository
65	
66	This section has three sub-flows. Execute exactly one branch — 3.2 *or* 3.3 — based on the user's response in 3.1, then end with the shared confirmation. Do not mix branches.
67	
68	#### 3.1 Collect target
69	
70	"**What repository or documentation do you want to create a skill for?**
71	
72	Provide one of:
73	- A **GitHub URL** (e.g., `https://github.com/org/repo`)
74	- A **local path** (e.g., `/path/to/project`)
75	- **Documentation URLs** for a docs-only skill (e.g., `https://docs.stripe.com/api`) — use this when no source code is available (SaaS, closed-source)
76	
77	**Target:**"
78	
79	Wait for user response. Branch on the response:
80	
81	- Documentation URLs only (no source location) → §3.2
82	- GitHub URL or local filesystem path → §3.3
83	
84	#### 3.2 Branch — Documentation URLs (docs-only)
85	
86	- Set `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data
87	- Collect one or more doc URLs with optional labels
88	- HEAD-check the collected URLs in parallel — do not loop sequentially. Issue all N `curl -sI {url}` (or equivalent) calls in a **single message with N parallel Bash calls**, then process the responses together. Each call must use a 5-second timeout (`curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`) to bound worst-case wall-time on hung hosts. Per response:
89	  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.
90	  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway."` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill.
91	- Set `source_authority: "community"` (forced for docs-only — T3 external documentation; the §3.3 source-authority prompt is skipped)
92	- Note: `source_repo` becomes optional (can be set to the main doc site URL for reference)
93	
94	Skip §3.3 and continue at "Confirm the target" below.
95	
96	#### 3.3 Branch — Source (GitHub URL or local path)
97	
98	- Set `source_type: "source"` (default)
99	- **Pre-validate the target before continuing — fail fast at point of capture, not 5+ minutes later in step-02.** Issue these probes in a single message with parallel Bash calls:
100	  - **GitHub URL:** `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`. On a 4xx (typically 404 for a typo'd repo or org), warn `"GitHub returned {status} for {url} — confirm the URL is correct."` and re-prompt. On 2xx, accept. (The full `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` check still runs in step-02 §1 to catch private-repo access issues — this HEAD probe is just for typo catch.)
101	  - **GitHub URL, in parallel with the above:** `gh auth status` — if it reports unauthenticated or the binary is missing, warn `"GitHub CLI not authenticated; step-02 will HALT when it tries to fetch the tree. Run 'gh auth login' before continuing, or supply a local clone path instead."` (Do not HALT here — let the user choose to fix or proceed; the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1's failure-class triage.)
102	  - **Local path:** verify the directory exists (`test -d {path}`). If not, warn `"Local path {path} does not exist."` and re-prompt.
103	- Optionally ask: "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)"
104	- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`
105	
106	**Source authority (this branch only — docs-only forces `community` in §3.2):**
107	
108	"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**"
109	- If maintainer: set `source_authority: "official"`
110	- If community user: set `source_authority: "community"` (default)
111	- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: "internal"`
112	
113	Default to `"community"` if user does not specify or skips.
114	
115	---
116	
117	Confirm the target.
118	
119	### 3b. Gather Target Version
120	
121	Load `{versionResolutionFile}` for the canonical precedence and invariant rules — this step only collects `target_version`; auto-detection runs in step 02 and resolution lands in step 05.
122	
123	**Headless:** if `target_version` was supplied as an argument, store it and skip the interactive prompt below. If `doc_urls` were also supplied, treat the version-vs-doc-URL confirmation prompt as auto-confirmed (Y).
124	
125	"**Are you targeting a specific version of this library?**
126	(Leave blank to auto-detect from source)"
127	
128	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
129	"Since this is a docs-only skill with no source code, specifying the version is recommended — otherwise it defaults to 1.0.0."
130	
131	Wait for user response.
132	
133	**If user provides a version:** Validate the shape against `^v?\d+\.\d+\.\d+([.\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\-+]*)?$` (full X.Y.Z form, with optional `v` prefix and pre-release / build suffix; CalVer like `2024.04.01` accepted; partial forms like `1`, `1.2`, `v2`, `latest` rejected). On a match, store as `target_version` and set `version` to this value. On a non-match, warn `"'{value}' doesn't look like semver — write the explicit triple (e.g. 1.0.0). Fix it now or skip auto-detection?"` and re-prompt for a corrected value or blank to fall through to step-02 auto-detection. Catching this here prevents the step-05 invariant check (`brief.target_version == brief.version` per `references/version-resolution.md`) from blowing up after the user has already approved the brief.
134	**If blank:** Proceed without `target_version` — version will be auto-detected in step 02.
135	
136	{If target_version was set AND doc_urls are being collected (either docs-only primary or supplemental):}
137	
138	"**You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these documentation URLs correspond to that version?** [Y/N]"
139	
140	- **If Y:** Proceed.
141	- **If N:** "Provide the correct documentation URLs for version {target_version}." Re-collect doc_urls.
142

144	
145	"**What's your intent for this skill?**

151	
152	Take your time — the more context you share, the better the brief."

157	
158	If the user mentioned scope preferences in their intent response, acknowledge them:
159	
160	"**I noted these scope hints from your response:**

120	If `{headless_mode}` is true, emit the success envelope on **stdout** immediately after the write (before §5 / §6 / §7):
121	
122	```bash
123	echo '{"status":"success","brief_path":"<from §3 response>","skill_name":"<name>","version":"<from §3 response>","language":"<language>","scope_type":"<scope.type>","halt_reason":null}' | \
124	  uv run {emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit
125	```
126	
127	The script derives `exit_code` deterministically from `halt_reason` (the canonical mapping is null→0, input-missing/input-invalid→2, forge-tier-missing/target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed→3, write-failed→4, overwrite-cancelled→5), validates against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-brief-result-envelope.v1.json`, and prints the prefixed `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {…}` line.
128	
129	The envelope shape on HARD HALT (any phase) is the same call with `--target stderr`, `status: "error"`, and the matching `halt_reason` (one of `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`) — see the §3, §2b, §5 (no-halt) branches above and the §1/§2 HALTs in step-01/step-02 for invocation sites. The script enforces the success/error halt_reason invariant (success requires null halt_reason; error requires non-null).
130	
131	When `{headless_mode}` is false, skip this section silently — no envelope is emitted.
132	
133	### 5. QMD Collection Registration (Deep Tier Only)
134	
135	**IF forge tier is Deep AND QMD tool is available:**
136	
137	Index the skill brief into a QMD collection so portfolio-level searches can find existing briefs and avoid duplicate skill creation across large monorepos.
138	
139	**Collection creation:**
140	
141	Create a QMD collection targeting only the brief file:
142	```bash
143	qmd collection add {forge_data_folder}/{skill-name} --name {skill-name}-brief --mask "skill-brief.yaml"
144	qmd embed
145	```
146	
147	If collection already exists (re-briefing): remove and recreate for atomic replace:
148	```bash
149	qmd collection remove {skill-name}-brief
150	qmd collection add {forge_data_folder}/{skill-name} --name {skill-name}-brief --mask "skill-brief.yaml"
151	qmd embed
152	```
153	
154	**Embed verification:**
155	
156	After `qmd embed` completes, verify the collection was embedded:
157	- Run `qmd status` or `qmd collection list` and confirm `{skill-name}-brief` shows document count > 0
158	- If verification succeeds: proceed to registry update with no `status` field
159	- If verification fails: log warning "QMD embed verification failed for {skill-name}-brief — collection may not be searchable yet", proceed to registry update but include `status: "pending"` in the entry
160	
161	**Registry update (delegated to script):**
162	
163	Build the entry JSON and pipe it to the `register-qmd-collection` subcommand:
164	
165	```bash
166	echo '{
167	  "name": "{skill-name}-brief",
168	  "type": "brief",
169	  "source_workflow": "brief-skill",
170	  "skill_name": "{skill-name}",
171	  "created_at": "{current ISO date}"
172	  // include "status": "pending" only when embed verification failed
173	}' | uv run {forgeTierRwScript} register-qmd-collection --target {forgeTierFile}
174	```
175	
176	The script handles the upsert deterministically (replace existing entry with same `name`, else append) and preserves all other forge-tier state (tools, tier, ccc_index, ccc_index_registry, other qmd_collections entries) — no need to reason about YAML re-rendering or section comments.
177	
178	**Error handling:**
179	- If `qmd embed` or `qmd collection add` fails: log the error. Do NOT fail the workflow — the brief file was already written successfully.
180	- If the `register-qmd-collection` script call fails: log the error JSON, continue. The brief is the user-visible artifact; the registry entry is a portfolio-search optimisation.
181	
182	**IF forge tier is NOT Deep:** Skip this section silently. No messaging.
183	
184	### 6. Display Success Summary
185	
186	"**Skill brief written successfully.**
187	
188	---
189	
190	**File:** `{brief_path from §3 response}`
191	**Skill:** {name}
192	**Language:** {language}
193	**Scope:** {scope type}
194	**Forge Tier:** {forge tier}
195	
196	---
197	
198	## Next Steps
199	
```

171	
172	## Human-Readable Presentation Format
173	
174	When presenting the brief for confirmation (brief-skill step 04 only — not applicable to analyze-source batch generation), display as:
175	
176	```
177	Skill Brief: {name}
178	====================
179	
180	Target:      {source_repo}
181	Language:    {language}
182	Forge Tier:  {forge_tier}
183	Description: {description}
184	
185	Scope: {scope.type}
186	  Include: {scope.include patterns, one per line}
187	  Exclude: {scope.exclude patterns, one per line}
188	  Notes:   {scope.notes}
189	
190	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
191	Source Type: docs-only
192	Doc URLs:
193	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
194	
195	{If source_type is "source" AND supplemental doc_urls collected:}
196	Supplemental Docs:
197	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
198	
199	{If scripts_intent or assets_intent was explicitly set (not default "detect"):}
200	Scripts:    {scripts_intent}
201	Assets:     {assets_intent}
202	
203	Source Authority: {source_authority}
204	
205	{If target_version is set:}
206	Target Version:   {target_version} (user-specified)
207	Detected Version: {detected_version or "N/A"}
208	{Else:}
209	Version:    {version}
210	{End if}
211	Created:    {created}
212	Created by: {created_by}
213	```
214	
215	## Validation Rules
216	
217	1. `name` must be unique within {forge_data_folder}
218	2. `source_repo` must be accessible (gh api for GitHub, path exists for local)
219	3. `language` must be a recognized programming language
220	4. `scope.type` must be one of the six defined types
221	5. `scope.include` must have at least one pattern (exception: `docs-only` scope, where include patterns are optional since no source code is available)
222	6. `forge_tier` must be one of: Quick, Forge, Forge+, Deep (Title Case, must match the tier from forge-tier.yaml, or default to Quick)
223	7. When `source_type: "docs-only"`: `doc_urls` must have >= 1 entry, `source_repo` becomes optional
224	8. Each `doc_urls` entry must have a valid `url` field
225	
```

```
1	# Version Resolution
2	
3	Single source of truth for how brief-skill resolves the `version` field of `skill-brief.yaml`. Loaded by steps 01 (gather), 02 (auto-detect), and 05 (resolve & write) so that all three operate on the same precedence rules and invariant.
4	
5	**Aligned with** `assets/skill-brief-schema.md` "Version Detection" section. If you change one, change the other.
6	
7	## Detection Algorithm
8	
9	For the detected source language, attempt the lookups in order. Stop at the first match.
10	
11	- **Python:** `pyproject.toml` `[project] version` (static) → if `dynamic = ["version"]`, check `__init__.py` for `__version__` → `_version.py` if exists → `setup.py` `version=` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
12	- **JavaScript / TypeScript:** root `package.json` (`"version"`). If the root has `"private": true` with a `"workspaces"` array or lacks a `"version"` field, fall back to a primary workspace package's `package.json` (e.g. `code/core/package.json`, or the first matching `packages/*/package.json`). For GitHub sources, prefer `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/releases/latest` → `tag_name` when a non-pre-release tag exists, over a default-branch pre-release. Treat a version containing `-alpha`, `-beta`, `-rc`, `-next`, or `-canary` as a pre-release.
13	- **Rust:** `Cargo.toml` `[package] version` (static). If `version = { workspace = true }`, resolve from workspace root `Cargo.toml` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`.
14	- **Go:** version tag from `go.mod`, or `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`.
15	
16	For remote GitHub sources, fetch version-bearing files via `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` (decode base64). For local sources, read the file directly.
17	
18	If every step fails or returns a non-semver value, the detected version is `null` — the resolver below falls back to `"1.0.0"`.
19	
20	**Pre-release handling:** preserve detected pre-release tags (`1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`) verbatim. Do not strip them.
21	
22	## Precedence — Resolving the `version` Field
23	
24	The brief's `version` field is resolved from three candidate sources, in priority order:
25	
26	1. **`target_version`** — collected interactively in step-01 §3b or supplied as a headless argument. When present, this value wins outright. The auto-detection above still runs for informational purposes (the user sees both "Target version" and "Detected version" side-by-side at the analysis summary), but the brief's `version` field is set from `target_version`.
27	2. **Auto-detected version** — from §"Detection Algorithm" above. Used when `target_version` is absent.
28	3. **Default** — `"1.0.0"` when both of the above fail or yield a non-semver value.
29	
30	## Invariant
31	
32	When `target_version` is set, the written brief MUST satisfy:
33	
34	```
35	brief.target_version == brief.version
36	```
37	
38	Step-05 §3 enforces this by setting both fields to the same string when `target_version` is present. Downstream tooling (e.g. `skf-create-skill`) can distinguish "user-requested" from "auto-detected" by the presence of `target_version` without re-deriving provenance — but the values themselves are identical. Different values are a contract violation and a bug.
39	
40	## Step-Level Responsibilities
41	
42	| Step | Responsibility |
43	|------|----------------|
44	| 01 §3b | Collect `target_version` (interactive prompt, or headless arg). Do not auto-detect — that is step 02's job. |
45	| 02 §4b | Run the detection algorithm regardless of whether `target_version` is set. If `target_version` is set and the detected version differs, surface the disagreement to the user — but the precedence above is unchanged: `target_version` wins. |
46	| 05 §3 | Apply the precedence rules and write `version`. If `target_version` is set, also write the `target_version` field with the identical value. Enforce the invariant. |
47	
```

```
1	---
2	name: skf-brief-skill
3	description: Design a skill scope through guided discovery. Use when the user requests to "create a skill brief" or "brief a skill".
4	---
5	
6	# Brief Skill
7	
8	## Overview
9	
10	Helps the user define what to skill — target repo, scope, language, inclusion/exclusion patterns — and produces a `skill-brief.yaml` that drives create-skill. This is the first step in the skill creation pipeline; the brief is the input contract for create-skill, which performs the actual compilation.
11	
12	A good skill brief sets a tight, cohesive boundary: one capability with 3-8 primary functions, an unambiguous public API surface, and a description short enough to fit in a registry row. Briefs that try to cover several unrelated concerns (e.g. authentication *and* data visualization) compile into skills that no agent can route to confidently — a brief covering too much is a worse failure mode than a brief covering too little, and this workflow steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear.
13	
14	Brief-skill is split from create-skill so the scoping conversation runs *once*, on cheap signals (manifests, top-level exports, intent), without paying for AST extraction. Compilation is expensive; scoping decisions are cheap to revise. Keeping them in separate workflows lets a user iterate on the brief, share it for review, and re-run create-skill against the same brief whenever the upstream version moves.
15	
16	## Role
17	
18	You are a skill scoping architect collaborating with a developer who wants to create an agent skill. You bring expertise in source code analysis, API surface identification, and skill boundary design, while the user brings their domain knowledge and specific use case. Work together as equals.
19	
20	## Workflow Rules
21	
22	These rules apply to every step in this workflow:
23	
24	- Read each step file completely before taking any action
25	- Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize
26	- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
27	- **Lazy-load references and assets:** `references/*.md` and `assets/*.md` files are loaded inside the section that needs them, not at step entry. If a section is skipped (e.g. `version-resolution.md` when `{extractPublicApiScript}` already returned a version, `scope-templates.md` for the `docs-only` branch that bypasses §2c), do not load that file. Each unnecessary load costs context (~5-10 KB per reference) and biases the LLM toward consulting material the current path does not need.
28	- Always communicate in `{communication_language}`
29	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
30	
31	## Stages
32	
33	| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
34	|---|------|------|--------------|
35	| 1 | Gather Intent | steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md | No (interactive) |
36	| 2 | Analyze Target | steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md | Yes |
37	| 3 | Scope Definition | steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md | No (interactive) |
38	| 4 | Confirm Brief | steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md | No (confirm) |
39	| 5 | Write Brief | steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md | Yes |
40	| 6 | Workflow Health Check (terminal) | steps-c/step-06-health-check.md | Yes |
41	
42	## Invocation Contract
43	
44	| Aspect | Detail |
45	|--------|--------|
46	| **Inputs** | `target_repo` [required], `skill_name` [required], `scope_hint` [optional], `language_hint` [optional], `target_version` [optional], `source_authority` [optional: official/community/internal, default community], `source_type` [optional: source/docs-only, default source], `doc_urls` [optional: list of `url[,label]` for source_type=docs-only or supplemental], `scope_type` [optional: full-library/specific-modules/public-api/component-library/reference-app/docs-only], `include` [optional: comma-separated globs], `exclude` [optional: comma-separated globs], `scripts_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `assets_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `intent` [optional: free-text used to derive description], `force` [optional: overwrite existing brief without prompting] |
47	| **Gates** | step-01: Input Gate [use args] | step-03: Confirm Gate [C] | step-04: Confirm Gate [C] |
48	| **Outputs** | `skill-brief.yaml` at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`; final `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` line on stdout when `{headless_mode}` is true |
49	| **Headless** | All gates auto-resolve with default action when `{headless_mode}` is true; pre-supplied inputs consumed at the gates that would otherwise prompt; existing briefs are preserved unless `--force` was supplied (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` otherwise) |
50	| **Exit codes** | See "Exit Codes" below |
51	
52	## Exit Codes
53	
54	Every HARD HALT in this workflow exits with a stable code so headless automators can branch on the failure class without grepping message text:
55	
56	| Code | Meaning              | Raised by                                                                                  |
57	| ---- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
58	| 0    | success              | step-06 (terminal)                                                                         |
59	| 2    | input-missing / input-invalid | step-01 GATE — required headless arg absent (`target_repo`, `skill_name`, or `doc_urls` when `source_type=docs-only`) → `input-missing`; enum violation, malformed semver, non-kebab `skill_name`, or step-05 brief-context schema validation failure → `input-invalid` |
60	| 3    | resolution-failure   | step-01 §1 (`forge-tier.yaml` missing); step-02 §1 (target inaccessible / `gh auth` fails) |
61	| 4    | write-failure        | step-05 §4 (write to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` failed)           |
62	| 5    | overwrite-cancelled  | step-05 §2 (existing brief, `force` not supplied)                                          |
63	
64	## Result Contract (Headless)
65	
66	When `{headless_mode}` is true, step-05 emits a single-line JSON envelope on **stdout** before chaining to step-06, and every HARD HALT emits the same envelope shape on **stderr** with `status: "error"`:
67	
68	```
69	SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"success|error","brief_path":"…|null","skill_name":"…","version":"…|null","language":"…|null","scope_type":"…|null","exit_code":0,"halt_reason":null}
70	```
71	
72	`status` is `"success"` on the terminal happy path, `"error"` on any HALT. `halt_reason` is one of: `null` (success), `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`. `exit_code` matches the table above.
73	
74	## On Activation
75	
76	1. Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and resolve:
77	   - `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `forge_data_folder`, `sidecar_path`
78	
79	2. **Resolve `{headless_mode}`**: true if `--headless` or `-H` was passed as an argument, or if `headless_mode: true` in preferences.yaml. Default: false.
80	
81	3. Load, read the full file, and then execute `./steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` to begin the workflow.
82	
```

src/skf-audit-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md:81:**Version preservation (non-destructive).** `update-skill` preserves the prior version at `{skill_group}/{baseline_version}/` unchanged and writes the new skill to `{skill_group}/{audit_version}/` (see `skf-update-skill/steps-c/step-04-merge.md` §6b, which creates the new version directory and leaves the previous one on disk). The `active` symlink at `{skill_group}/active` repoints to the new version (see `skf-update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` §5b). On the next export, the prior version's export-manifest entry transitions to `status: archived` — files retained for rollback (see `skf-export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md`). Do **not** recommend `skf-drop-skill` + `skf-create-skill` for a version bump — that destroys the prior version's artifacts.
src/skf-audit-skill/steps-c/step-02-re-index.md:119:Three collection states must be handled distinctly (same branching as step-04 §2 — keep them in sync):
src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:66:| 6    | compile-cancelled      | step-04 §6 (user selected [Q])                              |
src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:118:   | `--description "<string>"` | Override the LLM-derived description in step-04 §2 (used in SKILL.md frontmatter and metadata.json). Subject to the same agentskills.io length (1–1024 chars) and voice (third-person) checks as extracted descriptions. |
src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:119:   | `--exports "<name1,name2,...>"` | Override the extracted export list. Parse as comma-separated; trim whitespace per item; skip empty items. Used in step-04 §2 Key Exports and the count-derived metadata stats. |
src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:120:   | `--skip-snippet` | Skip context-snippet.md generation in step-04 §3 and its write in step-05 §2. Artifact omitted from `outputs`; step-05 §5 advisory snippet validation reports a "skipped" entry. |
src/skf-drop-skill/steps-c/step-02-execute.md:85:3. **Build the exported skill set (version-aware, deprecated-excluded)** using the same logic as export-skill step-04 section 4b:
src/skf-drop-skill/steps-c/step-02-execute.md:91:4. **Resolve and filter snippets** using export-skill step-04 section 4c logic:
src/skf-drop-skill/steps-c/step-02-execute.md:96:5. **Rewrite root paths for the current context file** using the generic rewrite algorithm from export-skill step-04 section 4d:
src/skf-drop-skill/steps-c/step-02-execute.md:98:   For each snippet, parse the `root:` line (`root: {prefix}{skill-name}/`), strip the trailing `{skill-name}/` to extract the current prefix, and replace it with the **effective target prefix** if different. The effective target prefix is `snippet_skill_root_override` when that key is set in config.yaml — applied uniformly to every snippet so the managed section references the real on-disk location and never mixes override and per-IDE paths — otherwise the current entry's `skill_root`. See `skf-export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md` §4d for full semantics.
src/skf-export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md:28:- If `--all` was passed, build the list from every skill in `{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json.exports` whose `active_version` entry is not `status: "deprecated"` (deprecated skills are excluded from all exports — see step-04 §4b)
src/skf-export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md:107:| step-04 | **Batch once** — §3b orphan detection, §4 skill-index rebuild, §5 managed-section assembly, and §6–9 diff + write all execute once for the entire batch. The exported skill set in §4b already enumerates every skill in the manifest — it does not need per-skill iteration. §9b adds/updates a manifest entry per skill in `skill_batch` (not just the last one), then writes the manifest once. |
src/skf-export-skill/assets/managed-section-format.md:74:- `export-skill/step-04` §4d (and its equivalents in `drop-skill/step-02` and `rename-skill/step-02`) treat the override as the effective target prefix for the managed-section rebuild: snippets whose prefix already matches the override pass through unchanged, and any other prefix — including per-IDE prefixes carried by sibling snippets that were exported before the override was adopted, and legacy `skills/` drafts — is rewritten to the override so the rebuilt managed section uniformly references the real on-disk location
src/skf-test-skill/references/migration-section-rules.md:3:> **Single source of truth.** Both `step-04-coherence-check.md` §2b (naive path)
src/skf-test-skill/references/scoring-rules.md:101:- **Migration & Deprecation Warnings section:** If T2-future annotations exist in the enrichment data, verify that Section 4b is present in SKILL.md Tier 1 and that each warning traces to a T2 provenance citation. If no T2-future annotations exist, Section 4b should normally be absent (not empty). Presence/absence mismatch is a Medium severity gap — with one Info-severity exception for historical-migration content (completed package renames, consolidated import paths, shipped API cutovers that remain load-bearing for training-data drift remediation). See `steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md` §2b/§5b for the three-case rule.
src/skf-test-skill/templates/test-report-template.md:45:<!-- Populated by step-04-coherence-check §6 (naive or contextual variant) -->
src/skf-test-skill/templates/test-report-template.md:49:<!-- Populated by step-04b-external-validators §5 -->
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md:163:**Frontmatter — pinned fields (MANDATORY):** emit YAML frontmatter at the top of `evidence-report.md` with at minimum `skill_name`, `generated`, `forge_tier`, and `t2_future_count`. Compute `t2_future_count` as the count of forward-looking (T2-future) temporal annotations in the enrichment data produced by step-04 (`qmd query` + temporal classification). **Emit `t2_future_count: 0` when no T2-future annotations exist** — omission is indistinguishable from "no data" for downstream consumers and would silently flip the skf-test-skill §2b/§5b migration-section gate into Case 2/3 for a Case-1 skill. This frontmatter is the authoritative detection contract — `migration-section-rules.md` Case Rules parse it deterministically rather than grepping prose.
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md:232:Build an empty extraction inventory with zero exports. **Set `top_exports = []` explicitly in context** — downstream steps (notably §3b targeted searches and step-04 enrichment fan-out) must see an empty list rather than an undefined/missing field so they can short-circuit deterministically. Set `extraction_mode: "docs-only"` in context. Auto-proceed through Gate 2 (section 6) — display the empty inventory and note that T3 content will be produced by the doc-fetcher step.

1	---
2	nextStepFile: './step-05-write-brief.md'
3	reviseStepFile: './step-03-scope-definition.md'
4	briefSchemaFile: 'assets/skill-brief-schema.md'
5	advancedElicitationSkill: '/bmad-advanced-elicitation'
6	partyModeSkill: '/bmad-party-mode'
7	---
8	
9	# Step 4: Confirm Brief
10	
11	## STEP GOAL:
12	
13	To present the complete skill brief in human-readable format, highlighting all fields that will be written to skill-brief.yaml, and obtain explicit user approval before writing.
14	
15	## Rules
16	
17	- Focus only on presenting and confirming — do not write files yet (Step 05)
18	- Do not proceed without explicit user approval (P2 confirmation gate)
19	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
20	
21	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
22	
23	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
24	
25	### 1. Assemble Complete Brief
26	
27	Compile all gathered data from steps 01-03 into the complete brief:
28	
29	- **name:** {skill name from step 01}
30	- **version:** {auto-detected from source, or "1.0.0" if not found — see schema for detection rules}
31	- **target_version:** {target_version from step 01, if set}
32	- **source_repo:** {target repo from step 01}
33	- **language:** {detected/confirmed language from steps 02-03}
34	- **description:** {derived from user intent in step 01}
35	- **forge_tier:** {tier from step 01}
36	- **created:** {current date}
37	- **created_by:** {user_name from config}
38	- **scope.type:** {scope type from step 03}
39	- **scope.include:** {include patterns from step 03}
40	- **scope.exclude:** {exclude patterns from step 03}
41	- **scope.notes:** {any scope notes from step 03}
42	- **source_type:** {source or docs-only, from step 01}
43	- **doc_urls:** {collected documentation URLs with labels, from steps 01/03 — include if source_type is "docs-only" or supplemental URLs were collected}
44	- **scripts_intent:** {detect/none/description from step 03, or "detect" if not explicitly set}
45	- **assets_intent:** {detect/none/description from step 03, or "detect" if not explicitly set}
46	- **source_authority:** {official/community/internal from step 01 — default "community"}
47	
48	### 2. Present Brief for Review
49	
50	Using the format below:
51	
52	"**Review the complete skill brief before I write it.**
53	
54	---
55	
56	```
57	Skill Brief: {name}
58	====================
59	
60	Target:      {source_repo}
61	Language:    {language}
62	Forge Tier:  {forge_tier} — {tier_gloss}
63	Description: {description}
64	
65	Scope: {scope.type}
66	  Include: {scope.include patterns, one per line}
67	  Exclude: {scope.exclude patterns, one per line}
68	  Notes:   {scope.notes}
69	
70	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
71	Source Type: docs-only
72	Doc URLs:
73	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
74	
75	{If source_type is "source" AND supplemental doc_urls collected:}
76	Supplemental Docs:
77	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
78	
79	Scripts:    {scripts_intent} — {scripts_gloss}
80	Assets:     {assets_intent} — {assets_gloss}
81	
82	Source Authority: {source_authority}
83	
84	{If target_version is set:}
85	Target Version: {target_version} (user-specified)
86	Detected Version: {detected_version or "N/A"}
87	{Else:}
88	Version:    {version}
89	
90	Created:    {created}
91	Created by: {created_by}
92	```
93	
94	---
95	
96	Glosses (substitute the matching one-liner for `{tier_gloss}`, `{scripts_gloss}`, `{assets_gloss}` above so the user can decode each value at a glance):
97	
98	- **Forge tier glosses** —
99	  - `Quick`: text-only extraction; AST and semantic discovery off
100	  - `Forge`: AST-grep on; semantic and re-ranking off
101	  - `Forge+`: AST-grep + ccc semantic discovery; re-ranking off
102	  - `Deep`: full pipeline — AST + ccc + qmd portfolio search + LLM re-ranking
103	
104	- **`scripts_intent` / `assets_intent` glosses** —
105	  - `detect`: SKF will scan source for the standard `scripts/`/`bin/`/`tools/`/`cli/` (or `assets/`/`templates/`/`schemas/`/`configs/`) directories during create-skill and decide automatically
106	  - `none`: no script/asset packaging — create-skill will skip the detection pass
107	  - free-text (anything else): a description of what to package; create-skill treats it as the user's spec
108	
109	(For `docs-only` and `public-api` scope types the scripts/assets prompt is skipped in step-03 §5b — the values default to `detect` but the create-skill detection pass also no-ops for these scope types, so the gloss just clarifies that the recorded value will not actually fire any scan.)"
110	
111	### 3. Highlight Items Needing Attention
112	
113	Flag any fields that may need review:
114	
115	{If language was overridden or low confidence:}
116	"**Note:** Language was {auto-detected / manually overridden}."
117	
118	"**Description:** synthesized and confirmed in step-01 §7b. Refine here if you want to tighten it now that the full brief is visible."
119	
120	{If forge tier was defaulted:}
121	"**Note:** Forge tier defaulted to Quick (no forge-tier.yaml found)."
122	
123	{If any scope patterns seem broad or narrow:}
124	"**Note:** {specific observation about scope breadth}."
125	
126	{If target_version is set AND detected_version exists AND they differ:}
127	"**Note:** Target version ({target_version}) differs from detected source version ({detected_version}). The target version will be used for compilation."
128	
129	"**This is your last chance to make changes before writing the file.**
130	
131	You can:
132	- Adjust any field by telling me what to change
133	- Revise scope boundaries by selecting [R]
134	- Proceed to write by selecting [C]"
135	
136	### 4. Handle Inline Adjustments
137	
138	If the user requests changes to specific fields (name, description, version, etc.):
139	- If the adjustment requires explaining a field's validation rule or allowed values, load `{briefSchemaFile}` now (otherwise skip the read — the common path does not need it)
140	- Make the adjustment
141	- Re-present the updated brief
142	- Return to the menu
143	
144	### 5. Present MENU OPTIONS
145	
146	Display: **Select an Option:** [R] Revise Scope [A] Advanced Elicitation [P] Party Mode [C] Approve and Write
147	
148	#### Menu Handling Logic:
149	
150	- IF R: Load, read entire file, then execute {reviseStepFile} to re-enter scope definition
151	- IF A: Invoke {advancedElicitationSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
152	- IF P: Invoke {partyModeSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
153	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
154	- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond, apply any field adjustments, re-present brief if changed, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#5-present-menu-options)
155	
156	#### EXECUTION RULES:
157	
158	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
159	- **GATE [default: C]** — If `{headless_mode}`: auto-proceed with [C] Confirm, log: "headless: auto-confirm brief"
160	- ONLY proceed to write step when user selects 'C'
161	- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
162	- User can chat, request field changes, or ask questions — always respond and then redisplay menu
163	
164	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
165	
166	ONLY WHEN C is selected and the user has explicitly approved the brief will you load and read fully `./step-05-write-brief.md` to write the skill-brief.yaml file.
167	
168	
```

10	
11	# Step 5: Write Brief
12	
13	## STEP GOAL:
14	
15	To generate the complete skill-brief.yaml from the approved brief data and write it to the forge data folder, completing the brief-skill workflow.
16	
17	## Rules
18	
19	- Focus only on writing the file — all decisions have been made
20	- Do not change any field values without user request — the brief was already approved
21	- Create the output directory if it doesn't exist
22	- Chains to the local health-check step via `{nextStepFile}` after completion — the user-facing success summary is NOT the terminal step
23	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`; written artifact (`description`, `notes`) in `{document_output_language}`
24	- **Determinism delegation:** YAML rendering, version-precedence, atomic write, the headless result envelope, and the QMD-collection registry mutation are all delegated to shared SKF scripts. The LLM's job in this step is to assemble inputs, branch on script results, and surface user-facing prose — not to render YAML, JSON envelopes, or YAML-mutation diffs in the model.
25	
26	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
27	
28	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
29	
30	### 1. Reference the Schema (LLM context only)
31	
32	`{briefSchemaFile}` and `{versionResolutionFile}` document the brief contract for human readers. The deterministic enforcement of that contract lives in `{writeSkillBriefScript}` and its JSON Schema artifact at `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skill-brief.v1.json`. Load `{briefSchemaFile}` only if you need to explain a specific field to the user during inline adjustments — otherwise skip the read; the script is the source of truth.
33	
34	### 2. Resolve Output Path
35	
36	Resolve the target write path:
37	- Primary: `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`
38	- Fallback (when `{forge_data_folder}` is not set or doesn't exist): `{output_folder}/forge-data/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` and inform user "**Note:** forge_data_folder not configured. Writing to {output_folder}/forge-data/{skill-name}/ instead."
39	
40	The script's atomic-write helper creates parent directories as needed (`mkdir -p`) — no separate mkdir call required.
41	
42	### 2b. Existing Brief — Overwrite Policy
43	
44	Before writing, check whether the resolved target path already exists.
45	
46	**Interactive (`{headless_mode}` is false):**
47	
48	If the file exists, present:
49	
50	"**An existing brief was found at `{path}`.**
```

```
130	
131	When `{headless_mode}` is false, skip this section silently — no envelope is emitted.
132	
133	### 5. QMD Collection Registration (Deep Tier Only)
134	
135	**IF forge tier is Deep AND QMD tool is available:**
136	
137	Index the skill brief into a QMD collection so portfolio-level searches can find existing briefs and avoid duplicate skill creation across large monorepos.
138	
139	**Collection creation:**
140	
141	Create a QMD collection targeting only the brief file:
142	```bash
143	qmd collection add {forge_data_folder}/{skill-name} --name {skill-name}-brief --mask "skill-brief.yaml"
144	qmd embed
145	```
146	
147	If collection already exists (re-briefing): remove and recreate for atomic replace:
148	```bash
149	qmd collection remove {skill-name}-brief
150	qmd collection add {forge_data_folder}/{skill-name} --name {skill-name}-brief --mask "skill-brief.yaml"
151	qmd embed
152	```
153	
154	**Embed verification:**
155	
156	After `qmd embed` completes, verify the collection was embedded:
157	- Run `qmd status` or `qmd collection list` and confirm `{skill-name}-brief` shows document count > 0
158	- If verification succeeds: proceed to registry update with no `status` field
159	- If verification fails: log warning "QMD embed verification failed for {skill-name}-brief — collection may not be searchable yet", proceed to registry update but include `status: "pending"` in the entry
160	
161	**Registry update (delegated to script):**
162	
163	Build the entry JSON and pipe it to the `register-qmd-collection` subcommand:
164	
165	```bash
166	echo '{
167	  "name": "{skill-name}-brief",
168	  "type": "brief",
169	  "source_workflow": "brief-skill",
170	  "skill_name": "{skill-name}",
171	  "created_at": "{current ISO date}"
172	  // include "status": "pending" only when embed verification failed
173	}' | uv run {forgeTierRwScript} register-qmd-collection --target {forgeTierFile}
174	```
175	
176	The script handles the upsert deterministically (replace existing entry with same `name`, else append) and preserves all other forge-tier state (tools, tier, ccc_index, ccc_index_registry, other qmd_collections entries) — no need to reason about YAML re-rendering or section comments.
177	
178	**Error handling:**
179	- If `qmd embed` or `qmd collection add` fails: log the error. Do NOT fail the workflow — the brief file was already written successfully.
180	- If the `register-qmd-collection` script call fails: log the error JSON, continue. The brief is the user-visible artifact; the registry entry is a portfolio-search optimisation.
181	
182	**IF forge tier is NOT Deep:** Skip this section silently. No messaging.
183	
184	### 6. Display Success Summary
185	
186	"**Skill brief written successfully.**
187	
188	---
189	
190	**File:** `{brief_path from §3 response}`
191	**Skill:** {name}
192	**Language:** {language}
193	**Scope:** {scope type}
194	**Forge Tier:** {forge tier}
195	
196	---
197	
198	## Next Steps
199	
200	Your skill brief is ready. To compile the actual skill from this brief, run:
201	
202	**create-skill** — Reads your skill-brief.yaml and compiles a complete SKILL.md with AST-backed analysis.
203	
204	After compilation, you can:
```
