Now I have enough to produce the full review. Let me compile the findings.

**Reviewing branch `refactor/skf-brief-skill-lazy-load-gaps` (4 commits) against `origin/main`**

---

## CRITICAL

No critical issues found. All four commits achieve their stated purpose without breaking the common path.

---

## IMPORTANT

### Issue 1 — Stale "Loaded by" claim in `version-resolution.md` after Commit 2

**Confidence: 90**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/version-resolution.md`, line 3

**Current text:**
> Single source of truth for how brief-skill resolves the `version` field of `skill-brief.yaml`. Loaded by steps **01** (gather), 02 (auto-detect), and 05 (resolve & write) so that all three operate on the same precedence rules and invariant.

Commit 2 explicitly removed the load of `versionResolutionFile` from step-01 (dropped the frontmatter alias, converted the §3b load instruction to a prose pointer). The reference file's opening sentence still advertises step-01 as a consumer. An LLM executing step-01 that happens to read this header summary — for instance when it resolves the prose pointer at step-01 line 120 ("The canonical precedence rules live in `references/version-resolution.md`") — would see a description that directly contradicts the step's instructions. More concretely, it creates maintenance confusion: the "Loaded by" claim is wrong and will mislead whoever edits this file next about where the load sites are.

**Fix:**
```
Single source of truth for how brief-skill resolves the `version` field of `skill-brief.yaml`. Loaded by step-02 (auto-detect, fallback path only) and step-05 (resolve & write) so that both operate on the same precedence rules and invariant. Step-01 §3b references this file for human-readable rationale but does not load it.
```

---

## VERIFIED CLEAN (your specific questions)

**Q1 — Renumbering coverage search for "step-04 §"**

The only in-file cross-references to step-04 section numbers are:

- `step-01-gather-intent.md` line 246: "...re-presented in step-04 §3 for a final review pass" — this now correctly points to the new §3 "Highlight Items Needing Attention", which is exactly the section that displays the description field. The update you recorded (was §4 → now §3) is correct.
- `assets/skill-brief-schema.md` line 174: "The runtime template lives in `steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md` §2 — that is the single source of truth..." — this refers to the section number within step-04 itself. The new §2 is "Present Brief for Review" (formerly §3). The pointer is correct; the schema's Human-Readable Presentation Format section now points at step-04 §2, which is the section that holds the canonical inline template.
- `SKILL.md` has no §-level references to step-04 sections.
- No references found in `references/` or `assets/` beyond the schema pointer above.

No missed cross-file references to step-04 section numbers. The grep sweep is clean.

**Q1 — Step-04 §2 self-containment (no schema load on common path)**

Verified. Step-04 §2 ("Present Brief for Review") now opens with "Using the format below:" and embeds the full rendering template inline (lines 50–109 of step-04). The LLM can render the complete brief — including tier glosses, scripts/assets glosses, conditional doc-URL blocks, and version lines — without ever touching `{briefSchemaFile}`. The schema load is correctly gated to §4 ("Handle Inline Adjustments") with the condition "if the adjustment requires explaining a field's validation rule or allowed values, load `{briefSchemaFile}` now (otherwise skip the read)". The common path (present → confirm) never hits that condition.

**Q2 — versionResolutionFile alias in step-02 and step-05**

Step-02 frontmatter line 4: `versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'` — present and used in §4b (conditional load on the non-script-supported language path). Step-05 frontmatter line 3: `versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'` — present and referenced in the §1 prose and §3 description. Neither was touched by commit 2. The alias is correctly declared where it is actually used.

**Q3 — Single source of truth for the presentation format**

Verified clean. `assets/skill-brief-schema.md` line 174 now reads: "The runtime template lives in `steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md` §2 — that is the single source of truth for how the brief is rendered for user confirmation." The inline block that previously existed in the schema's Human-Readable Presentation Format section has been replaced by this one-line pointer. No third copy of the presentation template was found in any other file (`scope-templates.md`, `version-resolution.md`, `qmd-collection-registration.md`, SKILL.md, or other steps).

**Q4 — Commit 4 faithful extraction (QMD registration)**

The extracted `references/qmd-collection-registration.md` covers all four required behaviors that would have been in the original inline block:
- Collection creation with `qmd collection add` + `qmd embed`
- Re-briefing (remove-then-recreate) path
- Embed verification with status field on failure
- Registry update via `{forgeTierRwScript}` `register-qmd-collection` subcommand with full error handling ("log but do not fail")

Step-05 §5 now correctly gates the load: `IF forge tier is Deep AND QMD tool is available: load {qmdRegistrationFile}` — the condition is identical to what the inline block would have conditioned on. The `{forgeTierRwScript}` and `{forgeTierFile}` aliases used inside the reference file are declared in step-05's frontmatter (lines 8 and 9), so alias resolution is inherited correctly when the reference is loaded from that step.

**Q4 — Test gap assessment**

The `validate:refs` CI job (`tools/validate-file-refs.js --strict`) will catch a missing `qmd-collection-registration.md` if the reference is declared in frontmatter but the file doesn't exist — it did exist in this commit. No dedicated test for lazy-load discipline exists (none before this PR either). The refactors are structural (moving text, removing load instructions, replacing inline blocks with pointers) rather than logic changes, so the absence of a new test is not a gap that would hide a regression. The existing `validate:skills`, `validate:refs`, and `test:workflow` coverage is sufficient to catch the most likely failure mode (broken alias, missing file).

**Q5 — Your architectural tension question**

Your resolution is correct. The report's Fix instructions for Finding 1 ("don't load schema on common path") and Finding 3 ("replace inline block with pointer to schema") are in tension if you read them as a pair directed at the same file, but they target different files:

- Finding 1 targets step-04's unconditional load instruction (removed in §1 → now gated in §4).
- Finding 3 targets the schema asset's `## Human-Readable Presentation Format` section, which previously duplicated the template that step-04 already owned.

The fix you chose — keep the inline template at step-04 §2 as the canonical runtime template, reduce the schema asset's section to a one-line pointer back to it — makes step-04 §2 the single source of truth. This is the right call. The alternative (making the schema asset canonical and having step-04 load it every time to render) would require loading ~18 KB on every common-path run, which directly contradicts Finding 1. The schema asset's job is data contract documentation, not runtime rendering; owning the presentation format was an overreach that produced the drift the report flagged. The current state is coherent: the asset says "the step owns the presentation, here's where it is" and the step contains it.

---

## SUMMARY

One real issue to fix before merge (Issue 1, confidence 90): `version-resolution.md` line 3 still lists step-01 as a load site. This is a documentation inconsistency, not a runtime regression, but it actively misleads future editors and contradicts the stated goal of the commit. All four behaviour-change risks you asked about checked out clean. The architectural call on Q5 is correct.

95	#### 3.3 Branch — Source (GitHub URL or local path)
96	
97	- Set `source_type: "source"` (default)
98	- **Pre-validate the target before continuing — fail fast at point of capture, not 5+ minutes later in step-02.** Issue these probes in a single message with parallel Bash calls:
99	  - **GitHub URL:** `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`. On a 4xx (typically 404 for a typo'd repo or org), warn `"GitHub returned {status} for {url} — confirm the URL is correct."` and re-prompt. On 2xx, accept. (The full `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` check still runs in step-02 §1 to catch private-repo access issues — this HEAD probe is just for typo catch.)
100	  - **GitHub URL, in parallel with the above:** `gh auth status` — if it reports unauthenticated or the binary is missing, warn `"GitHub CLI not authenticated; step-02 will HALT when it tries to fetch the tree. Run 'gh auth login' before continuing, or supply a local clone path instead."` (Do not HALT here — let the user choose to fix or proceed; the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1's failure-class triage.)
101	  - **Local path:** verify the directory exists (`test -d {path}`). If not, warn `"Local path {path} does not exist."` and re-prompt.
102	- Optionally ask: "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)"
103	- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`
104	
105	**Source authority (this branch only — docs-only forces `community` in §3.2):**
106	
107	"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**"
108	- If maintainer: set `source_authority: "official"`
109	- If community user: set `source_authority: "community"` (default)
110	- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: "internal"`
111	
112	Default to `"community"` if user does not specify or skips.
113	
114	---
115	
116	Confirm the target.
117	
118	### 3b. Gather Target Version
119	
120	This step only collects `target_version` and validates its shape with the regex below — auto-detection runs in step-02 and precedence/invariant resolution lands in step-05's writer script. The canonical precedence rules live in `references/version-resolution.md`; load it from step-02 / step-05 only when the relevant section needs it.
121	
122	**Headless:** if `target_version` was supplied as an argument, store it and skip the interactive prompt below. If `doc_urls` were also supplied, treat the version-vs-doc-URL confirmation prompt as auto-confirmed (Y).
123	
124	"**Are you targeting a specific version of this library?**
125	(Leave blank to auto-detect from source)"
126	
127	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
128	"Since this is a docs-only skill with no source code, specifying the version is recommended — otherwise it defaults to 1.0.0."
129	
130	Wait for user response.
131	
132	**If user provides a version:** Validate the shape against `^v?\d+\.\d+\.\d+([.\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\-+]*)?$` (full X.Y.Z form, with optional `v` prefix and pre-release / build suffix; CalVer like `2024.04.01` accepted; partial forms like `1`, `1.2`, `v2`, `latest` rejected). On a match, store as `target_version` and set `version` to this value. On a non-match, warn `"'{value}' doesn't look like semver — write the explicit triple (e.g. 1.0.0). Fix it now or skip auto-detection?"` and re-prompt for a corrected value or blank to fall through to step-02 auto-detection. Catching this here prevents the step-05 invariant check (`brief.target_version == brief.version` per `references/version-resolution.md`) from blowing up after the user has already approved the brief.
133	**If blank:** Proceed without `target_version` — version will be auto-detected in step 02.
134	
135	{If target_version was set AND doc_urls are being collected (either docs-only primary or supplemental):}
136	
137	"**You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these documentation URLs correspond to that version?** [Y/N]"
138	
139	- **If Y:** Proceed.
140	- **If N:** "Provide the correct documentation URLs for version {target_version}." Re-collect doc_urls.
141

143	
144	"**What's your intent for this skill?**

107	- Emits a JSON success envelope on stdout: `{"status":"ok","brief_path":"…","version":"…","bytes":…,"warnings":[…]}`
108	
109	**On script failure (non-zero exit):**
110	- Exit 1 (validation/invariant): The error JSON on stderr names the offending field. This indicates a context-assembly bug, not a user error — surface the message to the user, log it, then HALT.
111	  - Interactive: **HALT** — display the error JSON's `message` field.
112	  - Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr` with envelope-context `halt_reason: "input-invalid"` (the script derives `exit_code: 2` automatically — do NOT pass `exit_code` in the JSON payload), then `exit 2`.
113	- Exit 2 (I/O failure): The atomic write failed (target unwritable, disk full, etc.).
114	  - Interactive: **HALT** — "**Error:** Failed to write skill-brief.yaml. Check that the directory is writable and try again."

## No Critical Issues Found

## Commit-by-Commit Analysis

### ae21df1e — "2-3 non-colliding" alternates (step-01 §6)

**Conditional template syntax — consistent.** The new `{if a third alternate was produced:} [3] {alternate-3}` appears inside a fenced code block (lines 185–194 of step-01). The existing convention for conditional prose is `{If source_type is "docs-only":}` outside code blocks (step-04 line 70, step-03 line 36, etc.) — those are not inside fenced blocks. The new conditional is structurally different: it is inside a literal code block that the LLM is expected to render verbatim, not interpret as a branch. In that context, a lowercase `{if …}` with descriptive prose inside the block is the right form because it is an inline rendering hint, not a prose branch directive. Step-04 line 84 (`{If target_version is set:}`) is the precedent outside blocks; this new marker follows a lowercase-if-inside-block pattern that does not conflict. No inconsistency.

**Headless branch unaffected.** The headless path (line 198) logs and proceeds — it never reaches the alternate-presentation block. Confirmed unaffected.

**Downstream consumer — no "exactly 3" contract.** Nothing in step-02, step-03, step-04, or step-05 reads back the alternates list. The alternates are ephemeral, presented only for user selection in §6, and the result is just the chosen `{name}`. No downstream consumer expects a fixed count.

**Rule logic — internally consistent.** Rule 1 always fires (yields alternate-1). Rule 2 fires when `target_version` is set (yields alternate-2 if rule 2 fires first, otherwise alternate-2 from rule 2 and alternate-3 from rule 3). Rule 3 fires only when `source_authority` is not `community`. The prose at line 183 correctly summarizes this: "typically 2 alternates for community-authority briefs, 3 otherwise." The numbering instruction `[1] [2] [3]…` is correct — surviving alternates are numbered in order, so if only rules 1 and 2 fire you get [1] and [2]; if all three fire you get [1] [2] [3]. No logic defect.

**One observation (confidence 82):** Rule 2 (`{name}-{target_version}`) and rule 3 (`{name}-{source_authority}`) are described as the second and third candidates, but a community-authority brief with a `target_version` set produces exactly 2 alternates — rule 1 and rule 2. The result summary at line 183 says "typically 2 alternates for community-authority briefs", but note the phrase "3 otherwise" implies rule 3 is always the difference-maker. The actual count is: if only rule 1 fires → 1 alternate (no `target_version`, `community` authority); if rules 1+2 → 2; if rules 1+3 → 2; if rules 1+2+3 → 3. The text "typically 2" is only accurate when `target_version` is set or when `source_authority` is not `community`, but can actually be only 1 alternate when neither rule 2 nor rule 3 fires. However, rule 1 always fires (it always produces a non-colliding `{name}-v2`) so the minimum is always 1. The concern is the parenthetical "typically 2 alternates for community-authority briefs" — a community brief without a `target_version` will produce only 1 alternate, not 2. That said, the presentation template handles this gracefully via `{if a third alternate was produced:}` for [3], but there is no equivalent guard for [2]. If only 1 alternate was produced, the template will still unconditionally render `[2] {alternate-2}` with an empty or undefined token.

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, lines 183–192.

The presentation block unconditionally emits `[2] {alternate-2}` without a guard. If `target_version` is not set and `source_authority` is `community`, only rule 1 fires and there is no alternate-2 to substitute. The guard should be `{if a second alternate was produced:} [2] {alternate-2}` in the same style as the existing [3] guard. The current text "2–3 non-colliding candidate alternates" in the lead (line 178) acknowledges the range, and rule 1 says "always applies" — so the minimum is 1, not 2. The unguarded `[2]` line in the rendered block is the gap.

Suggested fix — change lines 189–191 of step-01 from:

```
  [1] {alternate-1}
  [2] {alternate-2}
  {if a third alternate was produced:} [3] {alternate-3}
```

to:

```
  [1] {alternate-1}
  {if a second alternate was produced:} [2] {alternate-2}
  {if a third alternate was produced:} [3] {alternate-3}
```

---

### e3ee08a8 — rationale trim (step-01 lines 99, 132)

**Line 99 — directive is sufficient.** The current text "Pre-validate the target before continuing." is a complete directive. The removed rationale ("fail fast at point of capture, not 5+ minutes later in step-02") explained *why*, not *what* or *how*. The section that follows is the fully specified mechanic (parallel probes, per-outcome branches, do-not-HALT note). LLM behaviour is driven by the directives; the rationale was clarifying for human readers but added no conditional branch. Safe trim.

**Line 132 — regex block is self-contained.** The paragraph at line 132 already contains the full regex, the enumerated accept/reject examples, the warn-message template, and the re-prompt instruction. The removed clause ("Catching this here prevents the step-05 invariant check...") was a cross-reference to the downstream enforcement point. Nothing in the LLM's required action at this point depends on knowing step-05 will also check — the mechanic is fully specified. Safe trim.

No issues.

---

### 657def2e — language distinction in SKILL.md Workflow Rules

**No contradiction introduced.** SKILL.md line 28 now reads (in effect): communicate in `{communication_language}`; written artifact text in `{document_output_language}`; the two may be the same; per-step rules call this out where it applies (see step-05). Step-05 line 24 reads: "All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`; written artifact (`description`, `notes`) in `{document_output_language}`". The SKILL.md rule is the global statement and explicitly defers "where it applies" to step rules. Step-05's local rule is an exact refinement (it names the specific fields: `description`, `notes`) — not a contradiction or a redundancy that conflicts with the global rule.

The step-04 Rules section (line 19) says only "All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`" — it does not mention `document_output_language`. That is coherent because step-04 does not write the artifact; it only presents it for review. No step other than step-05 has a reason to mention `document_output_language`. The global rule in SKILL.md correctly scopes the distinction to where it fires.

No issues.

---

### 7334e872 — drop version-precedence parenthetical (step-05 §3)

The current bullet at step-05 line 103 reads: "Applies the version-precedence rule from `{versionResolutionFile}`". The removed parenthetical was `(target_version > detected_version > 1.0.0)`. The bullet still names the authoritative reference file by its frontmatter alias — a reader or LLM that needs the exact ordering can open `{versionResolutionFile}`. The script enforces the rule deterministically regardless. For the LLM executing this step, the operative information is that the rule is applied by the script (not something the LLM reasons through), and the reference is named if a human needs to audit it. That is sufficient.

No issues.

---

## Summary

One real issue identified, confidence 82:

**Commit ae21df1e** — the `[2] {alternate-2}` line in the presentation block is unguarded. When only rule 1 fires (community authority + no `target_version`), only 1 alternate is produced, but the template will still emit a `[2]` entry with an unresolved token. Add `{if a second alternate was produced:}` as a guard around the `[2]` line, mirroring the existing guard already added for `[3]`.

All other changes — the rationale trim, the language-distinction clarification, and the version-precedence parenthetical drop — are safe and achieve exactly their stated intent with no regressions.

201

203

205	- **Intent:** {user's intent summary}

217	
218	### 7b. Synthesize Skill Description
219	
220	The schema's `description` field is 1-3 sentences and surfaces in skill registries — it must exist by the time step-04 presents the brief. Synthesize it explicitly here, while the user's intent is fresh, instead of letting it fall out implicitly later.
221	
222	Compose a candidate 1-3 sentence description from the gathered material. **Write like a human library maintainer would** — what does an agent get from this skill, and when should it route here? Two facts must come through (what the skill is, when to use it); everything else is voice. Resist filling in the same skeleton every time.
223	
224	Examples of the range — note that voice, structure, lead, and emphasis all vary:
225	
226	> Render Markdown to HTML using the marked library. Use when the user pastes raw Markdown and wants formatted output, or asks how to convert MD files in a build pipeline.
227	
228	> Stripe API client for Node.js — payment intents, subscriptions, customer portal, webhooks. Triggers on tasks involving Stripe-managed payments, subscription billing, or webhook event handling.
229	
230	> Charts and visualizations powered by D3.js. Reach for this when the user asks to plot data, build interactive graphs, or wants bare D3 control instead of a React-charts abstraction.
231	
232	> Lint Python code with Ruff. Use when the user wants to add or configure Ruff in a Python project, debug rule selectors, or understand why a specific check fired.
233	
234	> Date and time arithmetic via Luxon — parsing, formatting, time zones, durations, intervals. Use when working with dates in ways that exceed `Date.toISOString()` but you don't want a full Moment.js footprint.
235	
236	Notice how each one leads differently (verb / noun / "Charts and..." / verb / noun-phrase) and how the trigger ("Use when...", "Triggers on...", "Reach for this when...") is matched to the voice rather than copy-pasted. Compose in that spirit using the gathered material — the target repo, the user's intent, the version if set, and any scope hints — but do not template-stamp.
237	
238	Present:
239	
240	"**Proposed skill description:**
241	
242	> {synthesized description}
243	
244	This is what shows up when agents discover the skill. Edit it, replace it, or accept as-is."
245	
246	Wait for user confirmation or alternative. Store the accepted text as the brief's `description` field. The same field is re-presented in step-04 §3 for a final review pass — refinements there flow back to this value.
247	
248	**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, run the same synthesis against it and store the result. If `intent` was not supplied, derive from `target_repo` + `skill_name` (`"Use the {skill_name} skill to work with code or content from {target_repo}."`) and log `"warn: description synthesized without intent — narrow registry text."`

1	---
2	nextStepFile: './step-03-scope-definition.md'
3	versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'
4	extractPublicApiScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py'
5	---
6	
7	# Step 2: Analyze Target
8	
9	## STEP GOAL:
10	
11	To analyze the target repository by resolving its location, reading its structure, detecting the primary language, and listing top-level modules and exports — providing the user with a factual foundation for scoping decisions.
12	
13	## Rules
14	
15	- Focus only on analysis — do not define scope yet (Step 03)
16	- Do not make scoping decisions or recommendations
17	- Do not hallucinate or guess about repository contents
18	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
19	
20	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
21	
22	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
23	
24	### 1. Resolve Target Location
25	
26	**For GitHub URLs:**
27	- Issue both probes in **one message with two parallel Bash calls** — they are independent:
28	  - `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` (verify repo exists)
29	  - `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/HEAD?recursive=1` (fetch file tree)
30	- If the repo-existence probe fails, fall through to the failure-class triage below; the tree response from the parallel call is discarded in that case.
31	
32	**Truncation detection:** After receiving the tree response, check the `truncated` field in the JSON output. If `truncated: true`:
33	- Display: "Note: GitHub API returned a truncated tree response ({count} items). Full analysis may require a local clone."
34	- Record in analysis summary: "Tree listing is partial — some files may not appear in the analysis."
35	- For very large repos (>1000 files in tree response): offer a recovery path instead of just warning. Interactive — present:
36	  ```
37	  Tree is truncated. How would you like to proceed?
38	    [L] Clone locally and re-analyze (slower but complete)
39	    [P] Proceed with the partial tree (faster, may miss exports under deeper paths)
40	  ```
41	  On `[L]`: shallow-clone (`git clone --depth 1 {url} {tmp_dir}`), restart this section against the local path, and remove `{tmp_dir}` after the analysis summary in §5. On `[P]` (or under headless): record `tree_truncated: true` in the analysis summary and continue without HALT.
42	
43	**On API failure (non-200 from `gh api`):**
44	
45	Distinguish the failure class before reporting:
46	- Auto-run `gh auth status` and capture its output. If it reports an unauthenticated state or expired token: HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** GitHub CLI is not authenticated. `gh auth status` says: `{captured output}`. Run `gh auth login` and retry."
47	- If `gh auth status` reports authenticated but the call still failed (404/403): HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "target-inaccessible"`) — "**Error:** Cannot access repository at `{url}`. The CLI is authenticated but the API returned `{status}`. Check the URL and that the account has access to private repositories if applicable."
48	- If `gh auth status` itself fails to run (binary missing): HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** `gh` CLI not found on PATH. Install it from <https://cli.github.com> and re-run."
49	
50	**For local paths:**
51	- Verify the directory exists
52	- List the directory tree
53	- If path doesn't exist: **HALT** — "**Error:** Directory not found at {path}. Verify the path is correct."
54	
55	Display: "**Resolving target...**"
56	
57	### 1b. Detect Monorepo / Workspace Layout
58	
59	Before listing the structure, check whether the root manifest declares a workspace layout:
60	
61	- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** root `package.json` has a `"workspaces"` array, OR a `pnpm-workspace.yaml` / `lerna.json` exists at the root.
62	- **Rust:** root `Cargo.toml` has `[workspace]` with a `members = [...]` list.
63	- **Python:** repo contains multiple `pyproject.toml` under `packages/*/` or `apps/*/`.
64	- **Generic:** top-level `apps/`, `packages/`, `code/`, or `libs/` directory each containing manifests.
65	
66	If a workspace layout is detected, list the discovered workspaces and ask:
67	
68	```
69	This looks like a monorepo with these workspaces:
70	  1. {workspace_name} ({path})
71	  2. {workspace_name} ({path})
72	  ...
73	Which one should the skill cover? Pick a number, type 'all' to scope at the repo root, or 'list' to keep listing more.
74	```
75	
76	Interactive: wait for the user choice. On a numbered choice, store `monorepo_workspace: {path}` and rebase §2-§4b against that path. On `'all'`, leave `monorepo_workspace` unset and proceed at the repo root with a note in the analysis summary that scope is unfiltered.
77	
78	Headless: if the input contract supplied an `include` glob that begins with one of the workspace paths, auto-select that workspace (log `"headless: auto-selected workspace {name} from include glob"`). Otherwise default to repo root and log `"warn: monorepo detected but no workspace pre-selected — analyzing at repo root"`.
79	
80	If no workspace layout is detected, skip this section silently.
81	
82	### 2. Read Repository Structure
83	
84	List the top-level directory structure:
85	
86	"**Repository Structure:**
87	```
88	{repo-name}/
89	├── {top-level files}
90	├── {top-level directories}/
91	│   └── ...
92	└── ...
93	```
94	**Total:** {file count} files, {directory count} directories"
95	
96	### 3. Detect Primary Language
97	
98	Examine file extensions and configuration files to detect the primary language:
99	
100	**Detection signals (check in order):**
101	1. `package.json` → JavaScript/TypeScript
102	2. `tsconfig.json` → TypeScript
103	3. `Cargo.toml` → Rust
104	4. `pyproject.toml` or `setup.py` or `setup.cfg` → Python
105	5. `go.mod` → Go
106	6. `pom.xml` or `build.gradle` → Java
107	7. `*.csproj` or `*.sln` → C#
108	8. `Gemfile` → Ruby
109	9. File extension frequency analysis as fallback
110	
111	"**Detected language:** {language}
112	**Confidence:** {high/medium/low}
113	**Detection source:** {what config file or pattern confirmed it}"
114	
115	If confidence is low or ambiguous: flag for user override in step 03.
116	
117	### 4. List Top-Level Modules and Exports
118	
119	Identify the public API surface. **Delegate the parsing to `{extractPublicApiScript}` whenever the detected language is supported** — the script is the single source of truth for manifest parsing, export discovery, and version detection across the whole SKF pipeline. Hand-rolling these in prose creates drift seams the LLM cannot fully close.
120	
121	**Script-supported languages** (use the script): `js`, `ts`, `javascript`, `typescript`, `python`, `rust`, `go`, `java`, `kotlin`.
122	
123	This section runs exactly one of §4.1 (script path) or §4.2 (fallback path) based on the detected language, then always emits §4.3 (output format) and conditionally §4.4 (semantic signals).
124	
125	#### 4.1 Procedure — script-supported languages
126	
127	1. Read the relevant files into memory (no parsing yet — just collect content). For GitHub sources, issue **all N `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` calls in a single message with N parallel Bash calls** (one per manifest + each entry point), then base64-decode the responses together — these are 2-4 independent fetches per typical run. For local sources read directly (also parallelisable, but local reads are fast enough that serial Read tool calls are acceptable).
128	
129	   | Language | Manifest | Entry points (mode=quick) |
130	   |----------|----------|--------------------------|
131	   | js / ts / javascript / typescript | `package.json` (root, or primary workspace package per `references/version-resolution.md`) | `index.{ts,js}` and/or `src/index.{ts,js}` if present |
132	   | python | `pyproject.toml` (or `setup.py` / `setup.cfg` if no `pyproject.toml`) | top-level `__init__.py` of the package, plus `_version.py` if present |
133	   | rust | `Cargo.toml` (`[package]` — workspace root if `version = { workspace = true }`) | `src/lib.rs` |
134	   | go | `go.mod` | top-level `*.go` exporting the package surface |
135	   | java | `pom.xml` | (manifest alone is sufficient for the modules listing) |
136	   | kotlin | `build.gradle` / `build.gradle.kts` | (manifest alone) |
137	
138	2. Build a JSON payload matching the script contract:
139	
140	   ```json
141	   {
142	     "language": "<one of the supported values>",
143	     "manifest": {"path": "<relative path>", "content": "<file contents>"},
144	     "entries":  [{"path": "<relative path>", "content": "<file contents>"}, ...],
145	     "mode":     "quick"
146	   }
147	   ```
148	
149	3. Invoke the script and parse its JSON stdout:
150	
151	   ```bash
152	   echo '<payload-json>' | uv run {extractPublicApiScript} --language <lang> --mode quick
153	   ```
154	
155	   On a non-zero exit (codes 1 or 2 per the script's docstring), capture stderr, log it, and fall through to §4.2 (the prose-fallback path) — never HALT just because the script choked on an unusual manifest.
156	
157	4. Render the returned `package_name`, `exports` (each entry's `name`/`type`/`source_file`), `dependencies`, and any `warnings` to the user. The script also returns `version` — feed that into §4b instead of re-deriving.
158	
159	5. The script does not enumerate directories under `src/`. The LLM still lists those as "Top-Level Modules/Directories" so the user sees structural context (Maven and Gradle are the exception — for those, the script returns a `modules` array which IS the list).
160	
161	#### 4.2 Procedure — fallback (not script-supported)
162	
163	Languages outside the script coverage (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc.) take this path. The §4.1 fall-through on script error also lands here.
164	
165	Fall back to ad-hoc inspection — `Gemfile` / `*.csproj` / `*.sln` / `Package.swift` / file extension frequency. List top-level source directories as potential modules and note any obvious entry points. Flag the limitation in the analysis summary so the user knows scoping is on coarser signals.
166	
167	#### 4.3 Output format (both paths)
168	
169	"**Top-Level Modules/Directories:**
170	{numbered list of modules with brief description of each}
171	
172	**Detected Exports/Entry Points:**
173	{numbered list of public-facing items found — from script output when available, ad-hoc inspection otherwise}"
174	
175	#### 4.4 Semantic Signals (Forge+/Deep with ccc only)
176	
177	**Remote source guard:** If the target source was resolved via GitHub API (remote URL, not a local file path), skip this CCC subsection — CCC requires a local source index and cannot operate on remote-only sources. Note: "CCC semantic discovery skipped — target is remote. CCC discovery will run automatically during create-skill after the source is cloned."
178	
179	If `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml, supplement the module listing with a semantic discovery pass:
180	
181	**CCC Semantic Discovery:**
182	- **Claude Code:** Use `/ccc search "{repo_name} public API exports modules" {source_path}`
183	- **Cursor:** Use `ccc` MCP server `search` tool with query `"{repo_name} public API exports modules"` and path `{source_path}`
184	- **CLI fallback:** `ccc search "{repo_name} public API exports modules" --path {source_path} --limit 10`
185	
186	See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` for full bridge-to-tool mapping.
187	
188	If results are returned, display:
189	
190	"**Semantic Signals (ccc):**
191	{numbered list of file:snippet pairs from CCC results — top 5 most relevant}"
192	
193	This supplements — never replaces — the explicit module list above. CCC may surface non-obvious entry points (dynamically constructed exports, re-export chains) that static directory analysis misses.
194	
195	If CCC is unavailable or returns no results: skip this subsection silently.
196	
197	### 4b. Detect Source Version
198	
199	**When the language was script-supported (§4 took the script path):** the `version` field returned by `{extractPublicApiScript}` IS the detected version — do not re-derive it and do not load `{versionResolutionFile}`. The script already implements the language-specific lookups documented in that reference, so loading the reference here only burns context.
200	
201	**When the language was not script-supported:** load `{versionResolutionFile}` and follow the prose Detection Algorithm directly (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc. fall outside the script's coverage).
202	
203	Surface the result regardless of which path produced it:
204	
205	**If `target_version` was provided in step 01:**
206	- Display: "**Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)"
207	
208	Display: "**Detected version:** {version or 'Not detected — will default to 1.0.0'}"
209	
210	{If target_version was provided AND auto-detected version differs:}
211	"**Note:** Detected version ({detected_version}) differs from your target version ({target_version}). Using target version (per `references/version-resolution.md` precedence rules)."
212	
213	If detection fails or returns a non-semver value: note that version will default to `"1.0.0"` and the user can override in step 04. The actual write happens in step 05.
214	
215	### 5. Report Analysis Summary
216	
217	Present the complete analysis:
218	
219	"**Analysis Complete**
220	
221	---
222	
223	**Target:** {repo URL or path}
224	**Language:** {detected language} ({confidence})
225	**Structure:** {file count} files across {directory count} directories
226	
227	**Key Modules ({count}):**
228	{bulleted list of modules}
229	
230	**Public Exports/Entry Points ({count}):**
231	{bulleted list of exports}
232	
233	**Notable Files:**

---
# /// script
# requires-python = ">=3.9"
# dependencies = []
# ///
"""SKF Validate Brief Inputs — pre-pass validation for brief-skill headless invocation.

Validates and normalizes the headless argument set passed to skf-brief-skill
before the workflow's interactive sequence runs. Catches malformed inputs at
point of capture instead of letting them surface 5+ minutes later in step-02
or step-05.

CLI:
  uv run skf-validate-brief-inputs.py --json '{...}'
  echo '{...}' | uv run skf-validate-brief-inputs.py

Input (JSON object on stdin or via --json):
  Required:
    target_repo  — string (URL or path); error if absent
    skill_name   — string (kebab-case); error if absent or malformed

  Optional with enum constraints:
    source_type      — "source" | "docs-only" (default "source")
    source_authority — "official" | "community" | "internal" (default "community")
    scope_type       — "full-library" | "specific-modules" | "public-api"
                       | "component-library" | "reference-app" | "docs-only"
    scripts_intent   — "detect" | "none" | free-text (default "detect")
    assets_intent    — "detect" | "none" | free-text (default "detect")

  Optional with format constraints:
    target_version — loose semver string (matches 1.2.3, 1.2.3-rc.1, 1.2.3+build.5)

  Conditional:
    doc_urls — required when source_type == "docs-only"

  Free-text / pass-through:
    scope_hint, language_hint, intent, include, exclude, force

  Unrecognized keys are passed through unchanged but flagged as warnings.

Output (JSON on stdout):
  {
    "valid": bool,
    "errors":   [{"field": "...", "message": "..."}, ...],
    "warnings": [{"field": "...", "message": "..."}, ...],
    "normalized": { ...input dict with defaults applied... },
    "halt_reason": "input-missing" | "input-invalid" | null
  }

Exit codes:
  0 — valid (errors empty)
  1 — invalid (errors present); halt_reason is set
  2 — internal error (bad JSON input, IO failure)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import re
import sys
```

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Tests for skf-validate-brief-inputs.py.

The validator is pure — it parses a dict and returns a result envelope.
Tests build payloads inline and call validate() directly.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import json
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

SCRIPT_PATH = (
    Path(__file__).parent.parent
    / "src"
    / "shared"
    / "scripts"
    / "skf-validate-brief-inputs.py"
)

spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("skf_validate_brief_inputs", SCRIPT_PATH)
mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(mod)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Required fields
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


class TestRequiredFields:
    def test_both_required_present_minimal_valid(self):
        out = mod.validate({"target_repo": "https://github.com/foo/bar", "skill_name": "foo-bar"})
        assert out["valid"] is True
        assert out["errors"] == []
        assert out["halt_reason"] is None

    def test_missing_target_repo_halts_input_missing(self):
        out = mod.validate({"skill_name": "foo-bar"})
        assert out["valid"] is False
        assert any(e["field"] == "target_repo" for e in out["errors"])
        assert out["halt_reason"] == "input-missing"

    def test_missing_skill_name_halts_input_missing(self):
        out = mod.validate({"target_repo": "https://github.com/foo/bar"})
        assert out["valid"] is False
        assert any(e["field"] == "skill_name" for e in out["errors"])
        assert out["halt_reason"] == "input-missing"

    def test_both_missing_emits_two_errors(self):
        out = mod.validate({})
        assert out["valid"] is False
        fields = {e["field"] for e in out["errors"]}
        assert {"target_repo", "skill_name"}.issubset(fields)

    def test_both_missing_halt_reason_is_input_missing(self):
        out = mod.validate({})
        assert out["halt_reason"] == "input-missing"

    def test_missing_required_plus_invalid_enum_prefers_input_missing(self):
        # target_repo absent + scope_type invalid — "missing" should dominate
        out = mod.validate({"skill_name": "foo", "scope_type": "not-real"})
        assert out["valid"] is False
        assert out["halt_reason"] == "input-missing"


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# skill_name format
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


class TestSkillNameFormat:
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ["foo", "foo-bar", "foo-bar-baz", "foo123", "a", "a1-b2"])
    def test_valid_kebab(self, name):
        out = mod.validate({"target_repo": "/x", "skill_name": name})
```

Now I have everything I need. Let me compile my findings.

**Summary of what I've verified:**

1. The asset file exists at the correct skill-root-relative path
2. The reference file exists at the correct skill-root-relative path
3. Both have 5 examples / 15 rows respectively
4. The KNOWN_FIELDS set in the script has exactly 15 entries matching the table rows
5. The cross-ref was updated from "table above" to "table in `{headlessArgsFile}`"
6. The §7b load directive is imperative ("Load") but the headless synthesis path says "run the same synthesis" which is ambiguous about whether it carries the load
7. The §8 GATE phrasing "load it now if you need to look up a specific argument" is intentionally weak/conditional
8. One content duplication: the "Compose in that spirit using the gathered material..." sentence appears in both step-01 §7b and the asset's Notes on Voice section

The one genuine issue I need to flag concerns the "run the same synthesis" headless instruction not explicitly carrying forward the Load directive, and the content duplication of the "compose in that spirit" sentence. Let me assess confidence levels.

---

Reviewing the 2 commits on branch `refactor/skf-brief-skill-extract-references` against `origin/main` (748486a).

Files examined:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/assets/description-voice-examples.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/headless-args.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md`

---

## Critical

None found.

---

## Important

### 1. Headless §7b synthesis does not explicitly carry the Load directive forward — confidence 82

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 238

**Issue:** The §7b headless note says "**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, run the same synthesis against it and store the result." The phrase "run the same synthesis" is supposed to carry forward the imperative `Load {descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` directive from the lines immediately above it. In the old inline version, the examples were literally present in the prompt and the LLM saw them unconditionally. After extraction, the headless path must now issue a Load. The words "run the same synthesis" do imply "the same steps," but a model processing the headless gate sees a compact instruction that resolves to "synthesize a description from intent + target_repo" — the Load is a prerequisite buried in the section above the headless note, not restated at the point of use.

The interactive path has no ambiguity: the Load is imperative ("Load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}`...Compose in that spirit..."), and the LLM reads it sequentially. The headless path bypasses the interactive presentation block and lands directly at the headless note, which does not restate the Load.

**Concrete fix:** Make the Load imperative explicit at the headless note too:

```
**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and run the same synthesis against it...
```

This eliminates the ambiguity at zero cost — the file load is the same instruction either way, and it makes the headless path self-contained.

---

### 2. "Compose in that spirit..." sentence is duplicated between step-01 §7b and the asset — confidence 80

**Files:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 226
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/assets/description-voice-examples.md`, line 19

**Issue:** The sentence "Compose in that spirit using the gathered material — the target repo, the user's intent, the version if set, and any scope hints" appears verbatim in both places (step-01 ends the Load directive with it; the asset's Notes on Voice closes with "Compose in that spirit... but **do not template-stamp**"). This is not the same as the original inline state where it appeared once. The duplication means if the framing of "gathered material" ever changes, both locations need to be updated in sync — which is exactly the kind of drift that extractions are meant to prevent.

More practically: the LLM sees the instruction twice per synthesis — once in step-01 (without the "do not template-stamp" ending) and once in the loaded asset (with it). The step-01 copy omits the **do not template-stamp** anchor, so the instruction in step-01 is a weakened version of the one in the asset. This creates a subtle inconsistency: the step-01 prose says "Compose in that spirit" but only the loaded asset repeats "do not template-stamp."

**Concrete fix:** Remove the trailing "Compose in that spirit using the gathered material..." clause from step-01 line 226, letting the asset be the sole source. The step-01 Load directive becomes:

```
Load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` for the five voice examples (range of acceptable leads, structures, and trigger phrasings) and the "do not template-stamp" guidance, then compose in that spirit.
```

This keeps step-01 as an orchestration pointer and lets the asset own the full instruction including the "do not template-stamp" emphasis.

---

## All-clear items (no issues found)

**1. Asset/reference round-trip fidelity (5 examples + meta-paragraph):** The asset contains all five description voice examples verbatim with distinct leads (verb / noun / "Charts and..." / verb / noun-phrase) and trigger phrasings. The "Notes on Voice" meta-paragraph is present and structurally intact. The quality report paraphrased the opening as "Notice how each one leads differently..." but the actual text says "Each example leads differently..." — there is no "Notice how" phrasing in the asset. This is consistent with the quality report paraphrasing the original, not quoting it; no verbatim change was made.

**2. Headless args table round-trip fidelity (15 rows):** All 15 argument rows are present in `references/headless-args.md` with correct halt codes, exit codes, defaults, and enum values. The "16-row" count in the commit message is explained: header row + 15 data rows = 16 counted. Every halt_reason and exit code annotation is preserved. The table is internally consistent with SKILL.md's Invocation Contract table and with the script's `KNOWN_FIELDS` set (both list exactly the same 15 field names).

**3. Lazy-load gating — `descriptionVoiceExamplesFile`:** The Load directive appears only inside §7b (line 226). It is not loaded at step entry, at §3, §4, §5, §6, or anywhere else in step-01. The headless skip path in §7b (when `intent` is absent, fall to generic stub) correctly bypasses both the Load and the synthesis — no unnecessary load there.

**4. Lazy-load gating — `headlessArgsFile`:** The reference to `{headlessArgsFile}` appears only inside the §8 GATE block (line 252), conditional on `{headless_mode}`. The "load it now if you need to look up a specific argument" phrasing is intentionally weak: because validation is fully delegated to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`, an LLM that receives `valid: true` + normalized output from the script has no reason to look up any specific argument, and the file will not be loaded. This is the correct design — the table is operator-facing documentation, not execution instructions.

**5. Frontmatter alias paths:** Both `descriptionVoiceExamplesFile: 'assets/description-voice-examples.md'` and `headlessArgsFile: 'references/headless-args.md'` use skill-root-relative paths, consistent with every other frontmatter alias in the workflow (e.g., step-02's `versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'`, step-04's `briefSchemaFile: 'assets/skill-brief-schema.md'`). Both files exist at the expected locations under `src/skf-brief-skill/`. Path resolution is correct.

**6. Cross-reference update:** The single inline cross-ref that previously said "the table above" has been updated to "the table in `{headlessArgsFile}`" (step-01 line 265). No other location in SKILL.md, other steps, or docs was found to reference the old inline table or the voice examples block directly. The SKILL.md Invocation Contract table is independent summary content and is not affected.

1	# /// script
2	# requires-python = ">=3.11"
3	# dependencies = []
4	# ///
5	"""SKF Extract Public API — pure parser for manifest + entry-point content.
6	
7	Takes a JSON payload on stdin describing one logical package (one
8	manifest, one or more entry-point files) and emits a structured
9	extraction inventory: package name, version, description, public
10	exports, declared dependencies, and (for Maven/Gradle) discovered
11	sub-modules. The helper does NO file I/O — the caller fetches files
12	(via gh api / web browsing / local filesystem / wherever) and pipes
13	their contents in. Multi-module monorepos are caller-orchestrated:
14	invoke once per module, aggregate the results.
15	
16	Supported languages (--language values; first listed is preferred):
17	
18	  js, ts, javascript, typescript    package.json     index.{js,ts}, src/index.{js,ts}
19	  python                            pyproject.toml   __init__.py / setup.py
20	  rust                              Cargo.toml       src/lib.rs
21	  go                                go.mod           top-level *.go
22	  java                              pom.xml          src/main/java/**/*.java
23	  kotlin                            build.gradle*    src/main/kotlin/**/*.kt
24	
25	Mode flag (currently `quick` only; `full` reserved for skf-create-skill):
26	
27	  --mode quick    Top-level exports only (one entry file per language).
28	
29	Input JSON shape (stdin):
30	
31	  {
32	    "language": "python",
33	    "manifest": {"path": "pyproject.toml", "content": "..."},
34	    "entries":  [{"path": "src/foo/__init__.py", "content": "..."}, ...],
35	    "mode":     "quick"
36	  }
37	
38	Output JSON shape (stdout):
39	
40	  {
41	    "language":    "python",
42	    "package_name": "foo",
43	    "version":      "1.2.3",
44	    "description":  "...",
45	    "exports":      [{"name": "Bar", "type": "class", "source_file": "..."}, ...],
46	    "dependencies": ["requests", "pydantic", ...],
47	    "modules":      ["server", "client"],         (Maven/Gradle only)
48	    "extra":        {"group_id": "com.example"}, (Maven only)
49	    "warnings":     ["..."]                        (parse failures, fallbacks)
50	  }
51	
52	Exit codes:
53	
54	  0    success
55	  1    payload-level error (unknown language, no manifest content)
56	  2    stdin / argparse / JSON-decode error
57	"""
58	
59	from __future__ import annotations
60	
61	import argparse
62	import json
63	import re
64	import sys
65	import tomllib
66	import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
67	from typing import Callable
68	
69	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
70	# Manifest parsers
71	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

73

78	        return {"_parse_error": f"package.json JSON parse error: {e}"}

  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py",
  "content": "# /// script\n# requires-python = \">=3.11\"\n# dependencies = [\"pyyaml\"]\n# ///\n\"\"\"SKF Detect Workspaces — pure detector for monorepo / multi-package layouts.\n\nTakes a JSON payload on stdin describing a target repo's file tree plus the\ncontents of a small set of root manifests, and returns whether a workspace\nlayout is present, which manifest kind drives it, and the list of resolved\nworkspaces. The helper does NO file I/O — the caller (typically\nskf-brief-skill step-02 §1b) fetches files via `gh api` / local filesystem\nand pipes the relevant content in.\n\nDetection runs in priority order; the first matching detector wins:\n\n  1. npm-workspaces       package.json has `workspaces: [...]` or `{packages: [...]}`\n  2. pnpm-workspaces      pnpm-workspace.yaml exists with a `packages:` list\n  3. lerna                lerna.json exists (`packages` field optional, defaults to `packages/*`)\n  4. cargo-workspace      Cargo.toml has `[workspace]` with `members = [...]`\n  5. python-multi-package multiple pyproject.toml under packages/* or apps/*\n  6. generic-folders      apps/, packages/, libs/, or code/ each with subdirs\n                          containing a recognisable manifest\n\nGlob members (`packages/*`, `apps/*`, etc.) are resolved against the supplied\nfile tree; only directories whose recognisable manifest is present in the tree\nbecome workspaces. A detector that finds zero non-excluded matches falls\nthrough to the next detector instead of returning is_monorepo: true with an\nempty list.\n\nInput JSON shape (stdin):\n\n  {\n    \"tree\": [\"package.json\", \"packages/foo/package.json\", \"packages/foo/src/index.js\", ...],\n    \"manifests\": {\n      \"package.json\":        \"<raw text>\",\n      \"Cargo.toml\":          \"<raw text>\",\n      \"pnpm-workspace.yaml\": \"<raw text>\",\n      \"lerna.json\":          \"<raw text>\"\n    }\n  }\n\n  - `tree` is a flat list of repository-relative file paths (POSIX separators).\n    Per-workspace child manifests (e.g. `packages/foo/package.json`) MUST appear\n    here for glob resolution; their contents are optional.\n  - `manifests` is a dict keyed by repository-relative path; only root-level\n    manifests must be supplied. Per-workspace manifest contents may be\n    included to populate the workspace `name` field, but absence is fine\n    (the path basename is used as a fallback).\n\nOutput JSON shape (stdout):\n\n  {\n    \"is_monorepo\":   true | false,\n    \"manifest_kind\": \"npm-workspaces\" | \"pnpm-workspaces\" | \"lerna\"\n                   | \"cargo-workspace\" | \"python-multi-package\"\n                   | \"generic-folders\" | null,\n    \"workspaces\":    [\n      {\"name\": \"foo\", \"path\": \"packages/foo\", \"manifest\": \"packages/foo/package.json\"},\n      ...\n    ],\n    \"warnings\":      [\"...\"]\n  }\n\nExit codes:\n\n  0  success (regardless of is_monorepo value)\n  1  payload error (malformed top-level JSON shape)\n  2  stdin / argparse / JSON-decode error\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport fnmatch\nimport json\nimport re\nimport sys\nimport tomllib\nfrom typing import Optional\n\n\nGENERIC_PARENTS = (\"apps\", \"packages\", \"libs\", \"code\")\nRECOGNISED_MANIFESTS = (\n    \"package.json\",\n    \"Cargo.toml\",\n    \"pyproject.toml\",\n    \"setup.py\",\n    \"go.mod\",\n    \"build.gradle\",\n    \"build.gradle.kts\",\n    \"pom.xml\",\n)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Helpers\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _normalise_path(path: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Strip leading './' and trailing '/' so comparisons are stable.\"\"\"\n    while path.startswith(\"./\"):\n        path = path[2:]\n    return path.rstrip(\"/\")\n\n\ndef _match_globs(tree: set[str], globs: list[str]) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Resolve workspace globs against the tree.\n\n    Returns the sorted list of *directory paths* that have at least one matching\n    manifest under them. Patterns starting with `!` are exclusions applied after\n    the inclusion pass. Brace expansion is not supported (npm/pnpm rarely use\n    it in published configs); a literal pattern without `*` is treated as a\n    literal directory path.\n    \"\"\"\n    includes: list[str] = []\n    excludes: list[str] = []\n    for raw in globs:\n        if not isinstance(raw, str) or not raw.strip():\n            continue\n        cleaned = _normalise_path(raw.strip())\n        if cleaned.startswith(\"!\"):\n            excludes.append(cleaned[1:])\n        else:\n            includes.append(cleaned)\n\n    candidate_dirs: set[str] = set()\n    for pat in includes:\n        if \"*\" in pat:\n            for path in tree:\n                # match the directory portion of every file path against the glob\n                parts = path.split(\"/\")\n                for depth in range(1, len(parts)):\n                    candidate = \"/\".join(parts[:depth])\n                    if fnmatch.fnmatchcase(candidate, pat):\n                        candidate_dirs.add(candidate)\n        else:\n            # literal directory; include only if the tree contains a manifest under it\n            candidate_dirs.add(pat)\n\n    # Apply excludes\n    survivors: set[str] = set()\n    for cand in candidate_dirs:\n        if any(fnmatch.fnmatchcase(cand, ex) or cand == ex for ex in excludes):\n            continue\n        survivors.add(cand)\n\n    return sorted(survivors)\n\n\ndef _find_workspace_manifest(workspace_path: str, tree: set[str]) -> Optional[str]:\n    \"\"\"Return the first recognised manifest under `workspace_path`, or None.\"\"\"\n    for manifest in RECOGNISED_MANIFESTS:\n        candidate = f\"{workspace_path}/{manifest}\"\n        if candidate in tree:\n            return candidate\n    return None\n\n\ndef _read_workspace_name(manifest_path: str, manifests: dict[str, str], fallback: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Extract a sensible name from a workspace's manifest.\n\n    Best-effort: returns the manifest's declared package name when parseable,\n    otherwise the directory basename.\n    \"\"\"\n    content = manifests.get(manifest_path)\n    if content is None:\n        return fallback\n    if manifest_path.endswith(\"package.json\"):\n        try:\n            data = json.loads(content)\n            name = data.get(\"name\")\n            if isinstance(name, str) and name:\n                return name\n        except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError):\n            pass\n    elif manifest_path.endswith(\"Cargo.toml\"):\n        try:\n            data = tomllib.loads(content)\n            name = data.get(\"package\", {}).get(\"name\")\n            if isinstance(name, str) and name:\n                return name\n        except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError:\n            pass\n    elif manifest_path.endswith(\"pyproject.toml\"):\n        try:\n            data = tomllib.loads(content)\n            name = data.get(\"project\", {}).get(\"name\")\n            if isinstance(name, str) and name:\n                return name\n        except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError:\n            pass\n    return fallback\n\n\ndef _build_workspaces(\n    workspace_dirs: list[str],\n    tree: set[str],\n    manifests: dict[str, str],\n) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Resolve each workspace dir to its manifest + name, drop dirs without a manifest.\"\"\"\n    out: list[dict] = []\n    for ws_path in workspace_dirs:\n        manifest = _find_workspace_manifest(ws_path, tree)\n        if manifest is None:\n            continue\n        fallback_name = ws_path.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1] or ws_path\n        name = _read_workspace_name(manifest, manifests, fallback_name)\n        out.append({\"name\": name, \"path\": ws_path, \"manifest\": manifest})\n    return out\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Detectors\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef detect_npm_workspaces(\n    tree: set[str], manifests: dict[str, str], warnings: list[str]\n) -> Optional[list[dict]]:\n    content = manifests.get(\"package.json\")\n    if content is None:\n        return None\n    try:\n        data = json.loads(content)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n        warnings.append(f\"package.json JSON parse error: {e}\")\n        return None\n    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n        return None\n    ws = data.get(\"workspaces\")\n    if ws is None:\n        return None\n    if isinstance(ws, dict):\n        ws = ws.get(\"packages\", [])\n    if not isinstance(ws, list) or not ws:\n        return None\n    dirs = _match_globs(tree, ws)\n    return _build_workspaces(dirs, tree, manifests)\n\n\ndef detect_pnpm_workspaces(\n    tree: set[str], manifests: dict[str, str], warnings: list[str]\n) -> Optional[list[dict]]:\n    content = manifests.get(\"pnpm-workspace.yaml\")\n    if content is None:\n        return None\n    try:\n        import yaml  # local import keeps top-level cheap when YAML is unused\n    except ImportError:\n        warnings.append(\"pyyaml not available — pnpm-workspace.yaml not parsed\")\n        return None\n    try:\n        data = yaml.safe_load(content)\n    except yaml.YAMLError as e:\n        warnings.append(f\"pnpm-workspace.yaml parse error: {e}\")\n        return None\n    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n        return None\n    pkgs = data.get(\"packages\")\n    if not isinstance(pkgs, list) or not pkgs:\n        return None\n    dirs = _match_globs(tree, pkgs)\n    return _build_workspaces(dirs, tree, manifests)\n\n\ndef detect_lerna(\n    tree: set[str], manifests: dict[str, str], warnings: list[str]\n) -> Optional[list[dict]]:\n    content = manifests.get(\"lerna.json\")\n    if content is None:\n        return None\n    try:\n        data = json.loads(content)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n        warnings.append(f\"lerna.json JSON parse error: {e}\")\n        return None\n    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n        return None\n    pkgs = data.get(\"packages\", [\"packages/*\"])\n    if not isinstance(pkgs, list):\n        return None\n    dirs = _match_globs(tree, pkgs)\n    return _build_workspaces(dirs, tree, manifests)\n\n\ndef detect_cargo_workspace(\n    tree: set[str], manifests: dict[str, str], warnings: list[str]\n) -> Optional[list[dict]]:\n    content = manifests.get(\"Cargo.toml\")\n    if content is None:\n        return None\n    try:\n        data = tomllib.loads(content)\n    except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as e:\n        warnings.append(f\"Cargo.toml parse error: {e}\")\n        return None\n    workspace = data.get(\"workspace\")\n    if not isinstance(workspace, dict):\n        return None\n    members = workspace.get(\"members\")\n    if not isinstance(members, list) or not members:\n        return None\n    excludes = workspace.get(\"exclude\", [])\n    patterns = list(members) + [f\"!{e}\" for e in excludes if isinstance(e, str)]\n    dirs = _match_globs(tree, patterns)\n    return _build_workspaces(dirs, tree, manifests)\n\n\ndef detect_python_multi_package(\n    tree: set[str], manifests: dict[str, str], warnings: list[str]\n) -> Optional[list[dict]]:\n    candidates: list[str] = []\n    for path in tree:\n        if not path.endswith(\"/pyproject.toml\"):\n            continue\n        parent = path[: -len(\"/pyproject.toml\")]\n        # match `packages/<x>/pyproject.toml` or `apps/<x>/pyproject.toml`\n        if re.match(r\"^(packages|apps)/[^/]+$\", parent):\n            candidates.append(parent)\n    if len(candidates) < 2:\n        return None\n    return _build_workspaces(sorted(set(candidates)), tree, manifests)\n\n\ndef detect_generic_folders(\n    tree: set[str], manifests: dict[str, str], warnings: list[str]\n) -> Optional[list[dict]]:\n    \"\"\"Last-resort: a top-level apps/ or packages/ or libs/ or code/ with subdirs.\"\"\"\n    discovered: list[str] = []\n    for parent in GENERIC_PARENTS:\n        children: set[str] = set()\n        for path in tree:\n            if not path.startswith(parent + \"/\"):\n                continue\n            parts = path.split(\"/\")\n            if len(parts) < 3:\n                continue  # need at least parent/child/file\n            children.add(f\"{parent}/{parts[1]}\")\n        for child in children:\n            if _find_workspace_manifest(child, tree) is not None:\n                discovered.append(child)\n        if len(discovered) >= 2:\n            # Stick with the first parent that yields ≥2 manifested children\n            break\n        discovered = []\n    if len(discovered) < 2:\n        return None\n    return _build_workspaces(sorted(set(discovered)), tree, manifests)\n\n\nDETECTORS: list[tuple[str, callable]] = [\n    (\"npm-workspaces\",       detect_npm_workspaces),\n    (\"pnpm-workspaces\",      detect_pnpm_workspaces),\n    (\"lerna\",                detect_lerna),\n    (\"cargo-workspace\",      detect_cargo_workspace),\n    (\"python-multi-package\", detect_python_multi_package),\n    (\"generic-folders\",      detect_generic_folders),\n]\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Entry point\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef detect(payload: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Pure detection — the CLI wraps this with stdin/stdout I/O and exit-code mapping.\"\"\"\n    tree_raw = payload.get(\"tree\")\n    manifests = payload.get(\"manifests\")\n    warnings: list[str] = []\n    if not isinstance(tree_raw, list):\n        return {\"_payload_error\": \"missing or non-list 'tree' field\"}\n    if not isinstance(manifests, dict):\n        return {\"_payload_error\": \"missing or non-dict 'manifests' field\"}\n    tree: set[str] = {_normalise_path(p) for p in tree_raw if isinstance(p, str) and p.strip()}\n\n    for kind, detector in DETECTORS:\n        result = detector(tree, manifests, warnings)\n        if result and len(result) >= 1:\n            return {\n                \"is_monorepo\":   True,\n                \"manifest_kind\": kind,\n                \"workspaces\":    result,\n                \"warnings\":      warnings,\n            }\n\n    return {\n        \"is_monorepo\":   False,\n        \"manifest_kind\": None,\n        \"workspaces\":    [],\n        \"warnings\":      warnings,\n    }\n\n\ndef main(argv: Optional[list[str]] = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"Detect monorepo / workspace layouts from a tree + manifests payload.\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--json\", help=\"Inline JSON payload (otherwise read from stdin).\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n\n    raw = args.json\n    if raw is None:\n        raw = sys.stdin.read()\n    if not raw.strip():\n        print(json.dumps({\"error\": \"empty input\"}), file=sys.stderr)\n        return 2\n\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(raw)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n        print(json.dumps({\"error\": f\"json decode error: {e}\"}), file=sys.stderr)\n        return 2\n\n    result = detect(payload)\n    if \"_payload_error\" in result:\n        print(json.dumps({\"error\": result[\"_payload_error\"]}), file=sys.stderr)\n        return 1\n\n    print(json.dumps(result, separators=(\",\", \":\")))\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n"
}

_run(self, payload: dict | str, stdin: bool = True) -> tuple[int, str, str]:\n        body = json.dumps(payload) if isinstance(payload, dict) else payload\n        if stdin:\n            cmd = [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH)]\n            proc = subprocess.run(cmd, input=body, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10)\n        else:\n            cmd = [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH), \"--json\", body]\n            proc = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10)\n        return proc.returncode, proc.stdout, proc.stderr\n\n    def test_stdin_happy_path_exit_0(self):\n        rc, out, err = self._run(\n            {\n                \"tree\": [\"package.json\", \"packages/foo/package.json\"],\n                \"manifests\": {\n  }\n        )\n        assert rc == 0, f\"stderr={err}\"\n        result = json.loads(out)\n        assert result[\"is_monorepo\"] is True\n\n    def test_argv_json_flag(self):\n        rc, out, _ = self._run(\n            {\"tree\": [], \"manifests\": {}},\n            stdin=False,\n        )\n        assert rc == 0\n        result = json.loads(out)\n        assert result[\"is_monorepo\"] is False\n\n    def test_empty_input_exits_2(self):\n        rc, _, err = self._run(\"\", stdin=True)\n        assert rc == 2\n        assert \"empty input\" in err\n\n    def test_invalid_json_exits_2(self):\n        rc, _, err = self._run(\"{ not valid\", stdin=True)\n        assert rc == 2\n        assert \"json decode error\" in err\n\n    def test_payload_missing_tree_exits_1(self):\n        rc, _, err = self._run({\"manifests\": {}}, stdin=True)\n        assert rc == 1\n        assert \"tree\" in err\n\n    def test_payload_missing_manifests_exits_1(self):\n        rc, _, err = self._run({\"tree\": []}, stdin=True)\n        assert rc == 1\n        assert \"manifests\" in err\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Schema artifact\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestSchemaArtifact:\n    def test_schema_file_exists_and_is_valid_json(self):\n        with SCHEMA_PATH.open(\"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n            schema = json.load(fh)\n        assert schema[\"$schema\"].startswith(\"https://json-schema.org/\")\n        assert schema[\"title\"]\n        # Required result envelope properties\n        assert set(schema[\"required\"]) == {\"is_monorepo\", \"manifest_kind\", \"workspaces\", \"warnings\"}\n"

{
  "command": "git add -A src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py src/shared/scripts/schemas/workspace-detection.v1.json test/test-skf-detect-workspaces.py package.json && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfeat(shared/scripts): add skf-detect-workspaces.py with JSON Schema and 35 tests\n\nAdds a pure detector for monorepo / multi-package layouts. Takes a JSON payload (file tree + selected manifest contents) on stdin, returns a result envelope describing whether the target is a monorepo, which manifest kind drives it, and the list of resolved workspaces.\n\nDetection runs in priority order; first matching detector wins:\n  1. npm-workspaces       — package.json `workspaces` (array or {packages: [...]})\n  2. pnpm-workspaces      — pnpm-workspace.yaml `packages:` list\n  3. lerna                — lerna.json (defaults to packages/* when packages field absent)\n  4. cargo-workspace      — Cargo.toml [workspace] members + optional exclude\n  5. python-multi-package — multiple pyproject.toml under packages/* or apps/*\n  6. generic-folders      — apps/ / packages/ / libs/ / code/ with manifested subdirs\n\nGlob members (`packages/*`, `crates/*`, etc.) are resolved against the supplied tree and only directories with a recognisable child manifest become workspaces. Detectors return None and fall through to the next one when their manifest fails to parse, with a warning attached to the envelope.\n\nThe script does NO file I/O — the caller (typically skf-brief-skill step-02 §1b) fetches files via gh api / local FS / wherever and pipes content in. This matches the existing pattern of skf-extract-public-api.py and skf-validate-brief-inputs.py.\n\nIncludes:\n  - Result envelope JSON Schema at src/shared/scripts/schemas/workspace-detection.v1.json\n  - 35-test pytest suite covering single-package, npm/pnpm/lerna/cargo/python-multi/generic\n    detectors, priority resolution, malformed-manifest fall-through, glob/exclude resolution,\n    and the CLI exit-code contract (0 success, 1 payload error, 2 input error)\n  - Wired into npm run test:python (now 708 tests total)\n\nRefs: skills/reports/skf-brief-skill/quality-analysis/20260502-201702/quality-report.md (Theme 2, finding \"§1b monorepo detection is pure file-system rules phrased as LLM inspection prose\")\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Commit the new script",
  "timeout": 300000
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md",
  "old_string": "### 1b. Detect Monorepo / Workspace Layout\n\nBefore listing the structure, check whether the root manifest declares a workspace layout:\n\n- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** root `package.json` has a `\"workspaces\"` array, OR a `pnpm-workspace.yaml` / `lerna.json` exists at the root.\n- **Rust:** root `Cargo.toml` has `[workspace]` with a `members = [...]` list.\n- **Python:** repo contains multiple `pyproject.toml` under `packages/*/` or `apps/*/`.\n- **Generic:** top-level `apps/`, `packages/`, `code/`, or `libs/` directory each containing manifests.\n\nIf a workspace layout is detected, list the discovered workspaces and ask:\n\n```\nThis looks like a monorepo with these workspaces:\n  1. {workspace_name} ({path})\n  2. {workspace_name} ({path})\n  ...\nWhich one should the skill cover? Pick a number, type 'all' to scope at the repo root, or 'list' to keep listing more.\n```\n\nInteractive: wait for the user choice. On a numbered choice, store `monorepo_workspace: {path}` and rebase §2-§4b against that path. On `'all'`, leave `monorepo_workspace` unset and proceed at the repo root with a note in the analysis summary that scope is unfiltered.\n\nHeadless: if the input contract supplied an `include` glob that begins with one of the workspace paths, auto-select that workspace (log `\"headless: auto-selected workspace {name} from include glob\"`). Otherwise default to repo root and log `\"warn: monorepo detected but no workspace pre-selected — analyzing at repo root\"`.\n\nIf no workspace layout is detected, skip this section silently.",
  "new_string": "### 1b. Detect Monorepo / Workspace Layout\n\nDelegate workspace detection to `{detectWorkspacesScript}` instead of reasoning through manifest rules in prose. Build a payload from the tree fetched in §1 plus the small set of root manifests the detector needs, then invoke the script:\n\n```bash\necho '{\"tree\": [<flat list of repo-relative file paths>], \"manifests\": {\"package.json\": \"<raw text>\", \"Cargo.toml\": \"<raw text>\", \"pnpm-workspace.yaml\": \"<raw text>\", \"lerna.json\": \"<raw text>\"}}' | \\\n  uv run {detectWorkspacesScript}\n```\n\n- **`tree`** — pass the flat list of repo-relative file paths already fetched in §1 (for GitHub: the `path` values from the `gh api .../git/trees/HEAD?recursive=1` response; for local: the equivalent listing).\n- **`manifests`** — only the root manifests need contents; child-workspace manifests are looked up from the tree by the script. Include any of `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `lerna.json` that appears at the repo root. Fetch them in **one message with N parallel Bash calls** (`gh api .../contents/{path}` for GitHub, file reads for local), then base64-decode together. Per-workspace manifest contents (e.g. `packages/foo/package.json`) are optional — including them populates the workspace `name` field with the manifest's declared package name; omitting them falls back to the directory basename.\n\nThe script returns a JSON envelope: `{is_monorepo, manifest_kind, workspaces[], warnings[]}`. Apply the result deterministically — see `src/shared/scripts/schemas/workspace-detection.v1.json` for the full contract.\n\n**If `is_monorepo: false`** — skip this section silently and continue to §2.\n\n**If `is_monorepo: true`** — present the discovered workspaces and prompt:\n\n```\nThis looks like a monorepo ({manifest_kind}) with these workspaces:\n  1. {workspaces[0].name} ({workspaces[0].path})\n  2. {workspaces[1].name} ({workspaces[1].path})\n  ...\nWhich one should the skill cover? Pick a number, type 'all' to scope at the repo root, or 'list' to keep listing more.\n```\n\nInteractive: wait for the user choice. On a numbered choice, store `monorepo_workspace: {path}` and rebase §2-§4b against that path. On `'all'`, leave `monorepo_workspace` unset and proceed at the repo root with a note in the analysis summary that scope is unfiltered.\n\nHeadless: if the input contract supplied an `include` glob that begins with one of the workspace paths, auto-select that workspace (log `\"headless: auto-selected workspace {name} from include glob\"`). Otherwise default to repo root and log `\"warn: monorepo detected ({manifest_kind}) but no workspace pre-selected — analyzing at repo root\"`.\n\nSurface any non-empty `warnings[]` from the script to the operator log so a malformed root manifest is debuggable; the workflow does not HALT — falling back to repo-root analysis is always safe."
}
