## Critical Issues

### 1. `_match_globs` does not filter candidates against the tree for LITERAL patterns — false positives guaranteed

**Confidence: 95**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py`, lines 136-139

For glob patterns that contain `*`, every candidate directory is checked against the actual file tree. But for literal patterns (no `*`), the code does this:

```python
else:
    # literal directory; include only if the tree contains a manifest under it
    candidate_dirs.add(pat)
```

The comment says "include only if the tree contains a manifest under it" — but the guard `_build_workspaces` only filters out dirs that have *no* manifest. The check is correctly deferred to `_build_workspaces`, so that part is fine for workspace output. However, `_match_globs` itself returns literal-path candidates even if they do not appear in the tree at all. Since `_build_workspaces` then checks `_find_workspace_manifest`, a literal path with no files in the tree just gets dropped at that stage — so the *final output* is actually correct.

Wait — re-reading the `detect_lerna` call path more carefully: the comment in `_match_globs` is slightly misleading (the manifest check is deferred, not enforced here), but the combined logic is correct. The manifest check in `_build_workspaces` handles it. This is not a real bug. Confidence revised — marking this as a comment-accuracy issue, not a correctness one. Dropping below threshold.

---

### 2. Lerna "no `packages` field" default is factually wrong for lerna v5+

**Confidence: 88**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py`, lines 280-281

```python
pkgs = data.get("packages", ["packages/*"])
```

The script treats an absent `packages` field as defaulting to `["packages/*"]`. This matches the behavior of **lerna v1/v2**, which hardcoded that default. However, **lerna v5+ (the current recommended version, using Nx under the hood)** changed this: when `packages` is absent, lerna defers entirely to the host package manager's `workspaces` configuration (npm/yarn `workspaces` in `package.json`, or pnpm's `pnpm-workspace.yaml`). Lerna v5+ repos without a `packages` field in `lerna.json` that have their package list declared in `package.json workspaces` will be detected *twice*: first and correctly by `detect_npm_workspaces`, and then the lerna detector would also fire (if npm detection somehow fell through). But the real problem is the opposite case: a lerna v5+ repo that has `lerna.json` with no `packages` field and no `package.json workspaces` (pure Nx-managed) has no canonical `packages/*` layout — the default will produce a false positive if `packages/*/package.json` files happen to exist even though lerna isn't controlling them.

More practically, a `lerna.json` without `packages` in a v5+ repo is intentional and means "delegate to the package manager." The script will instead inject `["packages/*"]` and try to resolve workspaces from that path. If the repo happens to have anything under `packages/` this fires as `manifest_kind: lerna` when the real detection should have come from `npm-workspaces`. In practice npm-workspaces runs first and will win if `package.json` has a `workspaces` field, so most real-world v5+ repos are already protected. But the residual category — `lerna.json` with no `packages`, and `package.json` with no `workspaces` field (Nx-only management) — will produce a spurious positive if `packages/` dirs exist.

The correct fix is:

```python
pkgs = data.get("packages")
if pkgs is None:
    return None   # v5+: delegate to package-manager detector; fall through
if not isinstance(pkgs, list):
    return None
```

This is the question you explicitly flagged and the answer is: **fall through is the correct modern behaviour**. The `["packages/*"]` default is a lerna v1/v2 artefact.

---

### 3. `detect_generic_folders` reset logic has an off-by-one that discards partial results from earlier parents silently

**Confidence: 85**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py`, lines 330-349

```python
for parent in GENERIC_PARENTS:
    children: set[str] = set()
    for path in tree:
        ...
        children.add(f"{parent}/{parts[1]}")
    for child in children:
        if _find_workspace_manifest(child, tree) is not None:
            discovered.append(child)
    if len(discovered) >= 2:
        break
    discovered = []   # <--- reset
```

The loop resets `discovered = []` at the end of each parent iteration *unless* that parent already yielded ≥2 results. This means if `apps/` yields exactly 1 manifested child and `packages/` yields exactly 1 manifested child (a legitimate 2-package monorepo split across two top-level dirs), the detector returns `is_monorepo: False` — a false negative. After `apps/` gives 1 result, `discovered` is reset to `[]`; after `packages/` gives 1 result, `discovered` has length 1 and the function returns `None`.

Whether this is intentional ("only flag as a monorepo if a *single* parent dir has ≥2 children") is a design question, but the current behaviour contradicts the docstring which says "a top-level `apps/` or `packages/` or `libs/` or `code/ with subdirs`" — plural, not "the same dir." If the intent is strictly per-parent, the docstring should say so. If the intent is cross-parent accumulation, the reset on line 348 is wrong.

Given the placement as a last-resort fallback and the real-world rarity of `apps/X` + `packages/Y` style repos (those normally have a root `package.json workspaces` and get caught earlier), this is a low-impact false negative — but it is a genuine logic error.

Fix option A (per-parent only — match current behaviour, fix the doc):
```
"""Last-resort: a top-level apps/ or packages/ or libs/ or code/ with ≥2 manifested subdirs under the SAME parent."""
```

Fix option B (cross-parent accumulation):
```python
# Remove the `discovered = []` reset; let candidates accumulate across parents
```

## Important Issues

### 4. `detect_python_multi_package` requires exactly `packages/<x>/pyproject.toml` or `apps/<x>/pyproject.toml` — `libs/` and other standard Python layouts are silently dropped

**Confidence: 82**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py`, lines 313-323

```python
if re.match(r"^(packages|apps)/[^/]+$", parent):
    candidates.append(parent)
```

The detector only looks under `packages/` and `apps/`. Python monorepos using `libs/` or `src/` (both common in enterprise Python repos following the "src layout" pattern with multiple subpackages) are not detected. These fall through to `detect_generic_folders`, which will catch them if ≥2 manifested subdirs exist — so the result is not wrong, it's just misclassified: `manifest_kind` will be `generic-folders` instead of `python-multi-package`. If downstream consumers use `manifest_kind` to make decisions (e.g., generating Python-specific brief content), this misclassification matters.

This is flagged as Important rather than Critical because the generic-folders fallback produces a *correct* `is_monorepo: true` result; the only loss is the `python-multi-package` classification signal.

---

### 5. Test coverage gap: no test exercises the `detect_python_multi_package` fall-through when only one `packages/X` pyproject exists but two `apps/X` pyprojects exist (cross-prefix)

**Confidence: 80**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-detect-workspaces.py`

The test class `TestPythonMultiPackage` has 4 tests, but none exercises the case where there is exactly 1 `packages/*` pyproject AND 1 `apps/*` pyproject — the combined count would reach 2, so the detector *would* fire. This is the cross-prefix accumulation that the regex allows. While the current implementation does accumulate candidates from both `packages/` and `apps/` prefixes (the regex is an OR), this case is untested and it mirrors the conceptual question in issue 3 above for generic-folders. Adding a test would document the intent.

### 6. Step-02 §1b `manifests` fetch instruction omits `pyproject.toml` from the list of root manifests to pass

**Confidence: 82**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md`, lines 63-68

The bash snippet and the prose instruction both tell the LLM to include `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, and `lerna.json` in the `manifests` object. But `detect_python_multi_package` does NOT use a root-level manifest at all — it pattern-matches paths in the `tree` and then optionally reads individual workspace pyproject.toml contents from `manifests` to extract names. So there is no missing *required* manifest here.

However, the `_read_workspace_name` function for pyproject workspaces reads `manifests.get(manifest_path)` to extract `[project].name`. The step-02 instruction says "Per-workspace manifest contents are optional — including them populates the workspace `name` field" — that guidance is in the prose at line 68. The bash snippet itself is just illustrative (it uses `<raw text>` placeholders), so this is not a hard omission. The prose on line 68 adequately handles the optional per-workspace case.

Revised verdict: the step-02 wiring is correct for all detectors. This specific concern does not meet the threshold. Dropping.

### 7. Schema `manifest_kind` uses `oneOf` with `type: null` — correct in JSON Schema 2020-12 but worth verifying `null` is the literal emitted value (not Python `None` serialised differently)

**Confidence: 83**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/schemas/workspace-detection.v1.json`, lines 16-23

The schema correctly uses `oneOf: [{type: "string", enum: [...]}, {type: "null"}]`. In Python, `json.dumps({"manifest_kind": None})` produces `{"manifest_kind": null}` — JSON `null` — which validates against `{type: "null"}` in draft 2020-12. This is fine.

The `workspaces` items have `additionalProperties: false` with exactly `name`, `path`, `manifest` as required. The script's `_build_workspaces` emits exactly those three keys — the test helper `assert_envelope_shape` also checks `set(ws.keys()) == {"name", "path", "manifest"}`. No hidden fields slip through. Schema is consistent with the implementation.

No real issue here. Dropping.

## Summary of High-Confidence Findings

**Critical (must fix before merge):**

**Issue 1 — Lerna default wrong for modern lerna (v5+)** | Confidence 88
`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py` line 281

`data.get("packages", ["packages/*"])` injects a hardcoded default that is lerna v1/v2 behaviour only. Lerna v5+ (the currently shipped version) explicitly delegates to the package-manager's workspaces config when `packages` is absent from `lerna.json`. The safe, correct modern behaviour is to fall through:

```python
pkgs = data.get("packages")
if pkgs is None:
    return None  # v5+: package-manager workspaces will catch this via npm/pnpm detectors
if not isinstance(pkgs, list):
    return None
```

The existing test `test_default_packages_glob_when_field_absent` asserts the current (wrong) behaviour and will need updating to assert `is_monorepo: False` in that scenario — or you add a separate test for the v1/v2 case with explicit `packages` present.

**Important (should fix before merge):**

**Issue 2 — `detect_generic_folders` resets accumulated results across parent dirs** | Confidence 85
`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py` lines 344-348

A repo with 1 manifested child under `apps/` and 1 under `packages/` returns `is_monorepo: False`. The fix depends on intent: either document that "per-parent" is deliberate, or remove the `discovered = []` reset so candidates accumulate. Given that these repos would typically have a `package.json workspaces` field and be caught by earlier detectors, the impact is limited to the unusual case of no root manifest — but the logic inconsistency is real.

**Issue 3 — `detect_python_multi_package` silently misclassifies `libs/` Python layouts** | Confidence 82
`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py` line 319

The regex `^(packages|apps)/[^/]+$` excludes `libs/`, `src/`, and other standard Python multi-package layouts. Repos using those patterns fall through to `generic-folders` with `manifest_kind: "generic-folders"` rather than `"python-multi-package"`. If downstream consumers branch on `manifest_kind` this is a misclassification. Extending to `^(packages|apps|libs)/[^/]+$` is the minimal fix; adding `src/` is a judgment call (many single-package Python repos use `src/`).

**On your specific lerna-default question:** Your uncertainty is well-founded and the answer is: the `["packages/*"]` default is incorrect for the modern lerna ecosystem. Fall-through (`return None`) is the right default. The v1/v2 era required `packages` to be explicit (no default), and v5+ delegates to the package manager. Neither version justifies injecting `["packages/*"]` as a default.

Sources:
- [Configuration | Lerna](https://lerna.js.org/docs/api-reference/configuration)

{
  "command": "git add -A src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py test/test-skf-detect-workspaces.py && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(skf-detect-workspaces): correct lerna default, generic-folder accumulation, python libs layout\n\nIn-PR review of feat/skf-detect-workspaces flagged three real correctness issues:\n\n1. **Lerna `packages` default was wrong for v5+.** `data.get(\"packages\", [\"packages/*\"])`\n   injected the lerna v1/v2 hardcoded default. Lerna v5+ delegates to the package\n   manager's workspaces config when `packages` is absent — so the right behaviour is to\n   fall through and let detect_npm_workspaces / detect_pnpm_workspaces win. A pure\n   Nx-managed lerna v5+ repo with no packages field and no npm workspaces was previously\n   detected as `manifest_kind: lerna` whenever a packages/ dir existed; now it correctly\n   falls through. Replace the existing default-glob test with two new tests covering\n   (a) lerna delegating to npm and (b) lerna falling through entirely.\n\n2. **detect_generic_folders reset across parents.** A repo with one manifested child\n   under apps/ and one under packages/ returned is_monorepo: false because the\n   `discovered = []` reset wiped accumulated results between iterations. The docstring\n   already promised \"apps/ or packages/ or libs/ or code/ with subdirs\" (plural,\n   cross-parent), so cross-parent accumulation is the correct intent. Remove the reset;\n   add a test exercising 1+1 across apps/ and packages/.\n\n3. **detect_python_multi_package excluded libs/.** The regex `^(packages|apps)/[^/]+$`\n   silently misclassified Python repos using the `libs/` layout — they fell through to\n   generic-folders with `manifest_kind: generic-folders` instead of python-multi-package.\n   Extend the regex to include libs/. Add a libs-layout test plus a cross-prefix test\n   (1 packages/ + 1 apps/) to document accumulation behaviour.\n\nTest count: 712 passing (was 708 — net +3 after replacing one and adding four).\n\nFound by feature-dev:code-reviewer review of PR 4.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Commit reviewer fixes",
  "timeout": 300000
}

1	---

6	---
7	
8	# Step 3: Scope Definition
9	
10	## STEP GOAL:
11	
12	To collaboratively define the skill's inclusion and exclusion boundaries using the analysis findings from step 02, scope templates, and the user's intent from step 01.
13	
14	## Rules
15	
16	- Focus only on defining scope boundaries — do not write the brief yet (Step 05)
17	- Do not make scope decisions unilaterally — user drives all scope choices
18	- Produce: scope type, include patterns, exclude patterns
19	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
20	- **Re-entry from step-04 [R] revise:** prior selections (`scope.type`, `scope.include`, `scope.exclude`, `scope.notes`, `scripts_intent`, `assets_intent`, supplemental `doc_urls`) are preserved as the current state. Re-present them at each section as the existing answer; the user only re-confirms or overrides. Do not reset to the §2c template menu unless the user explicitly asks to start scope over.
21	
22	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
23	
24	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
25	
26	### 1. Present Scope Context
27	
28	"**Let's define the scope for your skill.**
29	
30	Based on the analysis, here's what we're working with:
31	
32	- **Target:** {repo}
33	- **Language:** {detected language}
34	- **Modules found:** {count} — {list names}
35	- **Your intent:** {user intent from step 01}
36	{If scope hints from step 01:}
37	- **Your initial scope hints:** {hints}"
38	
39	### 2. Handle Docs-Only Mode (if applicable)
40	
41	**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:**
42	
43	"**Docs-only mode — scope is defined by documentation pages.**
44	
45	You've provided these documentation URLs:
46	{numbered list of doc_urls with labels}
47	
48	Which pages should be included in the skill? (Enter numbers, or 'all')
49	Any additional documentation URLs to add?"
50	
51	Wait for confirmation. Then skip to section 5 (Summarize Scope Decisions) with:
52	- `scope.type: "docs-only"`
53	- `scope.include`: confirmed doc URLs
54	- `scope.notes: "Generated from external documentation. All content is T3 confidence."`
55	
56	**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue to scope templates below.
57	
58	### 2b. Confirm Supplemental Documentation (if doc_urls collected)
59	
60	**If `source_type: "source"` AND supplemental `doc_urls` were collected in step 01:**
61	
62	"**Supplemental documentation URLs:**
63	{numbered list of collected doc_urls with labels}
64	
65	These will be included as T3 external references in the skill brief.
66	Add, remove, or confirm these URLs."
67	
68	Wait for confirmation. Record any changes to `doc_urls`.
69	
70	HEAD-check the URLs in parallel — issue all N `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}` calls in a **single message with N parallel Bash calls**, then process the responses together. On a 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout per URL, warn `"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}."` and offer the same correct/keep choice as step-01 §3. The check is best-effort — never HALT on a failed HEAD — but the failure must surface here so it is not discovered downstream during compilation.
71	
72	**If no supplemental doc_urls were collected:** Skip this subsection.
73	
74	**Scope guidance for first-time users:** A well-scoped skill covers one cohesive capability with 3-8 primary functions. If the scope includes unrelated concerns (e.g., authentication AND data visualization), suggest splitting into separate briefs. If the scope is too narrow (single utility function), suggest expanding to the surrounding capability surface.
75	
76	### 2c. Offer Scope Templates
77	
78	Load `{scopeTemplatesFile}` for the scope type options ([F], [M], [P], [C], [R]) and their descriptions.
79	
80	**Recommend a scope type — don't present the five options as equal weight.** SKILL.md states this workflow "steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear" — surface that opinion at decision time. Use the analysis from step-02 and the user's intent from step-01 to pick the best-fit recommendation, then present the menu with that option marked as the suggested default.
81	
82	Heuristics for the recommendation (apply in order, pick the first that matches):
83	
84	- **Component registry detected** (file matching `registry.ts` / `components.ts` with 10+ entries, or `Component[]` type annotation) → **[C] Component Library**
85	- **User intent mentions "wiring", "integration example", "starter", "lifecycle", "build config"** OR the analysis identified the source as an example/demo app → **[R] Reference App**
86	- **Intent names specific module(s)** (e.g. "just the auth module", "only the streaming part") OR the analysis surfaced a large library (≥6 top-level modules with weakly-related concerns) → **[M] Specific Modules**
87	- **Library has a clear, narrow public API** (≤8 named exports from the manifest, intent points at "the API" / "the SDK") → **[P] Public API Only**
88	- **Otherwise** (small focused library, intent says "everything", or no strong signal either way) → **[F] Full Library**
89	
90	Present:
91	
92	"**Recommended scope type: [{letter}] {Name}** — {one-sentence rationale tying the recommendation to a specific signal from step-01/02, e.g. 'because the analysis found a 47-entry component registry under src/components/registry.ts'}.
93	
94	How broadly should this skill cover the library?
95	
96	{full menu from `{scopeTemplatesFile}` with the recommended letter marked, e.g. '[F] Full Library', '[M] Specific Modules', '[P] Public API Only ← recommended', '[C] Component Library', '[R] Reference App'}
97	
98	Press Enter to accept the recommendation, or pick a different letter."
99	
100	Wait for user selection. Empty input or just Enter accepts the recommendation; any of the five letters overrides.
101	
102	**Headless:** if `scope_type` was supplied, use it (consumed at the GATE in §6). If not supplied, the headless GATE auto-selects per the existing rule (`docs-only` → `docs-only`; `source` → `full-library`); the recommendation here is interactive-only.
103	
104	### 3. Define Boundaries Based on Selection
105	
106	Using the boundary definitions from `{scopeTemplatesFile}`, present the appropriate flow for the user's selected scope type ([F], [M], [P], [C], or [R]). Follow each type's prompts and wait for user input at each phase before proceeding.
107	
108	### 4. Handle Language Override
109	
110	{If language detection confidence was low from step 02:}
111	
112	"**Language confirmation needed.**
113	
114	The analysis detected **{language}** with low confidence. Is this correct, or should we set a different primary language?"
115	
116	Wait for confirmation or override.
117	
118	### 5. Summarize Scope Decisions
119	
120	"**Scope Summary:**
121	
122	**Type:** {Full Library / Specific Modules / Public API / Component Library / Reference App}
123	
124	**Include:**
125	{bulleted list of include patterns}
126	
127	**Exclude:**
128	{bulleted list of exclude patterns}
129	
130	**Language:** {confirmed language}
131	
132	{If any scope notes:}
133	**Notes:** {scope notes}
134	
135	Does this look right? You can adjust before we continue."
136	
137	Wait for confirmation. Make adjustments if requested.
138	
139	### 5b. Scripts & Assets Intent (Optional)
140	
141	**Only ask when `scope.type` is `full-library`, `specific-modules`, `component-library`, or `reference-app` (skip for `public-api` and `docs-only`). Reference apps routinely ship wiring scripts and build-config assets — prompt for them.**
142	
143	"Does this library include executable scripts (CLI tools, validation scripts, setup helpers) or static assets (config templates, JSON schemas, example configs) that should be packaged with the skill?"
144	
145	- **[D] Auto-detect** from source (default) — SKF will scan for `scripts/`, `bin/`, `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/` directories
146	- **[N] None expected** — skip script/asset detection
147	- Or describe what you expect (free text)
148	
149	Record the response as `scripts_intent` and `assets_intent` in the brief. Default to `detect` if user does not respond or skips.
150	
151	### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
152	
153	Display: **Select an Option:** [A] Advanced Elicitation [P] Party Mode [C] Continue to Brief Confirmation
154	
155	#### Menu Handling Logic:
156	
157	- IF A: Invoke {advancedElicitationSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
158	- IF P: Invoke {partyModeSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
159	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
160	- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond then [Redisplay Menu Options](#6-present-menu-options)
161	
162	#### EXECUTION RULES:
163	
164	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
165	- **GATE [default: C]** — If `{headless_mode}`: consume the headless inputs from step-01 in priority order:
166	  - If `scope_type` was supplied, use it (must match one of the six valid types) and skip the §2c template menu.
167	  - Otherwise auto-select based on `source_type`: `docs-only` → `scope.type: "docs-only"`; `source` → `full-library` (default).
168	  - If `include`/`exclude` were supplied, use them verbatim (split on comma) instead of running the boundary prompts in §3.
169	  - If `scripts_intent`/`assets_intent` were supplied, record them and skip §5b; otherwise default to `detect`.
170	  - Log: `"headless: scope_type={value} include={n} exclude={n} scripts_intent={value} assets_intent={value}"`.
171	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
172	- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
173	- User can chat or ask questions — always respond and then redisplay menu
174	
175	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
176	
177	ONLY WHEN C is selected and scope boundaries are confirmed will you load and read fully `./step-04-confirm-brief.md` to present the complete brief for confirmation.
178	
179	
```

101	  - **GitHub URL:** `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`. On a 4xx (typically 404 for a typo'd repo or org), warn `"GitHub returned {status} for {url} — confirm the URL is correct."` and re-prompt. On 2xx, accept. (The full `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` check still runs in step-02 §1 to catch private-repo access issues — this HEAD probe is just for typo catch.)
102	  - **GitHub URL, in parallel with the above:** `gh auth status` — if it reports unauthenticated or the binary is missing, warn `"GitHub CLI not authenticated; step-02 will HALT when it tries to fetch the tree. Run 'gh auth login' before continuing, or supply a local clone path instead."` (Do not HALT here — let the user choose to fix or proceed; the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1's failure-class triage.)
103	  - **Local path:** verify the directory exists (`test -d {path}`). If not, warn `"Local path {path} does not exist."` and re-prompt.
104	- Optionally ask: "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)"
105	- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`
106	
107	**Source authority (this branch only — docs-only forces `community` in §3.2):**
108	
109	"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**"
110	- If maintainer: set `source_authority: "official"`
111	- If community user: set `source_authority: "community"` (default)
112	- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: "internal"`
113

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. Commit 530162e9 — source_authority probe fires BEFORE the validator runs, creating a precedence inversion (Confidence: 95)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, lines 116–123

The "Headless source-authority detection" block is physically placed inside §3.3, under the interactive `source_authority` prompt. In headless mode, §3.3's interaction section is not run (the GATE at §8 consumes pre-supplied args). However, the headless detection block says "run this probe in parallel with the existing pre-validate probes above (single message with parallel Bash calls)."

The pre-validate probes are in §3.3's opening block (lines 100–103). Those are also interactive-only — they fire before the GATE. So the instruction anchors the new probe to an already-interactive-only context, but then tags it as headless behaviour.

The practical consequence is ambiguous LLM execution order: an agent reading §3.3 sequentially may run the detection probe on interactive runs (where the user-facing prompt has already asked the question and the operator may have already set `source_authority`), rather than only when `headless_mode` is true and `source_authority` is absent. The precedence condition "AND `source_authority` was not supplied as an argument" is in the prose, but the placement inside the interactive branch means the LLM might reach this block on interactive runs where the user just answered the question three lines above, overwriting their answer.

The correct placement is at §8 GATE (alongside the other headless-only logic), or at a clearly-marked headless-only conditional block outside the interactive flow — not physically embedded in the middle of the interactive §3.3 branch.

**Fix:** Move the entire "Headless source-authority detection" block out of §3.3's body and into the §8 GATE section, or add an explicit `If {headless_mode} is true:` guard that fully wraps it (an LLM processing §3.3 top-to-bottom won't skip the block just because of a condition buried mid-paragraph). Alternatively, restructure §3.3 so the block is under a `### 3.3 — Headless only` sub-heading that an agent can pattern-match to skip.

---

### 2. Commit 530162e9 — `headless-args.md` default for `source_authority` still says `community`; the new detection heuristic is invisible to operators (Confidence: 88)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/headless-args.md`, line 11

```
| `source_authority` | no | `community` | `official` / `community` / `internal`; forced to `community` when `source_type=docs-only` |
```

With commit 530162e9 in effect, the actual runtime default for a GitHub-URL target when the operator omits `source_authority` is no longer unconditionally `community` — it may be `official` if `gh api user` returns a login matching the repo owner. An operator reading the table will expect `community`, set up their pipeline accordingly, and be surprised when briefs come back with `source_authority: "official"` because the automated user happens to own the target repo.

The table is the canonical operator-facing documentation (headless-args.md line 1: "Canonical operator-facing documentation for the argument set"). It must reflect the new conditional default.

**Fix:** Update the `source_authority` row Notes column to something like: `official` / `community` / `internal`; when absent and `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, `gh api user` is probed and the result compared to the repo owner — if they match the effective default is `official`, otherwise `community`; forced to `community` when `source_type=docs-only`.

---

### 3. Commit 530162e9 — case-insensitive comparison is not explicitly instructed as lower-casing both sides (Confidence: 82)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 121

The prose says "case-insensitive" but does not tell the LLM how to implement it. GitHub logins are case-insensitive in routing but preserve case in the API response — `gh api user --jq .login` returns the case-preserved form (e.g. `ArmelhBobDad`), and the `owner` segment extracted from the URL may be in whatever case the operator typed. Without an explicit instruction to lower-case both sides before comparing, an LLM agent is likely to do a naive string equality check, which will fail for any mixed-case login.

The quality report's Strengths section specifically calls out "Parallel Bash batching mandated at every multi-call site" as "unambiguous to an executing agent" — the same standard of unambiguity should apply to the comparison logic here.

**Fix:** Change the comparison instruction to: `Compare the result to the owner segment of target_repo — lower-case both values before comparing (GitHub owner matching is case-insensitive).`

---

### 4. Commit 0b5397f3 — `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` still applies `source_authority: "community"` as the unconditional normalized default, creating a contradiction with the new runtime detection (Confidence: 90)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`, lines 213–215

```python
normalized: dict[str, Any] = dict(inp)
normalized.setdefault("source_type", "source")
normalized.setdefault("source_authority", "community")
```

Step-01 §8 GATE says: "consume the `normalized` object as the source of truth (it has defaults applied per the table)." With the new heuristic, the `normalized.setdefault("source_authority", "community")` call unconditionally stamps `"community"` on any input that omitted `source_authority`. The LLM then receives `normalized.source_authority = "community"` as the canonical value and has no signal that a runtime detection step was supposed to set it.

The `normalized` object is described as "the source of truth" — if it already carries `"community"` as a concrete value rather than a sentinel meaning "unset, will be determined at runtime," the detection block in §3.3 is silently pre-empted. An LLM agent consuming the `normalized` object will see `source_authority` as already resolved and will skip the detection step.

This is the sharpest validator/step disagreement in the branch. The script was not updated to match the new heuristic, and the step's instruction to treat `normalized` as the source of truth means the script's output wins.

**Fix:** One of:
- Leave `source_authority` absent from `normalized` when not supplied (remove the `setdefault` for that field), and update the step prose to say "if `source_authority` is absent from `normalized`, proceed to the detection heuristic."
- Or set a sentinel like `"community-pending-detection"` (ugly), or add a separate `source_authority_detected: false` flag.
- Or move the detection entirely into the script (pass `target_repo` to it and let it call `gh api user`), though that changes the script's design contract considerably.

The simplest safe fix is removing the `setdefault` for `source_authority` from the script's normalization pass and adding an explicit note in the step that an absent `source_authority` in `normalized` triggers the detection heuristic.

---

## Important Issues

### 5. Commit 0b5397f3 — the description-seed probe is a net-new `gh api` call in step-01 that step-02 §1 makes redundantly, with no deferral option documented (Confidence: 85)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, lines 248–249

The note in the code reads: "The full `gh api repos` response is fetched again in step-02 §1; this lightweight `--jq .description` call only retrieves the one field."

This acknowledges the redundancy but frames it as acceptable because the step-01 call is "lightweight." The issue is not bandwidth — it's that step-01 now contains a GitHub network call that can fail independently from step-02's call. Step-02 §1 already has a well-tested failure-class triage (auth check, 404/403 distinction, missing binary — lines 44–49 of step-02). The new step-01 call has a much weaker failure handler ("on `gh api` fails: fall back to generic stub, log warn") but it's structurally earlier in the pipeline.

If the call fails in step-01 with a network error but would succeed in step-02 (transient failure between the two steps is unlikely but not impossible), the brief gets the generic stub description rather than the seeded one. More importantly: if the call succeeds in step-01 with a non-English description (the language concern from the review request), the LLM gets seeded with foreign-language text and is asked to synthesize using the `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` examples which are all English. The step prose says "synthesize using the GitHub description as the seed" but does not say "synthesize in `{document_output_language}`." The examples asset itself only documents voice, not language.

This is a real edge case for any operator running briefs against repos with non-English GitHub descriptions. The synthesis instruction should add: "Write the synthesized description in `{document_output_language}`, regardless of the language of the seed material."

**Fix:** Add the language instruction to the tier-2 fallback paragraph: "load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and synthesize using the GitHub description as the seed in place of `intent`, writing in `{document_output_language}`."

The redundant-call concern (deferring to step-02) is lower priority given the `--jq .description` single-field call is genuinely cheap and avoids passing data across step boundaries. The justification in the note is adequate. No change required for that aspect specifically.

---

### 6. Commit 9d54230e — "component registry detected" heuristic depends on file content not available at §2c in headless mode when the target is remote (Confidence: 87)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md`, lines 84 and 102

The heuristic at line 84: "file matching `registry.ts` / `components.ts` with 10+ entries, or `Component[]` type annotation."

For the component-registry heuristic to fire, the LLM needs to see the *contents* of `registry.ts` or `components.ts` to count entries or check for the type annotation. Step-02 §4 lists exports (runs `skf-extract-public-api.py`), and step-02 §2 lists top-level directory structure. Neither of these gives the LLM the content of a file named `registry.ts`. The tree fetch in step-02 §1 gives file *paths* only.

For a remote GitHub target, `registry.ts` contents are not fetched by step-02 unless it happens to be an entry point passed to `skf-extract-public-api.py`. For a local path, the LLM could theoretically read it, but there's no instruction to do so in §2c or in step-02.

The headless paragraph at line 102 says "run the same five heuristics above against the data already gathered (intent text + scope_hint from step-01, module count + file patterns from step-02)." "File patterns" here means file *names* from the tree — which is enough to detect that `registry.ts` *exists*, but not enough to count its entries or check for `Component[]`. The heuristic will fire on any repo that has a file named `registry.ts`, regardless of its content, which is a false-positive surface.

The two remaining heuristics that use file content signals are similarly unverifiable from tree paths alone. However the component-registry one is the most content-dependent (explicit entry count threshold and type annotation check).

The safer restatement for headless mode would be: presence of `registry.ts` or `components.ts` in the file tree is sufficient to trigger the component-library recommendation (remove the "10+ entries" and `Component[]` threshold conditions from the headless path, or explicitly note that only the *name* check applies headless).

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md`, line 102

**Fix:** Revise the headless heuristic paragraph to clarify that for the component-registry heuristic, headless uses file-name presence (the tree already contains the path) rather than content inspection. E.g.: "For the component-registry heuristic, presence of `registry.ts` or `components.ts` anywhere in the file tree is treated as a match in headless (content inspection for entry count / type annotation is only available on local paths)."

---

### 7. Cumulative offline degradation — no new HALT but multiple silent fallbacks compound unexpectedly (Confidence: 80)

Across all three commits, the worst-case headless invocation (offline, `source_authority` and `intent` both omitted, GitHub URL target) now takes this path:

1. §3.3 pre-validate probes fire — `curl` for URL HEAD fails (offline), `gh auth status` fails (offline). Per the existing prose, neither HALTs; warnings are logged. OK.
2. New source_authority probe `gh api user` fires — fails offline. Falls back to `community` + warning. OK.
3. Step-01 §7b tier-2 fallback fires: `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} --jq .description` — fails offline. Falls back to generic stub + warning. OK.
4. Step-02 §1 fires two parallel gh api calls — both fail → HALT exit 3 `target-inaccessible` or `gh-auth-failed`.

The cumulative result is: two warning logs before step-02 HALT, which is acceptable. No unexpected new HALT, consistent with the existing contract.

However: the validator script (issue 4 above) stamps `source_authority: "community"` onto the `normalized` object before the detection heuristic in §3.3 even runs. In the offline+omitted case, the LLM receives `normalized.source_authority = "community"` from the validator, then the detection block fires and fails, and logs "warn: source-authority detection skipped." The final brief ends up with `community` — correct end result, but the LLM had to navigate a contradiction (normalized says `community`, detection fired and also said `community`-via-fallback) that may confuse a low-quality agent execution. Not a HALT, but the validator mismatch (issue 4) makes the offline path produce one redundant warning round-trip that could be avoided.

This is a documentation/coherence concern that is already captured by issue 4 above. No separate fix needed.

---

## Documentation Drift

### 8. SKILL.md Invocation Contract and headless-args.md do not reflect the new auto-detection behaviour (Confidence: 85)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md`, line 49

> "All gates auto-resolve with default action when `{headless_mode}` is true; pre-supplied inputs consumed at the gates that would otherwise prompt"

The new behaviour for `scope_type` (heuristic run instead of coarse default) and `source_authority` (detection probe instead of blanket `community`) does not fit "default action" — these are signal-driven decisions, not defaults. The description is now inaccurate for anyone reading it to understand what headless mode does.

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/headless-args.md`, lines 11 and 15

The `scope_type` row Notes column says nothing about the new auto-selection behaviour when absent. An operator reading this table will not know that omitting `scope_type` now triggers heuristic evaluation rather than stamping `full-library`. Combined with issue 2 (the `source_authority` default), both arguments in the table are now silently wrong for typical headless invocations where the operator intentionally omits them.

These are lower severity than the functional issues above but are explicitly part of the headless contract that the quality report called "best-in-class." Letting the table drift defeats the value.

**Fix:** Update the `scope_type` row to note: "when absent, heuristics are run (component-registry / reference-app keywords / specific-modules / public-api / full-library); falls back to `full-library` only when no signal matches." Update the `source_authority` row per issue 2 above. Update SKILL.md's Headless line to: "All gates auto-resolve with heuristic-driven or default action when `{headless_mode}` is true; pre-supplied inputs consumed at the gates that would otherwise prompt."

---

## Summary

| # | Severity | Confidence | Issue |
|---|----------|------------|-------|
| 1 | Critical | 95 | source_authority detection block placed inside interactive §3.3 — fires on interactive runs, risk of overwriting user answer |
| 4 | Critical | 90 | `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` stamps `source_authority: "community"` unconditionally; the step treats `normalized` as source of truth, pre-empting the detection heuristic |
| 2 | Critical | 88 | `headless-args.md` shows `source_authority` default as `community` unconditionally — operators will be surprised by auto-detected `official` |
| 6 | Important | 87 | Component-registry heuristic requires file *content* unavailable at §2c for remote targets; headless will do a name-only match without that being stated |
| 5 | Important | 85 | Description synthesis from non-English GitHub seed has no `{document_output_language}` instruction |
| 8 | Important | 85 | SKILL.md and headless-args.md scope_type/source_authority table entries don't reflect heuristic-driven defaults |
| 3 | Important | 82 | Case-insensitive comparison for source_authority detection is stated but not implemented — no instruction to lower-case both sides |

Issues 1 and 4 are the highest-priority fixes before merge: issue 1 because the detection block can silently overwrite a user-supplied answer in interactive mode, and issue 4 because the validator's `normalized` output is declared the source of truth and will pre-empt the detection heuristic in a headless invocation.

Log `\"headless: scope_type auto-selected as {value} from {heuristic-name}\"` so the choice is debuggable.\n\nFor the **component-registry** heuristic, the entry-count threshold (`10+ entries`) and `Component[]` type-annotation check require file *contents* — those are only available when step-02 §4.1 included `registry.ts` / `components.ts` as an entry point in the script payload (rare for the public-API mode), or when the target is a local path. This commit makes them actually fire.\n\n**Critical: validator was pre-empting the source_authority detection.**\n\n`skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` had `normalized.setdefault(\"source_authority\", \"community\")` which stamped a concrete value onto the normalized payload before the step ever saw it. Step-01 §8 GATE treats `normalized` as the source of truth — so the detection branch from commit 530162e9 never fired (the LLM saw `normalized.source_authority = \"community\"` and skipped detection). An LLM processing §3.3 sequentially could fire the probe on interactive runs after the user had just answered the question, overwriting their answer. Move the block to §8 GATE (the proper headless-only context) where it fires after the validator returns and only when source_authority is genuinely absent.\n\n**Documentation drift across the contract surface.**\n\nAfter commits 530162e9 and 9d54230e, the runtime defaults for `source_authority` and `scope_type` are no longer \"community\" and \"full-library\" — they're heuristic-driven. The case-insensitive comparison for source_authority detection now explicitly tells the LLM to lower-case both sides (GitHub logins are case-insensitive in routing but case-preserved in API responses; naive equality check would fail for any mixed-case login).\n\n2. The §7b description-from-GitHub fallback now explicitly directs the LLM to write the synthesized description in `{document_output_language}` regardless of the seed's language. A non-English GitHub description (which is common) was previously seeding the synthesis without any language-conformance instruction.\n\n3. The §2c headless heuristic for component-library detection now states what it can verify in headless mode: presence of `registry.ts` / `components.ts` in the file tree is sufficient, since the entry-count threshold and `Component[]` type-annotation check require file content that may not be available at §2c (those are only fetched if the file happened to be passed as an entry point in step-02 §4.1).\n\nFound by feature-dev:code-reviewer review of PR 5. 713 Python tests pass.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",

Promotes three signal-driven heuristics — currently interactive-only — into the headless code path. The headless contract was already best-in-class; the gap was one layer up where the headless path stamped coarse defaults (`scope_type: full-library`, `source_authority: community`, generic stub description) regardless of available signals. For an automator running 30 briefs overnight, this silently produced uniformly under-specified briefs.\n\n- **530162e9** — `feat(skf-brief-skill): detect headless source_authority from gh api user vs repo owner`. When `source_authority` is absent and `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, probe `gh api user` and compare to the URL owner.\n- **0b5397f3** — `feat(skf-brief-skill): seed headless §7b description from GitHub repo description when intent is missing`. Three-tier fallback in priority order: supplied intent → GitHub repo description → generic stub.\n- **9d54230e** — `feat(skf-brief-skill): run scope-type recommendation in headless instead of stamping full-library`. Promotes the five §2c heuristics into the headless auto-select; falls back to `full-library` only when no signal matches.\n- **e931d054** — `fix(skf-brief-skill): unblock headless heuristics — validator default, GATE placement, contract docs`. In-PR review caught two critical bugs that left the new heuristics non-functional and several documentation drifts:\n  - `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` stamped `setdefault(\"source_authority\", \"community\")` which pre-empted detection. Remove the setdefault; absence is now the \"run detection\" signal.\n  - The detection block was physically placed inside interactive §3.3 with only a buried `AND headless_mode is true` condition. An LLM could fire the probe on interactive runs after the user had answered. Moved to §8 GATE.\n  - `references/headless-args.md` and `SKILL.md` Invocation Contract said the defaults were `community` and `full-library` — both now document the heuristic-driven defaults.\n  - Three smaller correctness fixes: explicit lower-case instruction for case-insensitive owner comparison; explicit `{document_output_language}` directive on the GitHub-description-seeded synthesis (descriptions may be in any language); component-registry headless heuristic now states it uses file-tree-name presence (since content inspection is interactive-only at §2c).\n\n713 Python tests pass (added 1, modified 1 in the validator test suite).\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [ ] `npm run quality` passes locally (full suite ran via pre-commit hook on every commit — green)\n- [ ] CI green on PR\n- [ ] Sanity-run a headless invocation against your own GitHub repo (no `source_authority` arg) and confirm the brief lands `source_authority: official` instead of `community`\n- [ ] Sanity-run a headless invocation against another repo with no `intent` arg and confirm the brief's `description` field is seeded from the GitHub repo description, not the generic stub\n- [ ] Sanity-run a headless invocation against a repo that should be `public-api` scope (e.g. SaaS API SDK) without `scope_type` and confirm it lands `public-api` rather than `full-library`\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Push PR 5 and open"
}

> AGENT

**PR 5 opened: [#281](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/281)** — `feat(skf-brief-skill): promote interactive heuristics into headless defaults`

4 commits (3 findings + 1 substantial reviewer fix). 713 tests pass.

Reviewer caught two **critical bugs** that would have shipped the heuristics in a non-functional state:

1. The validator script unconditionally stamped `source_authority: "community"` via `setdefault`, which pre-empted the new detection branch — `normalized` is treated as the source of truth at the GATE, so the LLM never had a reason to run detection.
2. The detection block was placed under interactive §3.3 with only a buried "AND headless_mode is true" condition — an LLM processing §3.3 sequentially could have fired the probe after the user answered the prompt, overwriting their answer.

Both fixed in `e931d054`, plus 5 documentation/correctness drifts (validator test updates, headless-args.md table updates for both `source_authority` and `scope_type`, SKILL.md contract line, explicit lower-case instruction, `{document_output_language}` directive, headless-only caveat for the component-registry heuristic).

Waiting for your merge before I rebase PR 6 (cancel + pre-flight write probe) onto fresh `main`.

238	Wait for user confirmation or alternative. Store the accepted text as the brief's `description` field. The same field is re-presented in step-04 §3 for a final review pass — refinements there flow back to this value.
239	
240	**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and run the same synthesis against it (in `{document_output_language}`), then store the result. If `intent` was not supplied, fall back in priority order:
241	
242	1. **GitHub repo description** — when `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, fetch `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} --jq .description` (5-second timeout). If a non-empty description comes back, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and synthesize using the GitHub description as the seed in place of `intent`. Write the synthesized description in `{document_output_language}` regardless of the seed's language (the seed may be in any language; the output's language is dictated by the workflow's document-output configuration). Log `"info: description seeded from GitHub repo description"`. (The full `gh api repos` response is fetched again in step-02 §1; this lightweight `--jq .description` call only retrieves the one field.)
243	2. **Generic stub** — when no GitHub description is available (local-path target, GitHub repo with empty description, or `gh api` fails): derive from `target_repo` + `skill_name` (`"Use the {skill_name} skill to work with code or content from {target_repo}."`) — the generic fallback does not need the asset — and log `"warn: description synthesized without intent or repo description — narrow registry text."`
244	
245	### 8. Present MENU OPTIONS
246	
247	Display: "**Select:** [C] Continue to Target Analysis"
248	
249	#### Menu Handling Logic:
250	
251	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
252	- IF Any other: Help user, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#8-present-menu-options)
253	
254	#### EXECUTION RULES:
255	
256	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
257	- **GATE [default: use args]** — If `{headless_mode}`, consume pre-supplied arguments and auto-proceed. The full argument set (required/optional, defaults, halt codes, enum values) is documented in `{headlessArgsFile}` — load it now if you need to look up a specific argument. Validation is delegated to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`; the table is the canonical operator-facing documentation, the script enforces it.

259	  **Delegate validation to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`** instead of reasoning through the table rules in prose:

# /// script
# requires-python = ">=3.10"
# dependencies = ["pyyaml"]
# ///
"""SKF Preflight — Config and sidecar loader for all SKF skills.

Loads config.yaml, validates sidecar_path, loads preferences.yaml and
forge-tier.yaml, and outputs a unified JSON blob for step consumption.

CLI — invoke via `uv run` so the PEP 723 PyYAML dependency declared
above is auto-resolved on first call and cached. `docs/getting-started.md`
documents uv as the runtime prerequisite for exactly this. Bare
`python3` falls back to a defensive ImportError handler that emits
`{"error": "PyYAML not installed...", "code": "MISSING_DEPENDENCY"}`
on stdout — that fallback is the safety net, not the canonical path:

  uv run skf-preflight.py <project-root>
  uv run skf-preflight.py <project-root> --config-path <alt-config>

Output: JSON to stdout with all resolved config variables and sidecar state.
Exit 0 on success, exit 1 on hard-halt conditions (missing config, bad sidecar).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

try:
    import yaml
except ImportError:
    print(
        json.dumps({
            "error": "PyYAML not installed. Invoke via `uv run` (auto-resolves PEP 723 deps — see docs/getting-started.md) or install manually with `pip install pyyaml`.",
            "code": "MISSING_DEPENDENCY",
        }),
    )
    sys.exit(1)


def load_yaml_file(path):
    """Load a YAML file, returning (data, None) or (None, error_string)."""
    try:
        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            data = yaml.safe_load(f)
        return data or {}, None
    except FileNotFoundError:
        return None, f"File not found: {path}"
    except yaml.YAMLError as e:
        return None, f"YAML parse error in {path}: {e}"


def run_preflight(project_root, config_path=None):
    """Run preflight checks and return a result dict."""
    project_root = Path(project_root).resolve()

    # 1. Load config.yaml
    if config_path:
        cfg_path = Path(config_path)
```

  "new_string": "### 1. Pre-flight Write Probe\n\nBefore any conversational state accumulates, verify `{forge_data_folder}` is writable. A read-only mount, full disk, or permissions-denied path only surfaces at step-05's atomic write — by then the user has invested 5–15 minutes of intent gathering, scope decisions, and brief confirmation. Fail fast here instead.\n\nRun a single-byte write-and-remove probe:\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p \"{forge_data_folder}\" && \\\n  printf 'probe' > \"{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe\" && \\\n  rm \"{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe\"\n```\n\n`mkdir -p` succeeds on a pre-existing read-only mount (the directory exists), but the `printf > file` redirect actually attempts a write — that catches read-only mounts, disk-full, and permissions-denied uniformly.\n\n**On any failure** (non-zero exit at any step): HALT (exit code 4, `halt_reason: \"write-failed\"`) — `\"**Error:** {forge_data_folder} is not writable: {captured stderr}. Verify the path exists, the mount is writable, and there is free disk space, then re-run.\"` In headless mode, emit the standard error envelope via `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}` with `halt_reason: \"write-failed\"` before exiting.\n\n**On success:** Continue silently to §2.\n\n### 2. Discover Forge Tier"
}

```python
if envelope.get("exit_code") not in {0, 2, 3, 4, 5}:
    _die(f"exit_code invalid: {envelope.get('exit_code')!r}")
```

The allowlist was `{0, 2, 3, 4, 5}` before this PR. Commit `c44cc55d` added `user-cancelled` with exit code 6 to `HALT_TO_EXIT`, to the schema enum, and to the test for `assemble()` — but did **not** update this guard in `validate()`. The result is a split contract: `assemble()` correctly produces `exit_code: 6` for `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"`, but `validate()` immediately rejects that same envelope as invalid.

The failure is deterministic and will hit any caller of the `validate` subcommand that feeds back a `user-cancelled` envelope — including any paranoid pipeline that round-trips `emit → validate` as described in the script docstring. The line immediately below the guard (`expected_exit = HALT_TO_EXIT[...]`) would compute the correct expected value and pass the cross-check, so the cross-check is not what fails — it's the early-exit allowlist that blocks it.

Fix:

```python
if envelope.get("exit_code") not in {0, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}:
```

Additionally, the test suite has no test that calls `validate()` with a `user-cancelled` envelope at exit code 6. The parametrized `test_halt_reason_to_exit_code_mapping` only covers `assemble()`. A parallel row for `validate()` would have caught this divergence. Consider adding, in `TestValidate`:

```python
def test_validate_passes_user_cancelled_envelope(self):
    env = {
        "status": "error",
        "brief_path": None,
        "skill_name": "foo",
        "version": None,
        "language": None,
        "scope_type": None,
        "exit_code": 6,
        "halt_reason": "user-cancelled",
    }
    mod.validate(env)  # must not raise
```

---

## Important

### step-05 §4b's inline mapping restatement silently omits `user-cancelled→6`

**Confidence: 82**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`, line 128

The inline prose restatement of the exit-code mapping in §4b reads:

> "the canonical mapping is null→0, input-missing/input-invalid→2, forge-tier-missing/target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed→3, write-failed→4, overwrite-cancelled→5"

It stops at 5. `user-cancelled→6` is not listed. The argument for omitting it is that `user-cancelled` is interactive-only and headless code never calls the emit script with that halt_reason, so the omission is "correct by context." The argument against: this same inline mapping is the operator's mental model of the contract, and an operator auditing §4b against SKILL.md's Exit Codes table will see an unexplained gap. The mapping enumeration appears three times in the codebase — SKILL.md, the script docstring, and §4b. Two of the three are complete; §4b is the odd one out.

Whether this constitutes a doc drift issue or an intentional scope fence depends on whether you treat §4b as "headless envelope documentation" (scope fence is correct) or "full contract reference" (gap is drift). Either way the intent should be explicit. The least-risk fix is to append `user-cancelled→6` and annotate it:

> "…overwrite-cancelled→5, user-cancelled→6 [interactive-only — headless never raises this]"

This makes the omission intentional and documented rather than accidental.

---

### Write probe HALT in step-01 §1 says "emit the SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON envelope" but does not reference the emit script — unlike every other headless HALT site

**Confidence: 80**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 36

The probe HALT instruction reads: "In headless mode, emit the error-variant `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope on stderr with `halt_reason: "write-failed"` (per the SKILL.md Result Contract) before exiting."

Every other headless HALT site delegates explicitly to `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}`:
- step-01 §8 GATE: `emit the error-variant SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON envelope on stderr` — same prose, no script ref
- step-05 §2b: "invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}` to emit the error-variant envelope on stderr"
- step-05 §3 (write failure): "invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr` with envelope-context…"

The step-01 probe and the §8 GATE both say "emit the… envelope" without naming the script. A model executing these instructions has two conformant interpretations: (a) call the script, (b) hand-assemble the JSON. Option (b) is exactly the schema-drift footgun the emit script was built to eliminate.

The fix is to use the same delegation pattern as step-05. For the probe HALT:

```
In headless mode, invoke {emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr with
`{"status":"error","skill_name":"<name>","halt_reason":"write-failed"}` before exiting.
```

The same change applies to the §8 GATE HARD HALT text for `user-cancelled`, though that path is interactive-only; it still sets the precedent cleanly.

---

## Lower-confidence observations (not flagged — below threshold, included for completeness)

**`[X]` at step-04 after brief approval but before write — is this truly non-destructive?** The step-04 §5 description says "step-05 has not run, no skill-brief.yaml file exists yet." That is correct. The only state accumulated by step-04's `[X]` is in-memory brief data. No files exist. The claim is accurate and the description matches reality.

**Could a headless invocation reach the `[X]` menus?** All three menus (step-01 §8, step-03 §6, step-04 §5) state their GATE auto-proceeds in headless mode and never reaches `[X]`. The GATE logic in all three steps auto-proceeds unconditionally when `{headless_mode}` is true — there is no code path in any GATE that presents the menu options. The "interactive-only" claim is correct.

**Does `user-cancelled` overlap or conflict with `overwrite-cancelled` (exit 5)?** No. `overwrite-cancelled` is raised only in step-05 §2b when the existing-brief check fires and `force` is absent. `user-cancelled` is raised only when the user types `[X]` at a menu. Step-01's `[X]` fires before any file check; step-03's `[X]` fires after scope definition; step-04's `[X]` fires after brief approval but before the write. None of these interact with the overwrite gate. The two codes are disjoint by construction.

**Write probe and shell compatibility on Windows/Git Bash.** `printf` is a Bash builtin available in Git Bash on Windows. The redirect `> file` is standard POSIX shell syntax. `mkdir -p` and `rm` are both available in Git Bash. This probe will work on Git Bash. WSL2 is a full Linux environment — no concern. The one edge case not covered: if `{forge_data_folder}` contains Windows-style backslash paths passed through Git Bash, the double-quoted `"{forge_data_folder}"` will pass the backslash through to the shell correctly. No issue found.

**`mkdir -p` on a pre-existing read-only mount.** The prose in step-01 §1 explicitly documents this: "`mkdir -p` succeeds on a pre-existing read-only mount, but the `printf > file` redirect actually attempts a write — that catches read-only, disk-full, and permissions-denied uniformly." This is accurate: on Linux, `mkdir -p` on an existing directory that is read-only returns exit 0 (the directory already exists, no write needed). The redirect is what exercises the write permission. The three failure classes are genuinely caught.

**Does the probe HALTing cleanly compose with the §8 `[X]`?** Yes. If the §1 probe HALTs, the workflow terminates before §8 is ever reached. If the probe passes and the user later selects `[X]` at §8, no probe-related state exists (the probe file was removed). No interaction issue.

**Does `headless-args.md` need updating for `[X]` cancel?** No. `[X]` is interactive-only and has no headless argument form. The table correctly has no row for it. No update needed.

**`write-failed` sharing between step-01 §1 and step-05 §4.** The SKILL.md Exit Codes table currently reads `step-05 §4 (write to {forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml failed)` as the sole raiser of exit code 4. With the write probe added in commit `caaab468`, step-01 §1 now also raises `write-failed` / exit 4. This is a documentation gap in the Exit Codes table. However, because the PR author specifically asked about whether it "muddies the operator's failure-class diagnosis": the operator experience is that exit 4 = "something is wrong with the write path" in both cases — the distinction is step-01 (directory unwritable) vs. step-05 (brief file unwritable). Both cases have the same recovery action (fix the path / mount). There is a real argument that a distinct `data-folder-unwritable` reason would be more precise, but it is also a new contract surface that needs to be propagated everywhere. The current approach is defensible; the Exit Codes table should at minimum be updated to acknowledge step-01 §1 as a second raiser of exit 4 so operators are not confused by an exit-4 that occurs before any brief work. Confidence is below the 80 threshold to flag this as a required fix, but the table text "Raised by: step-05 §4" is now factually incomplete.

Sources:
- No external sources were used — all findings are from direct inspection of the repository files.

270	
271	  **Delegate validation to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`** instead of reasoning through the table rules in prose:
272	
273	  ```bash
274	  echo '<headless-args-as-json>' | uv run {validateBriefInputsScript}
275	  ```
276	
277	  The script returns a JSON envelope: `{valid, errors[], warnings[], normalized, halt_reason}`. Apply the result deterministically:
278	
279	  - **`valid: false`** — emit the error-variant `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope on stderr with `exit_code: 2` and the script's `halt_reason` (`"input-missing"` for absent required args / docs-only without doc_urls; `"input-invalid"` for enum violations, malformed semver, malformed kebab-case skill_name). Surface `errors[]` to the operator log so the failure is debuggable. HALT.
280	  - **`valid: true`** — consume the `normalized` object as the source of truth (it has defaults applied per the table). Surface `warnings[]` to the operator log but do not HALT. Auto-proceed.
281	
282	  The script's `KNOWN_FIELDS` set must stay in sync with the table in `{headlessArgsFile}`.
283	
284	  **Headless source-authority detection** — the validator intentionally leaves `source_authority` ABSENT from `normalized` when not supplied (so detection can run here). After consuming `normalized`, if `source_authority` is absent AND `source_type=source` AND `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, run signal-driven detection:
285	
286	  ```bash
287	  gh api user --jq .login
288	  ```
289	
290	  Compare the result to the `owner` segment of `target_repo` (URL pattern `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`) — **lower-case both values before comparing** (GitHub owner matching is case-insensitive but the API preserves case in responses). If they match, set `source_authority: "official"` — the operator is the repo's GitHub owner. Otherwise set `source_authority: "community"`. On any error from `gh api user` (unauthenticated, network failure, missing binary), log `"warn: source-authority detection skipped — gh api user failed"` and fall back to `"community"`. For local-path targets the comparison cannot apply; set `"community"` directly. (When `source_authority` was already supplied in `normalized`, the supplied value wins — no detection runs.)
291	
292	
293	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
294

110	- Exit 1 (validation/invariant): The error JSON on stderr names the offending field. This indicates a context-assembly bug, not a user error — surface the message to the user, log it, then HALT.
111	  - Interactive: **HALT** — display the error JSON's `message` field.
112	  - Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr` with envelope-context `halt_reason: "input-invalid"` (the script derives `exit_code: 2` automatically — do NOT pass `exit_code` in the JSON payload), then `exit 2`.
113	- Exit 2 (I/O failure): The atomic write failed (target unwritable, disk full, etc.).
114	  - Interactive: **HALT** — "**Error:** Failed to write skill-brief.yaml. Check that the directory is writable and try again."
115	  - Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr` with envelope-context `halt_reason: "write-failed"` (script derives `exit_code: 4` automatically), then `exit 4`.
116	
117	**On success:** capture `brief_path` and `version` from the response envelope — both are needed for §4b and §6.
118	
119	### 4b. Headless Result Envelope
120	
121	If `{headless_mode}` is true, emit the success envelope on **stdout** immediately after the write (before §5 / §6 / §7):
122	
123	```bash
124	echo '{"status":"success","brief_path":"<from §3 response>","skill_name":"<name>","version":"<from §3 response>","language":"<language>","scope_type":"<scope.type>","halt_reason":null}' | \
125	  uv run {emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit
126	```
127	
128	The script derives `exit_code` deterministically from `halt_reason` (the canonical mapping is null→0, input-missing/input-invalid→2, forge-tier-missing/target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed→3, write-failed→4, overwrite-cancelled→5, user-cancelled→6 [interactive-only — headless never raises this]), validates against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-brief-result-envelope.v1.json`, and prints the prefixed `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {…}` line.
129	
130	The envelope shape on HARD HALT (any phase) is the same call with `--target stderr`, `status: "error"`, and the matching `halt_reason` (one of `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`) — see the §3, §2b, §5 (no-halt) branches above and the §1/§2 HALTs in step-01/step-02 for invocation sites. The script enforces the success/error halt_reason invariant (success requires null halt_reason; error requires non-null). The `user-cancelled` halt_reason is also accepted by the script for completeness (interactive `[X]` Cancel sites in step-01/03/04 use it) but never appears on the headless code path.
131	
132	When `{headless_mode}` is false, skip this section silently — no envelope is emitted.
133	
134	### 5. QMD Collection Registration (Deep Tier Only)

Headless does not run this check either — it would either need to HALT on duplicates (over-aggressive for an automator) or silently log (no operator to act on it); leaving it interactive-only keeps the headless path side-effect-free against the QMD index.\n\n**Draft-resume check (interactive only).**"

This confirms the `--query` flag and `--top` flag both do not exist in the real qmd CLI. The correct syntax would be `qmd query "{name} {intent}" -c {collection-name} -n 3 --min-score 0.6`. The `--collections` glob and both `--query`/`--top` are wrong.
