## Critical Issues

### Issue 1: The `qmd query` invocation uses three flags that do not exist in the real qmd CLI

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 218

**Confidence: 100**

The bash snippet in step-01 §6 is:
```bash
qmd query --query "{name} {synthesized-or-intent-text}" --collections "*-brief" --top 3 --min-score 0.6
```

Three of these four flags are fabricated:

1. `--query` — qmd does not have a `--query` named flag. The query is a positional argument: `qmd query "terms"`.
2. `--collections` (plural, with glob `"*-brief"`) — qmd has no `--collections` flag and no glob-style collection selection. The only collection filter is `-c <exact-name>` (singular, exact match, one collection per invocation). Querying all `*-brief` collections in one call is not supported.
3. `--top 3` — qmd uses `-n <num>` for result count, not `--top`.

The only flag that is real is `--min-score`.

The practical impact is lower than it sounds because step-01 §6 has an explicit fallback: "On any QMD failure (binary missing, collection list empty, query times out): log warn and continue silently." So the LLM will log a warning and skip the check — it will not HALT. But the check will never actually run on a correctly-installed Deep-tier environment, defeating the purpose of the commit, and the prose teaches the LLM a non-existent CLI surface.

**The correct approach** requires a two-step operation: first enumerate collections with `qmd collection list` (output includes `name` field per collection), filter for names ending in `-brief`, then run a separate `qmd query "{terms}" -c {collection-name} -n 3 --min-score 0.6` per collection, or accept the first positive match. This is substantially more prose than a single command, and whether it's worth the complexity is a design call — but the current single-command shortcut is inoperative.

**Fix:** Either rewrite the command to the two-step pattern (enumerate + query per collection), or mark it explicitly as `# NOTE: qmd CLI does not support glob collection selection — enumerate with "qmd collection list" first, then query each -brief collection separately` and supply the real loop. Alternatively, if the LLM is expected to enumerate collections itself, drop the bash snippet and write the instruction in prose.

---

### Issue 2: "Forge+/Deep with QMD" is wrong — QMD is Deep-only per the project's own canonical tier definition

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 215

**Confidence: 100**

The section header reads:

> **Portfolio-similarity check (Forge+/Deep with QMD only, interactive only).** When the forge tier is `Forge+` or `Deep` AND `tools.qmd` is true in `forge-tier.yaml`…

The canonical tier definition is the comment in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-forge-tier-rw.py`, line 218:

```
# Quick = no tools | Forge = + ast-grep | Forge+ = + ast-grep + ccc | Deep = + ast-grep + gh + QMD
```

QMD is a Deep-only tool. A Forge+ environment will always have `tools.qmd: false`. The `AND tools.qmd is true` guard means no Forge+ user will ever trigger this branch at runtime, so the behaviour is correct. But the prose says "Forge+/Deep", the tier_gloss in §2 says `Forge+` is "AST-grep + ccc semantic discovery", and step-05 §5 says `# Deep Tier Only` for QMD registration. The step-01 §6 header is the only place in the codebase that claims Forge+ supports QMD.

This is internally inconsistent within the same workflow. An operator reading step-01 would conclude their Forge+ environment would get the similarity check; the setup workflow's tier definitions and the tier_gloss in §2 of the same step contradict that.

**Fix:** Change line 215 to "Deep tier with QMD only" (dropping "Forge+") to match the canonical tier definition and step-05 §5. The runtime guard on `tools.qmd` remains unchanged.

---

## Important Issues

### Issue 3: "Jump directly to §8" on Resume leaves §7 and §7b execution ambiguous

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 241

**Confidence: 85**

The draft-resume instruction reads:

> On `[Y]`: load the JSON, restore the captured fields (target_repo, source_type, source_authority, target_version, doc_urls, intent, scope_hint, description), and jump directly to §8 — the user can still revise at step-04.

The problem: the LLM is executing `§6` (Derive Skill Name) when it discovers the draft. `§6` is a subsection inside the MANDATORY SEQUENCE. The prose says "jump directly to §8" but does not say "skip §7 and §7b". An LLM reading sequentially may interpret this as: restore fields, then continue the MANDATORY SEQUENCE from the next section after §6 (which is §7), and only then arrive at §8. If it executes §7, it will display the summary again (harmless, idempotent) and write a new `.brief-draft.json` checkpoint (also idempotent but redundant). If it executes §7b, it will re-synthesize the description (wasteful but idempotent since the restored `description` field is already present — it might overwrite the user's previously accepted description with a fresh synthesis).

The `description` field is in the restored field list, which is good. But the instruction does not tell the LLM "do not run §7b's description synthesis; the restored `description` is authoritative." On a resumed run the LLM will load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and present a new candidate description for acceptance, potentially replacing the description the user previously accepted.

**Fix:** Add an explicit skip clause: `"…and jump directly to §8 — skip §7, §7b, and the draft checkpoint write; the restored description is authoritative and does not need re-synthesis."` The current phrasing trusts the LLM to interpret "jump directly" correctly in the context of a numbered MANDATORY SEQUENCE, which is a weak instruction when intermediate sections exist.

---

### Issue 4: `preset` in `KNOWN_FIELDS` creates a silent passthrough into `normalized` that contradicts the stated drop contract

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`, lines 63-80 and 209-211, and step-01 §8 prose

**Confidence: 80**

The validator includes `preset` in `KNOWN_FIELDS` (line 79), which means it does not emit a warning when `preset` is present in the input. The `validate()` function does not strip it from `normalized` — line 214 does `normalized: dict[str, Any] = dict(inp)`, which carries `preset` through if the LLM did not drop it.

The step-01 §8 prose says: "Drop the `preset` key itself from the merged dict before passing to the validator (it is consumed at this level and is not a brief field)." This makes the drop the LLM's responsibility. Including `preset` in `KNOWN_FIELDS` was described as "defensive belt-and-braces" — but the actual effect is that if the LLM forgets the drop, `preset` silently flows into `normalized` with no warning, and the validator's response signals `valid: true`. The LLM then treats `normalized` as the source of truth for step-05's context assembly. The step-05 writer script ignores unknown fields, so nothing breaks at write time — but `preset` will be passed as part of the JSON pipe to `skf-write-skill-brief.py`, which is a contaminated context assembly.

The gap is: the prose says "drop it, then validate" (LLM owns the drop), but `KNOWN_FIELDS` turns a missed drop into a silent success instead of a warning. The "defensive" intent would actually be better served by removing `preset` from `KNOWN_FIELDS`, so that if the LLM forgets the drop, the validator emits `"unrecognized field 'preset' — passed through unchanged"` as a warning. That warning surfaces in `warnings[]`, which the §8 prose says is logged but non-halting. The LLM or a supervising operator would see the signal. Right now the signal is invisible.

**Fix:** Remove `preset` from `KNOWN_FIELDS`. The validator will then issue a `warnings[]` entry if `preset` leaks through, which is the correct observable. The prose comment in `headless-args.md` already explains `preset` is consumed at the GATE level, not by the validator. No behavior changes for the correct path; the incorrect path becomes diagnosable.

---

## Items Reviewed and Found Acceptable

**Commit 30ed4997 — Preset merge order and recursion.** The merge prose is unambiguous: "explicit args override preset values, key by key." An LLM reading that cannot confuse the precedence direction. Recursive presets (a preset YAML containing a `preset` field itself) are not guarded against in prose; in practice the second `preset` key would appear in the already-merged dict before it reaches the validator GATE. At that point the prose says "load `{sidecar_path}/brief-presets/{preset}.yaml`" — if that file doesn't exist the "proceed without preset" warning fires; if it does there could be infinite recursion. This is worth noting but is correctly classified as a non-issue for current operator usage patterns (operators write simple preset files; this is not a public API surface).

**Commit 942eba51 — Atomic-write adequacy.** The "write to `.brief-draft.json.tmp` then rename" instruction is adequate for a prose-driven LLM context. The draft is advisory — a half-written draft discovered on Resume would simply fail to parse and the LLM would continue as if no draft existed. Adding `mktemp` boilerplate would be over-engineering for a checkpoint file.

**Commit 942eba51 — Race condition on concurrent step-01 invocations.** "Last writer wins" is the correct outcome for an advisory draft. No lockfile is warranted.

**Commit 942eba51 — `rm -f` failure handling.** The brief was already written at that point. Silent failure on a cleanup `rm -f` is the right call — no HALT warranted.

**Commit 942eba51 — Draft file and sensitive data.** `intent` is freeform text. The "could include API keys" scenario is real but is in the same threat class as the brief's own `doc_urls` field. The `.brief-draft.json` is written under `{forge_data_folder}` which is presumably the operator's own data directory. Not worth a blocker note.

**Commit a0810378 — `--min-score 0.6` threshold.** The QMD MCP plugin documentation in the system prompt confirms `minScore: 0.5` as a base threshold. 0.6 is conservative (higher precision). Calibration-wise this is fine; it errs toward fewer false positives which is the right tradeoff for a non-halting check.

**Commit a0810378 — Headless skipping the similarity check.** The "interactive-only" rationale is sound. The envelope schema has no slot for similarity hits, adding one would be scope creep, and headless already has exact-name collision logged. Correct to leave it interactive-only.

**Cross-commit ordering (§6 sequence: collision → similarity → draft-resume).** The ordering is correct. Portfolio similarity runs after the name is locked because the search uses the proposed name as part of the query. Running it before name confirmation would require a name to search with anyway. The draft-resume check runs last because a prior draft's existence is the most actionable signal — if a draft exists and the user resumes, the similarity results are irrelevant (those were seen in the prior session). This composition is clean.

**SKILL.md Invocation Contract — `preset` not listed in Inputs row.** The `preset` arg is headless-only, consumed at the GATE and documented in `references/headless-args.md`. The Inputs row documents all user-facing args. The existing pattern is that headless-only arguments are documented in `headless-args.md` rather than the Inputs table (see `force`, which is also not in the Inputs row of SKILL.md). Consistent omission — not a gap.

---

## Summary

Two issues need fixing before merge. Issue 1 (wrong qmd CLI flags) means the QMD similarity check will always fail silently at runtime, making commit a0810378 a no-op on any real Deep-tier installation. Issue 2 (wrong tier label) is a documentation inconsistency that will mislead Forge+ operators. Issue 3 (ambiguous jump in resume flow) is a moderate execution-clarity risk that could cause §7b to re-synthesize and overwrite the user's accepted description on resume. Issue 4 (preset in KNOWN_FIELDS) is a minor contract cleanliness concern with no runtime impact.

Sources:
- [GitHub - tobi/qmd](https://github.com/tobi/qmd)
- [qmd README.md](https://github.com/tobi/qmd/blob/main/README.md)
- [Document Retrieval Commands - tobi/qmd - DeepWiki](https://deepwiki.com/tobi/qmd/4.3-document-retrieval-commands)
- [Collections and Indexing - tobi/qmd - DeepWiki](https://deepwiki.com/tobi/qmd/3.1-collections-and-indexing)

```json
{
  "command": "git add -A src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(skf-brief-skill): correct QMD CLI surface, tier guard, resume-jump skip, preset KNOWN_FIELDS\n\nIn-PR review of PR 7 caught two critical bugs and two important issues across the three new affordances:\n\n**Critical: QMD CLI flags were fabricated.** The portfolio-similarity snippet from a0810378 used `qmd query --query \"…\" --collections \"*-brief\" --top 3 --min-score 0.6`. Three of those flags do not exist in the real qmd CLI — query is positional, `-c` selects a single exact-named collection (no glob), and `-n` (not `--top`) limits result count. The check would have failed silently on every Deep-tier run because of the warn-and-continue fallback, defeating the entire commit. Replace with a two-step pattern: `qmd collection list | awk '/-brief$/{print $1}'` to enumerate brief collections, then per-collection `qmd query \"{name} {intent}\" -c {collection-name} -n 1 --min-score 0.6` calls in a single message with N parallel Bash calls. Aggregate the top 3 scoring hits across the union.\n\n**Critical: Forge+ does not have QMD.** The header said \"Forge+/Deep with QMD only\" but the canonical tier definition (`# Quick = no tools | Forge = + ast-grep | Forge+ = + ast-grep + ccc | Deep = + ast-grep + gh + QMD` in skf-forge-tier-rw.py) makes QMD a Deep-only tool. The runtime guard on `tools.qmd` happens to be correct, but the prose contradicted both the tier definition and step-05 §5 (\"# Deep Tier Only\" for QMD registration). Drop \"Forge+/\" from the header; update the trailing sentence to list \"Quick / Forge / Forge+\" as tiers that skip the check.\n\n**Important: resume-jump leaves §7/§7b execution ambiguous.** Telling the LLM to \"jump directly to §8\" while it is mid-execution of §6 inside a numbered MANDATORY SEQUENCE does not unambiguously prevent §7's summary-display + checkpoint-write or §7b's description re-synthesis from running. §7b in particular would overwrite the user's previously-accepted description with a fresh candidate. Add an explicit skip clause: \"skip the rest of §6, §7, and §7b\" plus \"the restored description is authoritative and does not need re-synthesis.\"\n\n**Important: `preset` in KNOWN_FIELDS suppressed the leakage signal.** The §8 GATE prose says \"drop the `preset` key before passing to the validator.\" Including `preset` in `KNOWN_FIELDS` was meant as belt-and-braces but actually suppressed the unknown-field warning if the LLM forgot to drop it — `preset` would silently flow into `normalized` and then into the step-05 brief context. Remove from `KNOWN_FIELDS`; if the drop is missed, the existing unknown-field path emits a warning that surfaces in `warnings[]` for debuggability.\n\n715 Python tests still pass — no test changes needed (the validator's unknown-field warning path is already covered by TestUnknownFields.test_unknown_field_warns_but_passes).\n\nFound by feature-dev:code-reviewer review of PR 7.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Commit reviewer fixes",
  "timeout": 300000
}
```

> AGENT

**PR 7 opened: [#283](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/283)** — `feat(skf-brief-skill): add --preset, draft checkpoint, and QMD portfolio similarity`

**Final PR in the series.** 4 commits (3 features + 1 reviewer fix). 715 tests pass.

Reviewer caught two **critical bugs** in the QMD commit alone:
1. **Fabricated CLI flags** — `--query`, `--collections "*-brief"`, and `--top 3` don't exist in the real qmd CLI. The check would have failed silently on every Deep-tier run via the warn-and-continue fallback, defeating the entire commit. Replaced with the real two-step pattern (`qmd collection list | awk` to enumerate, then per-collection `qmd query "{q}" -c {name} -n 1 --min-score 0.6` calls in parallel).
2. **Wrong tier label** — "Forge+/Deep with QMD" contradicted the canonical tier definition (`Deep = + ast-grep + gh + QMD` in `skf-forge-tier-rw.py`). QMD is Deep-only.

Plus 2 important issues (ambiguous resume-jump that could re-synthesize and overwrite the user's accepted description; `preset` in `KNOWN_FIELDS` suppressed the leakage signal if the LLM forgot to drop the key). All four fixed in `df788e9c`.

## Series complete

After this PR merges, every finding from the quality analysis is addressed:

| # | Theme | PR | Severity |
|---|-------|----|----| 
| 1 | Lazy-load gaps | [#277](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/277) | medium ✓ |
| 2a | Reference extraction | [#279](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/279) | medium ✓ |
| 2b | Monorepo detection script | [#280](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/280) | medium ✓ |
| 3 | Headless heuristics | [#281](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/281) | medium ✓ |
| 4a | Cancel + write probe | [#282](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/282) | low ✓ |
| 4b | Preset/draft/QMD | [#283](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/283) | low (this PR) |
| 5 | Prompt-craft cleanups | [#278](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/278) | low ✓ |

19 findings across 5 themes, 7 PRs, ~30 commits, code-review on every PR. Step-01 went from 303 → 273 lines despite gaining new affordances. Test count: 638 → 715 (one new shared script + 38 new tests + envelope/validator extensions).

Want me to `/schedule` an agent in 4 weeks to spot-check that the QMD similarity flow actually fires on a real Deep-tier brief once you've accumulated a few in the portfolio? That's the one Theme 4b feature whose end-to-end can only be verified after some real-world usage.

All 7 PRs merged. The full quality-analysis sweep is complete.

**Final tally:**
- 19 findings across 5 themes addressed
- ~30 commits, 7 PRs, code-reviewer pass on every PR before push
- Reviewer caught real bugs across the series — the most consequential were a fabricated qmd CLI surface (would have made the QMD similarity check a silent no-op), a validator `setdefault` that pre-empted source_authority detection, and a `validate()` allowlist missing exit code 6
- 638 → 715 Python tests (+1 new shared script `skf-detect-workspaces.py` with 38 tests, plus envelope/validator extensions)
- Step-01 dropped 303 → 273 lines despite gaining `[X]` cancel, write probe, draft checkpoint, preset arg, QMD similarity, source-authority detection, and description-from-repo seed

Want me to `/schedule` an agent in 4 weeks to spot-check that the new affordances (especially QMD similarity and `.brief-draft.json` resume) actually fire correctly once you've accumulated some real-world usage? Those two are the ones whose end-to-end behaviour can only be verified post-merge.
